VERS. 34.-- The meal sprinkled, etc. (AUG.) 220DMystically. Without leaven the children of Israel set out from Egypt; that we, going out of this world, may cast away the leaven of malice and of wickedness, and feast in the unleavened bread of sincerity and of truth.

VERS. 35, 36.-- And they asked, etc. (AUG., quaest. 45.) This had already been done before the deaths of the firstborn. But now it is repeated by way of recapitulation, for it was narrated when it was done. For how could it now come about that these things should be lent amid so great a mourning: unless perhaps one should say that by this plague also those Egyptians were not struck who dwelt with the Hebrews.

Vessels of gold, etc. These signify the doctrines which are learned out of the very custom of the nations by no unprofitable study; or precious souls out of the nations, as 221Avessels of gold and of silver; with garments, that is, with bodies: which join themselves to the people of God, that once for all they may be freed out of this world as out of Egypt.

VERS. 37-45.-- And they set forward, etc. (ORIG. hom. 5 in Exod.). He who prepares to set out from Egypt, who desires to leave behind the dark deeds of the world and the darkness of errors, must first set out from Ramesse, which is interpreted the commotion of the moth. If thou wilt come to this, that God may be thy leader, and that the rock may follow which bestows spiritual meat and drink, go out from Ramesse, lest thou lay up treasure where the moth destroys and thieves dig through, etc.

If thou wilt be perfect, go and sell all that thou hast, and give to the poor, and thou shalt have treasure in heaven, and come, follow me (Matth. VI): this is to set out from Ramesse, and to follow Christ.

221BFrom Ramesse to Socoth, etc. (ORIG. ibid.) Let us see what is the place of encampment to which they come. From Ramesse they came into Socoth, which is interpreted tabernacles. When therefore, leaving Egypt, thou hast shaken off the moths of every corruption and hast cast away the incitements of the vices, thou shalt dwell in tabernacles, by which, namely, we would not be unclothed but clothed upon. And the dwelling in tabernacles shows that he who hastens to God is unencumbered. But not even in this must one stand still: the camp must be moved from Socoth also, and one must hasten into Othon, which is interpreted signs to them, because here, namely, thou shalt hear that God goes before them by day in a pillar of cloud, and by night in a pillar of fire; not this in Ramesse, nor in Socoth, which are the second camp. The third are those in which the divine signs are wrought: this 221Cwas the three days to which Moses and Aaron were hastening, and Pharao contradicted them, saying: Go no further. He was unwilling that the children of Israel should reach the place of the signs, that they might be able to enjoy the mysteries of the third day. Whence Osee VI: God will raise us up after two days, and on the third day we shall rise again, and we shall live in his sight. [Vulg. Os 6:3 Vivificabit nos post duos dies; in die tertia suscitabit nos, et vivemus in conspectu ejus, "He will quicken us after two days: on the third day he will raise us up"] The first day is for us the passion of Christ; the second, that on which he descended into hell; the third, that on which he rose again: therefore on the third day he went before them by day in a pillar of cloud; but by night, in a pillar of fire. It is necessary therefore that they who are baptized in Christ should be baptized in his death, and be buried together with him, and on the third day rise again, whom, namely, he hath raised up with himself and hath made to sit in the heavenly places (Rom. VI).

About six hundred thousand, etc. (AUG., quaest. 47.) It is asked 221Dwhether the Hebrews came to so great a number through those years in Egypt, etc., as far as that is, after the death of Joseph they were oppressed with hard servitude.

VERS. 46-51.-- Nor of him, etc. The bone of Christ was not broken on the cross, because, seeing him already dead, they did not break his legs: or the strength of the Godhead in the flesh is not corrupted by blasphemy, because he is not mere man, but true God.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 2-8.-- Sanctifica mihi omne primogenitum, etc. Quia occidi omnia primogenita Aegyptiorum in liberatione vestra.

VERS. 9-12.-- Et erit quasi signum, etc. (AUG., quaest. 48 in Exod.). Quid est quod ait cum de pascha 222Apraecipit, etc., usque ad alioquin gratia jam non esset gratia.

VERS. 13-15.-- Omne autem primogenitum hominis, etc. (CYRILL.) Primogenita hominum redimi jubet, ut homicidium vetet: qui omnia ut incorruptibilia essent, creavit.

(GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 1.) Asinus immunditiam, ovis innocentiam significat. Asini ergo primogenitum ove mutare, est immundae vitae primordia innocentiae simplicitate convertere, ut postquam peccator illa egit quae ut immunda Dominus respuit, ea jam agendo proferat quae Dei sacrificio imponat. Omnes enim in peccato nati sumus, et ex carnis delectatione concepti, culpam originalem nobis contraximus. Unde etiam voluntate nostra peccatis 222Bimplicamur. Sed et qui post malitiam carnis ad innocentiam convertitur cordis, primogenitum asini mutat ove, malitiam scilicet prioris vitae. Qui enim qualis prius fuit desinit esse, et apprehensa carnis munditia innocentiam custodit, in oblationem Dei primogenitum asini ove mutavit.

VERS. 16.-- Et quasi appensum. Hoc male intelligentes Judaei, pictatia vel phylacteria capitibus circumligabant, in quibus decem verba scripta ante oculos habebant.

VERS. 17.-- Igitur cum emisisset, etc. (GREG. lib. XXIV Moral. cap. 13). Saepe conversi in ipso aditu suae conversionis tranquillitatem carnis, dona prophetiae, praedicamenta doctrinae, signorum miracula vel gratiam curationum accipiunt. Postea duris tentationum 222Cprobationibus fatigantur, a quibus liberos se esse credebant: quia si in principio tentationum amaritudo pulsaret, facile ad vitia redirent, a quibus non longe recesserunt. De Aegypto ergo exeuntibus e vicino bella subtrahuntur: quia relinquentibus saeculum quaedam prius tranquillitas ostenditur, ne in ipsa teneritudine et inchoatione turbati, ad hoc quod reliquerant, redeant: prius ergo suavitatem securitatis sentiunt, prius pacis quiete nutriuntur; post cognitam vero dulcedinem tanto tolerabilius tentationum certamina sustinent quanto in Deo altius cognoverunt quae ament.

VERS. 18-20.-- Non eos duxit Deus, etc. (AUG., quaest. 49). Hic ostenditur omnia fieri debere quae consilio recte fieri possunt, ad evitanda ea quae 222Dadversa sunt, etiam cum Deus apertissime adjutor est (Epist. ad Dam., tom. I).

Et armati ascenderunt filii, etc. (HIERON.) Aquila, qui non contentiosus, ut putant, sed studiosus verbum interpretatur ad verbum, ubi LXX posuerunt, quinta autem generatione ascenderunt filii Israel de terra Aegypti, transtulit, et armati ascenderunt filii Israel de terra Aegypti, licet pro eo quod nos armati diximus, secundum Graeci sermonis ambiguitatem instructi vel muniti propter supellectilem quo Aegyptios exspoliaverunt, possit intelligi. Sed ubi LXX, quinta generatione posuerunt, qui Hebraeorum libros diligenter inspexerunt, non quinta, sed quinque, scriptum afferunt, ut sit sensus: quinque autem ascenderunt 223Afilii Israel de terra Aegypti; quod quia minus videbatur intelligi, adjuncta generatio est. Aquila vero, et in caeteris, et in hoc praecipue loco, proprie transtulisse dicitur, sicut synagogarum consonant universa subsellia.

(AUG., quaest. 53.) Quinta autem progenie, etc. Utrum progeniem in centum annis vult computare, etc., usque ad Naason genuit Salmon.

VERS. 21-22.-- Dominus autem praecedebat eos, etc. (ORIG.) Cum a te tertiae diei mysterium fuerit receptum, incipiet te deducere Deus et viam salutis ostendere.

(GREG.) In igne terror, in nube visionis lene blandimentum. Dies, vita justi; nox, peccatoris. Unde Ephes. V: Fuistis aliquando tenebrae, nunc autem 223Blux in Domino, etc. In die per nubem columna monstrata est, et in nocte per ignem; quia omnipotens Deus et blandus justis, et terribilis apparebit impiis. Hos ad judicium veniens, per lenitatis mansuetudinem mulcet, illos per justitiae districtionem terret. Aliter columna nubis in eremo populum praeibat, et splendore ignis non in die, sed in nocte radiabat: quia Christus conversationis suae exemplo ducatum sequentibus praestans, de justitia sua confidentibus nulla luce claruit; peccatorum vero tenebras agnoscentibus, igne sui amoris infulsit. Christus nubes et columna, quia rectus, et firmus, et fulciens infirmitatem nostram; quia nocte lucens, per diem non lucens, ut qui non vident, videant; et qui vident, caeci fiant (Joan. IX). Christi quoque sacramentum 223Ctanquam in die manifestum est in carne velut in nube: in judicio vero tanquam in terrore nocturno; quia tunc erit magna tribulatio, tanquam ignis lucebit justis, et ardebit injustis.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 2-8.-- Sanctify unto me every firstborn, etc. Because I slew all the firstborn of the Egyptians in your deliverance.

VERS. 9-12.-- And it shall be as a sign, etc. (AUG., quaest. 48 in Exod.). What is it that he says when he gives commandment concerning the pasch, 222Aetc., as far as otherwise grace would now be no longer grace.

VERS. 13-15.-- But every firstborn of man, etc. (CYRILL.) He commands the firstborn of men to be redeemed, that he may forbid manslaying: he who created all things that they might be incorruptible.

(GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 1.) The ass signifies uncleanness, the sheep innocence. Therefore to exchange the firstling of an ass for a sheep is to turn the beginnings of an unclean life into the simplicity of innocence, so that after the sinner has done those things which the Lord rejected as unclean, he may now by his doing bring forth those things which he may lay upon the sacrifice of God. For we are all born in sin, and conceived of the delight of the flesh, and have contracted original guilt to ourselves. Whence also by our own will we are entangled 222Bin sins. But he too who after the malice of the flesh is turned to the innocence of the heart exchanges the firstling of an ass for a sheep, that is, the malice of his former life. For he who ceases to be such as he was before, and, having laid hold of cleanness of the flesh, keeps innocence, has exchanged the firstling of the ass for a sheep in the offering of God.

VERS. 16.-- And as a thing hung up. The Jews, understanding this ill, used to bind little patches or phylacteries about their heads, in which they had the ten words written before their eyes.

VERS. 17.-- When therefore he had sent them out, etc. (GREG. lib. XXIV Moral. cap. 13). Often the converted, in the very entrance of their conversion, receive tranquillity of the flesh, gifts of prophecy, the teachings of doctrine, miracles of signs or the grace of healings. Afterward they are wearied by the hard 222Cprovings of temptations, from which they believed themselves to be free: because if in the beginning the bitterness of temptations were to beat upon them, they would easily return to the vices from which they have not far withdrawn. From those therefore who go out of Egypt wars near at hand are taken away: because to those who leave the world a certain tranquillity is first shown, lest, troubled in that very tenderness and beginning, they return to what they had left: first therefore they feel the sweetness of security, first they are nourished by the quiet of peace; but after the sweetness has been known they bear the contests of temptations the more tolerably, the more deeply they have known in God the things they love.

VERS. 18-20.-- God led them not, etc. (AUG., quaest. 49). Here it is shown that all things ought to be done which can rightly be done by counsel, for the avoiding of those things which 222Dare adverse, even when God is most openly a helper (Epist. ad Dam., tom. I).

And the children went up armed, etc. (HIERON.) Aquila, who translates word for word, not, as they suppose, from contentiousness, but from diligence, where the Seventy set, but in the fifth generation the children of Israel went up out of the land of Egypt, rendered, and the children of Israel went up armed out of the land of Egypt, although, in place of what we have called armed, it may, according to the ambiguity of the Greek speech, be understood equipped or furnished, on account of the household stuff wherewith they despoiled the Egyptians. But where the Seventy set in the fifth generation, they who have diligently inspected the books of the Hebrews report that not "fifth" but "five" is written, so that the sense may be: but five went up, 223Athe children of Israel, out of the land of Egypt; and because this seemed to be less understood, "generation" was added. But Aquila is said to have translated properly both in the rest and chiefly in this place, as all the benches of the synagogues agree.

(AUG., quaest. 53.) But in the fifth progeny, etc. Whether he means to reckon a progeny within a hundred years, etc., as far as Naason begot Salmon.

VERS. 21-22.-- And the Lord went before them, etc. (ORIG.) When the mystery of the third day has been received by thee, God will begin to lead thee forth and to show thee the way of salvation.

(GREG.) In the fire is terror, in the cloud the gentle soothing of a vision. The day is the life of the just man; the night, of the sinner. Whence Ephes. V: You were once darkness, but now 223Blight in the Lord, etc. [Vulg. Eph 5:8 Eratis enim aliquando tenebrae: nunc autem lux in Domino, "For you were heretofore darkness"] By day the pillar was shown by the cloud, and by night by the fire; because almighty God will appear both gentle to the just and terrible to the wicked. Coming to judgment, he soothes these by the mildness of his lenity, and terrifies those by the strictness of his justice. Otherwise: the pillar of cloud went before the people in the desert, and shone with the splendour of fire not by day, but by night: because Christ, affording leadership to his followers by the example of his own conversation, shone with no light to those who trusted in their own justice; but upon those who acknowledged the darkness of their sins he shed the fire of his love. Christ is the cloud and the pillar, because he is upright, and firm, and upholds our infirmity; because he shines by night and does not shine by day, that they who see not may see; and they who see may become blind (Joan. IX). The sacrament of Christ also 223Cis manifest as it were by day in the flesh as it were in a cloud: but in the judgment as it were in the terror of night; because then there will be great tribulation, and it will shine as fire to the just, and burn the unjust.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 2.-- Phihahiroth, quae est inter Magdalum, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5 in Exod.) Videamus quae via praecipitur eligenda. De Othon, inquit, conversi, iter facite inter Epauleum et Magdalum, quod est contra Beelsephon, etc. Epauleum ascensio tortuosa, Magdalum turris, Beelsephon ascensio speculae, vel habens speculam, interpretatur. Credebat forsitan quod ostenderet Deus planum iter et molle, sed ascensio est et tortuosa. Non enim proclive iter est quo tenditur ad virtutes, sed ascenditur anguste et difficulter. 223DUnde Matth. VII: Arcta est enim et angusta via quae ducit ad vitam. Est ergo iter quo inceditur ascensio tortuosa, et ascensio speculae vel habens speculam. Ascensio ad actus pertinet, specula ad fidem; ostenditur ergo quia et in actibus et in fide multa est difficultas, multus labor: multae enim tentationes nobis occurrunt, multa offendicula, volentibus agere quae Dei sunt. Deinde in fide multa invenies tortuosa, plurimas quaestiones, objectiones haereticorum, contradictiones infidelium: hoc ergo iter agendum est sequentibus Dominum. Sed et turris est in hoc itinere, de qua dicitur Luc XIV: Quis vestrum volens turrim aedificare, non prius sedens computat sumptus, si habet unde perficiat? Ista est 224Aergo turris ex his ardua et excelsa virtutibus; quod videns Pharao dicit: Errant isti. Apud Pharaonem, qui Deum sequitur errare dicitur, quia iter sapientiae tortuosum, multas habens difficultates et anfractus. Si confitearis unum Patrem invisibilem, et unum unigenitum ejus Jesum Christum, et unum Spiritum sanctum, tortuosum, difficile et incredibile videtur hoc in infidelibus. Si dicis Deum majestatis crucifixum filium hominis esse, qui de coelo descendit: tortuosa videntur haec et difficilia; qui audit (nisi eum fide audiat), dicit quia errant isti. Sed tu fixus esto in hac fide, sciens quia Deus ostendit tibi hanc viam fidei, qui ait: Tollentes de Othon castra, collocate inter Epauleum et Magdalum.

224BFuisset. (ORIG.) Fugiens Aegyptum venis ad ascensionem operis et fidei, et ad aedificium turris: venis ad mare, et occurrunt tibi fluctus tentationum. Unde II Tim. III: Qui enim volunt pie vivere in Christo, persecutionem patiuntur, quia tentatio vita nostra est super terram. Sed si Moysen, id est legem Dei, sequeris, Aegyptus te insectatur. Sed vide quid fiet: Levavit, inquit, angelus Domini, qui antecedebat castra Israel, et abiit post illos. Elevavit et columnam nubis a facie eorum, et stetit post illos, et intravit inter castra Aegyptiorum et Israel. Haec columna nubis populo Dei efficitur murus: Aegyptiis imponit tenebras; non transfertur columna ignis ad Aegyptios, ut videant lucem: sed nubis, ut in tenebris maneant, quia dilexerunt tenebras, non 224Clucem. Et tu ergo si ab Aegyptiis recedas, si de potestate daemonum fugias, vide quanta tibi praeparantur auxilia, dummodo in fide fortis permaneas: nec Aegyptiorum equitatus et quadrigae perterreant, nec reclames contra Dei legem, id est Moysen, dicens: Tanquam non essent sepulcra in Aegypto, ita eduxisti nos.

VERS. 11-12.-- Forsitan non erant sepulcra, etc? (ORIG., homil. 5 in Exod.) Haec fatiscentis in tentatione animi verba sunt: beatus est qui sic tentationes excipit ut nulla ei cogitatio ambiguitatis obrepat. Petro etiam dicitur Matth. XIII: Modicae fidei, quare dubitasti? Tentationis et fragilitatis haec verba sunt. Alioquin falsum est, melius est enim in eremo mori quam Aegyptiis servire. Qui enim in 224Deremo moritur, hoc ipso quod separatus est ab Aegyptiis, id est rectoribus tenebrarum et e potestate Satanae, habuit aliquem profectum, etiamsi ad integrum non potuit pervenire. Melius est enim perfectam vitam quaerenti in itinere mori, quam nec proficisci. Unde eorum videtur falli opinio, qui dum arduum et periculosum iter virtutis exponunt, nec incipiendum judicant. Melius est enim in itinere mori, quam inter Aegyptios necari et falsis atque amaris fluctibus obrui.

VERS. 13-14.-- Aegyptios enim quos nunc, etc. (AUG. quaest. 5 in Exod.) Sicut enim vidistis Aegyptios hodie, non apponetis amplius videre eos in aeternum tempus. Quomodo autem sunt accipienda haec ververba, 225Acum viderint postea Israelitae Aegyptios? An quia isti qui tunc videbant, non eos ulterius viderunt, quia et illi mortui sunt qui sequebantur, et isti omnes quisque die mortis suae? Nam posteri eorum viderunt posteros illorum. An, non eos videbitis sicut hodie, intelligendum est, non sicut hodie persequentes et inimicos, ut nulla sit quaestio nec de aeterno tempore quod hic posuit, quia etsi videbunt se utrique tempore resurrectionis, non sic ut hodie.

VERS. 15-17.-- Quid clamas ad me? etc. (GREG.) Voces apud aures Dei non faciunt verba nostra, sed desideria. Aeternam enim vitam si ore petimus, nec corde desideramus, clamantes tacemus: si vero corde desideramus, ore tacemus, tacentes clamamus. 225BUnde in eremo cum populus vocibus perstreperet, et Moses a strepitu verborum taceret, silens auditur, et dicitur ei: Quid clamas ad me? In desiderio enim clamor secretus non pervenit ad aures humanas, divinas replet.

(ORIG., ut supra. ) Nulla vox Moysi auditur, et Dominus dicit: Quid clamas ad me? Velim scire quomodo sancti clamant sine voce ad Deum. Apostolus ad Gal. IV, dicit: Quia dabit Deus Spiritum Filii sui in cordibus nostris clamantem: Abba, Pater. Et addit: ipse Spiritus interpellat pro nobis gemitibus inenarrabilibus. Et iterum Rom. VIII: Qui autem scrutatur corda, scit quid desideret spiritus. Sic ergo postulante Spiritu sancto per silentium clamor sanctorum auditur. Jubetur Moses virga percutere mare, 225Cut ingredienti populo Dei cedat, et aquae quae timebantur, dextra laevaque murus effectae, non solum perniciem nesciant, sed etiam munimen exhibeant. Soliditatem recipit liquor, et solum maris arescit in pulverem: bonitatem Creatoris intellige, si voluntati ejus obtemperes, si legem ejus sequaris, elementa tibi contra naturam servire compellit. Audivi a majoribus traditum quod singulis tribubus singulae aquarum divisiones factae sunt, et propria unicuique via. Idque ostenditur ex eo quod dicitur psalmo CXXXV: Qui divisit mare Rubrum in divisiones, etc. Et alibi, psal. LXVII: Ibi Benjamin minor in stupore. Principes Juda, duces eorum, principes Zabulon et principes Nephthali: proprius unicuique tribui enumerari videtur ingressus. Apostolus 225Dbaptismum in Mose consummatum dicit in nube et in mari, ut qui baptizaris in Christo, in aqua et in Spiritu, scias te insectari Aegyptios et ad servitium revocare, rectores scilicet mundi, et spirituales nequitias, quibus ante servisti. Sed tu descendis in aquam, et evadis incolumis et novus ascendis peccatorum sordibus ablutus, paratus ad cantandum novum canticum. Aegyptii insequentes merguntur in abyssum, et si rogare videntur Jesum ne eos iterum mergat in abyssum.

(ID.) Aliter, si Aegyptum fugias, id est ignorantiae tenebras, si sequaris Mosen, id est Dei legem, occurrat autem mare, id est, contradicentium fluctus, percute et tu obluctantes undas virga, id est, verbo 226Alegis et vigilantia Scripturarum; iter tibi disputando per adversarios pande, et cedent undae victori, mirantibusque et stupore defixis qui ante contradicebant, legitimis disputationum lineis rectum fidei iter secabis, et in tantum doctrinae verbo proficies, ut auditores tui, quos tu in verba legis erudisti, jam contra Aegyptios, velut fluctus maris, insurgent, nec solum impugnent, sed et superent et exstinguant. Exstinguit enim Aegyptum, qui non agit opera tenebrarum, qui non carnaliter, sed spiritualiter vivit; qui cogitationes sordidas vel corde expellit, vel omnino non recipit, secundum illud Eph. VI: Assumentes scutum fidei, ut possitis omnia maligni ignita jacula exstinguere. Hoc ergo modo possumus etiam Aegyptios hodie videre mortuos, et ipsum 226BPharaonem, si tanta fide vivamus, ut Deus conterat Satanam sub pedibus nostris velociter per Jesum Christum.

VERS. 19-20.-- Tollensque se angelus Dei, etc. (STRAB.) Angelus, qui nuntius interpretatur, significat doctores, qui nobis praecepta vitae annuntiant, et cum nube, id est scientia Scripturarum castra Israel, id est Ecclesiam praecedunt, quia cum scientia Scripturarum praesident. Supervenientibus Aegyptiis retrorsum abit, quia tempore persecutionis opponent se pro subjectis. Nubes vero tenebrosa erat Aegyptiis, lucida Hebraeis.

VERS. 21-31.-- Tota nocte, etc. Quia toto tempore hujus vitae, quam nox significat, Spiritus sancti gratia aquas baptismi fidelibus meabiles facit, 226Cut de spirituali Aegypto in terram promissionis transeant: hostium vero suffocat exercitum.

(ISID.) Mare Rubrum baptismum Christi sanguine consecratum significat; hostes a tergo sequentes cum rege moriuntur, quia peccata praeterita in baptismo delentur, et diabolus suffocatur: premunt Aegyptii usque ad mare, et peccata usque ad baptismum. Post transitum maris Rubri cantat populus submersis hostibus infidelibus, et fideles de lavacro ascendentes, exstinctis peccatis, hymnum decantant dicentes: Cantemus Domino, gloriose enim, etc.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 2.-- Phihahiroth, which is between Magdalum, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5 in Exod.) Let us see what way is enjoined to be chosen. Turning back from Othon, he says, make your journey between Epauleum and Magdalum, which is over against Beelsephon, etc. Epauleum is interpreted a winding ascent, Magdalum a tower, Beelsephon the ascent of a watchtower, or having a watchtower. He believed perhaps that God would show a level and easy way, but it is an ascent, and a winding one. For the way by which one goes toward the virtues is not downhill, but the climb is narrow and difficult. 223DWhence Matthew VII: For the way which leadeth to life is strait and narrow. [Vulg. Mt 7:14 Quam angusta porta, et arcta via est, quae ducit ad vitam, "How narrow is the gate, and strait is the way that leadeth to life"] The journey therefore by which one goes forward is a winding ascent, and the ascent of a watchtower or having a watchtower. The ascent belongs to deeds, the watchtower to faith; it is shown therefore that both in deeds and in faith there is much difficulty, much labour: for many temptations meet us, many stumbling-blocks, when we would do the things that are God's. Then in faith thou wilt find many winding things, very many questions, the objections of heretics, the contradictions of unbelievers: this journey therefore must be made by those who follow the Lord. But there is also a tower on this road, of which it is said in Luke XIV: Which of you, wishing to build a tower, doth not first sit down and reckon the charges, whether he have wherewith to finish it? [Vulg. Lc 14:28 Quis enim ex vobis volens turrim aedificare, non prius sedens computat sumptus, qui necessarii sunt, si habeat ad perficiendum, "which are necessary, whether he have wherewith to finish it"] This then is 224Athe tower, high and lofty by these virtues; and Pharao, seeing it, says: These men are astray. With Pharao, he who follows God is said to go astray, because the way of wisdom is winding, having many difficulties and turnings. If thou confess one Father invisible, and his one only-begotten Jesus Christ, and one Holy Spirit, this seems winding, difficult and incredible among unbelievers. If thou say that the God of majesty was crucified and is the Son of man who came down from heaven, these things seem winding and difficult; and he who hears (unless he hear it with faith) says that these men are astray. But be thou fixed in this faith, knowing that God has shown thee this way of faith, who says: Taking up the camp from Othon, place it between Epauleum and Magdalum.

224BHe had been. (ORIG.) Fleeing Egypt thou comest to the ascent of works and of faith, and to the building of the tower: thou comest to the sea, and the waves of temptations meet thee. Whence II Tim. III: For they who will to live godly in Christ suffer persecution, [Vulg. 2Tim 3:12 Et omnes, qui pie volunt vivere in Christo Jesu, persecutionem patientur, "And all who will to live godly in Christ Jesus shall suffer persecution"] because our life upon earth is a temptation. But if thou follow Moses, that is, the law of God, Egypt pursues thee. But see what shall come to pass: There lifted himself up, he says, the angel of the Lord, who went before the camp of Israel, and he went away behind them. He lifted up also the pillar of cloud from before their face, and it stood behind them, and entered in between the camp of the Egyptians and Israel. [Vulg. Ex 14:19-20 Tollensque se angelus Dei, qui praecedebat castra Israel, abiit post eos: et cum eo pariter columna nubis, priora dimittens, post tergum stetit, inter castra Aegyptiorum et castra Israel, "the angel of God … and with him together the pillar of cloud, leaving the front, stood behind"] This pillar of cloud is made a wall to the people of God: on the Egyptians it lays darkness; the pillar of fire is not carried over to the Egyptians, that they may see the light: but the pillar of cloud, that they may remain in darkness, because they loved darkness, not 224Cthe light. And thou therefore, if thou withdraw from the Egyptians, if thou flee from the power of the demons, see how great helps are prepared for thee, provided thou remain strong in faith: let not the horsemen and chariots of the Egyptians terrify thee, nor do thou cry out against the law of God, that is, against Moses, saying: As though there were no sepulchres in Egypt, so hast thou led us forth. [Vulg. Ex 14:11 Forsitan non erant sepulchra in Aegypto, ideo tulisti nos ut moreremur in solitudine, "Perhaps there were no graves in Egypt, therefore thou hast taken us away"]

VERS. 11-12.-- Perhaps there were no sepulchres, etc? (ORIG., homil. 5 in Exod.) These are the words of a mind failing under temptation: blessed is he who so receives temptations that no thought of doubt creeps upon him. To Peter also it is said in Matthew XIII: O thou of little faith, why didst thou doubt? These are the words of temptation and of frailty. Otherwise it is false, for it is better to die in the desert than to serve the Egyptians. For he who dies in 224Dthe desert, by this very thing, that he is separated from the Egyptians, that is, from the rulers of darkness and from the power of Satan, has had some profit, even if he could not attain to the whole. For it is better for one seeking the perfect life to die on the road than not even to set out. Whence the opinion of those seems to be false who, while they set forth the way of virtue as arduous and perilous, judge that it is not even to be begun. For it is better to die on the road than to be slain among the Egyptians and overwhelmed by false and bitter waves.

VERS. 13-14.-- For the Egyptians whom you now, etc. (AUG. quaest. 5 in Exod.) For as you have seen the Egyptians today, you shall not again see them any more unto eternal time. [Vulg. Ex 14:13 Aegyptios enim, quos nunc videtis, nequaquam ultra videbitis usque in sempiternum, "the Egyptians whom you now see, you shall see no more for ever"] But how are these words ververba to be taken, 225Asince the Israelites afterwards saw Egyptians? Or is it because those who then saw them did not see them further, since both they died who were pursuing, and these all, each on the day of his own death? For their descendants saw the descendants of those. Or is it to be understood, you shall not see them as today, that is, not as today pursuing and enemies, so that there may be no question even about the eternal time which he has here set down; because although both shall see one another at the time of the resurrection, yet not so as today.

VERS. 15-17.-- Why criest thou to me? etc. (GREG.) It is not our words that make voices before the ears of God, but our desires. For if we ask eternal life with the mouth and do not desire it with the heart, in crying out we are silent: but if we desire with the heart, though we are silent with the mouth, in keeping silence we cry. 225BWhence in the desert, when the people made a din with their voices, and Moses was silent from the noise of words, being silent he is heard, and it is said to him: Why criest thou to me? For in desire the secret cry does not reach human ears, but fills the divine.

(ORIG., ut supra. ) No voice of Moses is heard, and the Lord says: Why criest thou to me? I would gladly know how the saints cry to God without a voice. The Apostle to the Galatians IV says: For God will give the Spirit of his Son into our hearts, crying: Abba, Father. [Vulg. Gal 4:6 Quoniam autem estis filii, misit Deus Spiritum Filii sui in corda vestra, clamantem: Abba, Pater, "And because you are sons, God hath sent the Spirit of his Son into your hearts"] And he adds: the Spirit himself intercedeth for us with unspeakable groanings. [Vulg. Rom 8:26 sed ipse Spiritus postulat pro nobis gemitibus inenarrabilibus, "the Spirit himself asketh for us"] And again in Romans VIII: And he that searcheth the hearts knoweth what the spirit desireth. So then, at the asking of the Holy Spirit, the cry of the saints is heard through silence. Moses is commanded to strike the sea with his rod, 225Cthat it may give way to the people of God as they enter, and that the waters which were feared, being made a wall on the right hand and on the left, may not only know no ruin, but may even afford a defence. The liquid takes on solidity, and the floor of the sea dries into dust: understand the goodness of the Creator; if thou obey his will, if thou follow his law, he compels the elements to serve thee against their nature. I have heard it handed down by our elders that for the several tribes there were made several divisions of the waters, and a way of its own for each. And this is shown from that which is said in Psalm CXXXV: Who divided the Red Sea into divisions, etc. And elsewhere, in Psalm LXVII: There is Benjamin the lesser in a stupor. The princes of Juda, their leaders; the princes of Zabulon and the princes of Nephthali: [Vulg. Ps 67:28 Ibi Benjamin adolescentulus, in mentis excessu; principes Juda, duces eorum; principes Zabulon, principes Nephthali, "There is Benjamin a youth, in ecstasy of mind"] an entrance seems to be enumerated proper to each tribe. The Apostle 225Dsays that a baptism was accomplished in Moses in the cloud and in the sea, so that thou who art baptized in Christ, in water and in the Spirit, mayest know that the Egyptians pursue thee and would call thee back to servitude, the rulers, namely, of the world, and the spiritual wickednesses which thou didst serve before. But thou goest down into the water, and comest out unharmed and risest up new, washed from the filth of thy sins, made ready to sing a new song. The Egyptians as they pursue are drowned in the deep, and if they seem to beseech Jesus not to drown them again in the deep.

(ID.) Otherwise, if thou flee Egypt, that is, the darkness of ignorance; if thou follow Moses, that is, the law of God, and the sea meet thee, that is, the waves of gainsayers, do thou also strike the struggling waves with the rod, that is, with the word 226Aof the law and with the vigilance of the Scriptures; open thyself a way by disputation through thy adversaries, and the waves will give place to the victor; and while they who before gainsaid stand amazed and fixed with astonishment, thou wilt cut a straight path of faith by the lawful lines of disputation, and thou wilt so far advance in the word of doctrine that thy hearers, whom thou hast instructed in the words of the law, will now rise up against the Egyptians like waves of the sea, and will not only assail but even overcome and extinguish them. For he extinguishes Egypt who does not the works of darkness, who lives not carnally, but spiritually; who either drives out filthy thoughts from his heart, or does not receive them at all, according to that of Ephesians VI: Taking the shield of faith, that you may be able to extinguish all the fiery darts of the wicked one. [Vulg. Eph 6:16 in omnibus sumentes scutum fidei, in quo possitis omnia tela nequissimi ignea extinguere, "in all things taking the shield of faith, wherewith you may be able to extinguish all the fiery darts of the most wicked one"] In this way therefore we can even today see the Egyptians dead, and Pharao himself, 226Bif we live with so great faith that God crush Satan under our feet speedily through Jesus Christ.

VERS. 19-20.-- And the angel of God lifting himself up, etc. (STRAB.) The angel, who is interpreted messenger, signifies the teachers, who announce to us the precepts of life, and with the cloud, that is, the knowledge of the Scriptures, go before the camp of Israel, that is, the Church, because with the knowledge of the Scriptures they preside. When the Egyptians come upon them he goes back behind, because in the time of persecution they set themselves in front on behalf of their subjects. But the cloud was dark to the Egyptians, bright to the Hebrews.

VERS. 21-31.-- All the night, etc. Because through the whole time of this life, which the night signifies, the grace of the Holy Spirit makes the waters of baptism passable to the faithful, 226Cthat they may pass over from spiritual Egypt into the land of promise: but it chokes the army of the enemy.

(ISID.) The Red Sea signifies the baptism consecrated by the blood of Christ; the enemies following at the back die together with their king, because past sins are blotted out in baptism, and the devil is choked: the Egyptians press on as far as the sea, and sins as far as baptism. After the passage of the Red Sea the people sing, the enemies being drowned; and the faithful, coming up from the laver, their sins extinguished, chant a hymn, saying: Let us sing to the Lord, for gloriously, etc.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 1.-- Tunc cecinit Moyses, etc. (ORIG., homil. 6 in Exod.) Multa cantica legimus in Scripturis, 226Dsed hoc primum omnium est quod, Aegyptiis et Pharaone submerso, post victoriam cantatur. Mos enim sanctorum est, ubi adversarius vincitur (quia sciunt non sua virtute, sed Dei gratia victoriam factam), hymnum Deo glorificationis offerre. Accipiunt tamen canentes tympana in manibus, sicut de Maria et Aaron refertur. Et tu ergo, si mare Rubrum transieris, si Aegyptios submergi videris, et Pharaonem praecipitari in abyssum, potes hymnum Deo gratanter canere, et dicere: Cantemus Domino gloriose, etc. Dignius autem hoc dices, si habueris in manu tympanum, id est, si carnem tuam crucifixeris cum vitiis et concupiscentiis.

Gloriose enim, etc. (ORIG.) Non sufficit, glorificatus 227Aest; aliud est enim glorificari, aliud gloriose glorificari. Cum enim Christus carnem pro nobis suscepit, glorificatus est, qui venit quaerere quod perierat, non tamen gloriose, quia non habebat speciem neque decorem; glorificatus est cum venit ad crucem, et pertulit mortem; unde Joan. XVII: Pater, venit hora, glorifica Filium tuum, etc. Erat ergo et gloria passio crucis, ubi et glorificatus, non gloriose quidem, sed humiliter; unde Phil. II: Humiliavit semetipsum, factus obediens usque ad mortem, mortem autem crucis. Et Sap. II: Morte turpissima condemnemus eum. In his ergo glorificatus est de se humiliter, non gloriose; unde Lucae XXIV: Quia oportebat pati Christum, et sic intrare in gloriam suam. Cum venerit in gloria Patris, et angelorum 227Bjudicare terram, et verum Pharaonem, id est diabolum, interficiet spiritu oris sui, cum refulgebit in majestate Patris: tunc gloriose glorificabitur, cum omnes honorificabunt Filium sicut honorificant Patrem.

Magnificatus est, equum et ascensorem dejecit in mare, etc. In signis Aegypti. Sed gloriose magnificatus est, quia Pharaonem et exercitum ejus submersit. Vel magnificatus est, miracula ante passionem faciendo, praedicando, in cruce moriendo: sed gloriose magnificatus est, cum resurrexit victor victo diabolo, spoliato inferno.

Equum, etc. (ORIG.) Homines qui nos insequuntur equi sunt; et omnes qui in carne nati sunt, figuraliter equi sunt, et habent ascensores suos. Sunt quos 227CDominus ascendit, et circumeunt omnem terram; de quibus dicitur Habac. III: Equitatus tuus salus. Alios ascendit diabolus et angeli ejus. Judas equus erat, et cum ascensorem habuit Dominum, de equitatu salutis erat: cum caeteris enim apostolis missus aegrotis salutem et languentibus praestitit sanitatem; ubi diabolo se substravit, qui post buccellam in eum intravit, equus Satanae factus, et illius habenis ductus contra Dominum equitavit. Omnes ergo persecutores equi sunt hinnientes, sed habent angelos malos, quibus aguntur ascensores, et ideo feroces sunt. Si videris persecutorem tuum nimis saevientem, scito quia ab ascensore pungitur, et ideo saevus est et truculentus.

VERS. 2.-- Iste Deus meus, etc. (ORIG.) Hic ergo 227Det Deus erit meus et Deus patris mei. Pater noster qui nos fecit et genuit, Christus est, qui dixit Joan. XVI: Vado ad Patrem meum, etc. Si ergo agnoscam quod Deus meus sit, glorificabo eum; si etiam quod patris mei, exaltabo eum: altior enim intellectus est quomodo Christus, ut unius Dei constringat et uniat veritatem, Deum suum dicit, quem natura Patrem novit.

VERS. 3.-- Dominus quasi vir pugnator, etc. (ORIG.) Alia littera: Dominus conteret bella. Amos IX: Dominus nomen est illi. Non modo pugnas visibiles conterit, sed eas etiam quae nobis sunt adversus principatus, et potestates, et rectores tenebrarum harum.--Dominus enim nomen est illi, et non est 228Aulla creatura cujus Dominus non sit. Quadrigas Pharaonis et exercitum ejus projecit in mare: electos ascensores, ternistratores demersit in mare Rubrum. Pharao, velut potentior in malitia, et regnum nequitiae tenens, quadrigas agit: non illi satis est unum equum ascendere, sed plures simul agit, et torto verbere cogit. Quos cum videris in luxuria turpiores, in crudelitate saeviores, in avaritia tetriores, scito de quadrigis esse Pharaonis, quas currui suo subjugat: in ipsis fertur et volitat per apertos scelerum campos, effusis habenis. Sunt alii ascensores electi, scilicet ad malitiam.

(ORIG.) Ternistratores ideo dicuntur, quia triplex est hominibus peccandi via. In facto enim, in dicto et in cogitatione peccant. Ideo ternistratores per 228Bsingulos dicuntur, quia istas tres in nobis possident vias peccandi, et semper speculantur et agunt insidiis, ut aut ille ex misero homine sermonem malum eliciat, aut ille iniquum opus extorqueat, aut ille cogitationem pessimam rapiat. Denique semen verbi Dei ubi cadit et deperit, triplex describitur locus, secus viam, in spinis, in petrosis. Econtrario terra bona affert triplicem fructum, sexagesimum, centesimum, tricesimum (Matth. XIII). Triplex enim est etiam bene agendi via, quia vel opere, vel cogitatione, vel verbo aliquid boni agitur; unde ait Apostolus I Cor. III: Qui aedificat supra fundamentum argentum, aurum, lapides pretiosos, triplicem bonorum indicans viam; viam malorum dicens ligna, fenum, stipulam. Isti ergo ternistratores sunt angeli 228Cnequam, qui in his viis observant, ut quemlibet agant in peccatum; quos immerget Dominus in mari Rubro, et ignitis fluctibus tradet in die judicii, et poenarum pelago teget.

VERS. 5, 6.-- Descenderunt in profundum quasi lapis. (ORIG.) Quia scilicet tales non erant lapides de quibus suscitarentur filii Abrahae, sed qui amarent profundum et liquidum elementum, id est amaram et fluxam praesentium voluptatem. Unde de ipsis dicitur: Demersi sunt quasi plumbum in aqua validissima. Peccatores graves sunt, et iniquitas super talentum plumbi legitur sedere: inde iniqui merguntur in profundum quasi plumbum, sancti vero ambulant super aquas, quia leves sunt, 228Det peccati pondere non gravantur. Christus super aquas ambulavit, qui vere peccatum non fecit: ambulavit et Petrus, sed paululum dubitavit; habuit enim aliquid plumbi, propter quod dicitur ei Matth. XIV: Modicae fidei, quare dubitasti? Omnes ergo per ignem salvantur, ut si forte de specie plumbi quidquam fuerit admissum, ignis decoquat et resolvat, ut efficiantur omnes aurum optimum, quia aurum terrae illius optimum quam habituri sunt sancti; et sicut fornax probat aurum, ita homines justos tribulatio. Veniendum est ergo omnibus ad conflatorium. Sedet enim Deus et conflat, et purgat filios Juda. Si quis multa opera bona et parum iniquitatis attulerit, illud parum tanquam igne resolvitur et purgatur, et totum remanet aurum purum. 229ASi quis plus plumbi attulerit, plus exuritur, ut amplius decoquatur; ut et si parum, tamen aliquid auri purgati resideat; qui vero totus plumbeus venit, demergitur quasi plumbum in aqua validissima.

VERS. 7-10.-- Misisti iram tuam, etc. (AUG. quaest. 55 in Exod.) Alia littera: Misisti spiritum tuum et cooperuit eos mare, etc. Ecce jam quinto commemoratur Spiritus Dei, etc., usque ad Scriptura tantummodo jubens sine adjutorio gratiae.

VERS. 11.-- Quis similis tui, etc. (ORIG., ut supra. ) Non simulacris gentium comparat Deum, nec daemonibus qui sibi falso nomen deorum asciscunt, sed dicit illos deos qui per gratiam et participationem Dei appellantur, secundum illud psal. LXXXI: 229BEgo dixi: Dii estis. Et ibid.: Deus stetit in synagoga deorum. Sed hi quamvis capaces sint Dei, et hoc nomine per gratiam donari videantur, nullus ei similis in potentia vel natura; quod autem dicitur, similes ei erimus, similitudo haec non ad naturam, sed ad gratiam revocatur, sicut pictura est similis ei cujus imago in ipsa exprimitur, quantum ad gratiam, sed longe dissimilis quantum ad substantiam. Illa enim species carnis est, et decor vivi corporis; ista colorum fucus, et cera tabulis sensu carentibus superposita. Nullus ergo in diis similis Domino, invisibilis scilicet, incorporeus, incommutabilis, sine initio et sine fine. Nullus creator omnium nisi Pater creans omnia per Filium, et in Spiritu sancto omnia sanctificans.

229CVERS. 12, 13.-- Extendisti, etc. (ORIG.) Monet me quod ait: Extendisti dexteram tuam et devoravit eos terra, quasi ut devorarentur a terra, causa fuerit quod dexteram extendit. Si consideres quomodo exaltatus Dominus in cruce tota die extendit manum ad populum non credentem, sed contradicentem, et quomodo infidelem populum qui clamavit: Crucifige, crucifige eum (Joan. XIX), mors iniquitatis oppressit, invenies quomodo extendit dexteram, et devoravit eos terra. Non tamen omnino desperandum. Si enim qui devoratus est, resipiscat, potest evomi sicut Jonas. Omnes nos aliquando terra devorabat et in inferni penetralibus retinebat: ideo Christus descendit non solum ad terras, sed etiam ad inferiora terrae: et ibi invenit nos devoratos, et 229Dsedentes in umbra mortis; inde educens nos non solum terrae, ne iterum devoremur, sed regnum praeparat coelorum.

(ORIG.) Impios etiam hodie terra devorat, qui semper de terra cogitant, terrena faciunt, de terra loquuntur, litigant, terram desiderant, in ea spem suam ponunt, ad coelum non respiciunt, futura non cogitant, judicium Dei non metuunt, nec promissa ejus desiderant; talem cum videris, dicite quia devoravit eum terra: et si quem videris luxuriae et voluptatibus corporis deditum, in quo nil animus valet, sed totum libido possidet, dicito quia devoravit eum terra.

VERS. 14, 15.-- Ascenderunt, etc. (ORIG.) Audierunt 230Agentes et iratae sunt, dolores apprehenderunt habitatores Philisthiim, etc. Quantum ad historiam, nullum de his gentibus interfuisse mirabilibus constat: quomodo ergo terrore territae vel iratae Philisthiim, et Moabitae, et Edom, et reliquae quas enumerat, nationes? Sed, spiritualiter, invenies quia Philisthiim, id est cadentes poculo; et Edom, scilicet terrenus, trepidant: et eorum principes cursitant et pavent constricti doloribus, cum vident regna sua, quae in inferno sunt, penetrata ab eo qui descendit in inferiora terrae, ut eriperet eos qui possidebantur a morte. Hinc timent et tremunt, quia senserunt magnitudinem brachii ejus; hinc etiam tabuerunt habitatores Chanaan, id est mutabiles et mobiles, cum viderunt moveri regna sua, et 230Balligari fortem. Veniat ergo super eos timor, et tremor: tremunt et timent daemones crucem Christi, in qua triumphati sunt, et exuti principatus eorum et potestate. Timor ergo et tremor cadent super eos, cum in nobis signum crucis fideliter fixum viderint, et magnitudinem brachii quod Dominus expandit in cruce, sicut dicit Isa. LXV: Tota die expandi manus meas ad populum non credentem, etc. Non te aliter timebunt, nec tremor tuus irruet super eos, nisi viderint in te crucem Christi, ut dicas: Mihi autem absit gloriari, nisi in cruce Domini nostri Jesu Christi (Gal. VI).

VERS. 15, 16.-- Fiant immobiles quasi lapis, etc. (ORIG.) Fieri tanquam lapidem non est natura esse lapidem. Non enim fieret nisi quod non erat. Hoc 230Cpropter illos diximus qui malae naturae Pharaonem vel Aegyptios dicunt fuisse, nec ex libertate arbitrii in hoc esse deductos; et qui creatorem Deum accusant, tanquam saevum, qui homines vertat in lapides: non dixit: Fiant tanquam lapides et tacuit, sed tempus statuit et mensuram dominationis, scilicet donec pertranseat populus tuus. Prior populus qui fuit ante nos, factus est tanquam lapis durus et incredulus, non ut lapidis natura maneat, sed donec pertranseat populus quem Dominus acquisivit. Unde Rom. XI: Caecitas enim ex parte contigit in Israel, donec plenitudo gentium intraret, et tunc Israel salvus fieret; qui per incredulitatem factus est tanquam lapis, unde Matth. III: Potens est enim Deus de lapidibus istis suscitare filios Abrahae. Sed 230Dcum creator sit omnium, quomodo acquisisse dicitur quod suum esse dubium non est? Dicitur enim in cantico Deuteronomii: Nonne hic ipse Dominus Deus tuus, qui fecit et creavit te, et acquisivit te? (Deut. XXXII.) Videtur enim unusquisque illud acquirere quod non fuit suum. Unde haeretici de Salvatore dicunt quia non erant sui quos acquisivit: dato enim pretio mercatus est homines quos Creator fecerat, et certum esse aiunt unumquemque emere quod suum non est. Apostolus enim ait I Cor. VII: Pretio empti estis. Sed audi Prophetam: Peccatis vestris venundati estis, et pro iniquitatibus vestris dimisi matrem vestram (Isa. L). Dei ergo creaturae omnes sumus, sed peccatis suis 231Aquisque venditur, et a Creatore discedit. Dei ergo sumus, secundum quod ab eo creati sumus: effecti vero servi diaboli, secundum quod peccatis nostris venundati sumus. Veniens autem Christus redemit nos servientes diabolo, cui nos peccando vendidimus, et ita videtur suos recepisse, quos creavit; et alienos acquisisse, qui alienum dominum peccando acquisierant. Sed recte nos redemisse dicitur, qui pro pretio nostro sanguinem suum dedit. Sed quid tale ut nos mercaretur diabolus dedit? Homicidium pecunia diaboli est, ille enim ab initio homicida est: fecisti homicidium, diaboli pecuniam accepisti. Idem dicendum est de adulterio, sacrilegio, furto, falso testimonio et hujusmodi: hac pecunia enim emit et efficit sibi servos omnes qui de hujusmodi 231Bcensu ejus quantumcunque susceperint.

VERS. 17.-- Introduces eos et plantabis, etc. (ORIG.) Non vult nos Dominus in Aegypto plantare, nec in dejectis et humilibus locis, sed in monte haereditatis suae. De hac plantatione Propheta dicit psal. LXXIX: Vineam de Aegypto transtulisti, ejecisti gentes et plantasti eam: dux itineris fuisti in conspectu ejus, et plantasti radices ejus, et replevit terram. Operuit montes umbra ejus, et arbusta ejus cedros Dei. Advertis quomodo plantat Deus et ubi? Non plantat in vallibus, sed in montibus, in excelsis et in sublimibus locis. Quos enim educit de Aegypto, id est saeculo ad fidem, non vult iterum in humilibus locare, sed conversationem eorum sublimem esse, et in montibus habitare et in excelsis, non per terram 231Crepere. Non ultra vult vineam suam in humili dejectos habere fructus, sed palmites sursum duci, in alto locari, traduces fieri: et traduces non in humilibus arboribus, sed in excelsis et in altissimis cedris Dei, prophetis scilicet et apostolis: quibus si nos adjungamur, vitis quam transtulit Deus de Aegypto, et per ramos eorum nostri palmites diffundantur, et ipsis innexi traduces efficiamur charitatis ad invicem vinculis nexi, fructum plurimum afferemus. Nam omnis arbor quae non facit fructum bonum excidetur et in ignem mittetur (Matth. III).

Firmissimo habitaculo tuo, etc. (ORIG.) In praeparata habitatione quam praeparasti, Domine. Vide clementis bonitatem Domini: non vult te inducere ad laborem, non vult ut ipse facias tibi habitaculum; 231Djam te ad praeparatam inducet habitationem. Unde Joan. IV: Alii laboraverunt, et vos in labores eorum introistis.

Sanctuarium tuum, etc. (ORIG.) Sanctimonium tuum, Domine, etc. Sanctimonium dicitur tabernaculum Dei, vel templum, eo quod sanctificat accedentes. Hebr. IX hoc non dicitur manu hominum factum, sed manu Dei: propter te ergo Deus et plantat et aedificat; agricola efficitur et structor, ne tibi aliquid desit. Unde Apostolus I Cor. III: Dei agricultura, Dei aedificatio estis. Quid est ergo hoc sanctimonium non manu hominum, sed Dei praeparatum. Prov. IX: Sapientia aedificavit sibi domum, id est, carnem Christi, non manu hominis, id est, non 232Aopere humano: templum carnis aedificatur in Virgine, sed lapis sine manibus excisus crevit, et factus est mons magnus (Dan. II): hoc est sanctimonium carnis assumptae, et sine manibus et sine opere hominum de monte humanae naturae et substantia carnis excisum.

VERS. 18.-- Dominus regnabit, etc. (ORIG.) Domine, qui regnas in saeculum et in saeculum et adhuc. Quoties dicitur in saeculum, longitudo quidem temporis, sed esse aliquis finis indicatur; et si in aliud saeculum dicitur, aliquid longius indicatur, sed tamen finis ponitur: et quoties saecula saeculorum nominantur, fortasse, licet ignotus nobis, tamen a Deo statutus aliquis terminus indicatur. Quod vero hic addidit et adhuc, nullum sensum termini 232Balicujus aut finem relinquit: quodcunque enim cogitaveris, in quo finem putes consistere, semper dicit tibi sermo propheticus, et adhuc; velut si tibi dicat, Putas in saeculum saeculi Dominum regnaturum, et adhuc putas in saecula saeculorum: et adhuc, et quodcunque dixeris de regni ejus spatiis, semper dicit tibi propheta, et adhuc.

VERS. 19.-- Filii autem Israel, etc. (ORIG.) Et tu si filius es Israel, potes ambulare per siccum in medio maris. Si fueris in medio nationis pravae et perversae, sicut lumen solis in medio peccatorum incedens, non te infundet humor peccati, et per hunc mundum transeuntem nulla libidinis unda resperget, nullus cupiditatis aestus verberabit. Qui Aegyptus est, et sequitur Pharaonem, vitiorum fluctibus 232Cmergitur. Qui vero sequitur Christum, et sicut ille ambulavit, ambulat, aqua ei fit murus dextra laevaque. Ipse autem media via incedit, per siccum non declinat ad dexteram neque ad sinistram, usquequo exeat ad libertatem, et hymnum victoriae Domino canat dicens: Cantemus Domino, etc.

VERS. 20, 21.-- Tympanum. (STRAB.) Secundum Hieronymum est genus tubae in summo habens stipulam vel fistulam; per quam vocem reddit sonoram. Chorus, secundum eumdem, pellis simplex cum duabus cicutis; per alteram inspiratur, per alteram sonum reddit: per utrumque ergo angusta intelligentia veteris legis designatur. Quam enim breve sit et minimum inde ostenditur, quia manu mulieris portatur. Secundum Cassiodorum tympanum est pellis extensa 232Dinter duo ligna: per quod maceratio carnis designatur. Chorus autem est multitudo simul canentium et dicitur chorus quasi coaevorum cantus. Quod ergo in choro aequaliter omnes voces resonant, per eum charitas significatur, qua omnes in Christo unum sumus. Quicunque ergo Deo in jubilum laudes reddit, in choro, hoc est in charitate, hoc agat, et in tympano, id est in mortificatione carnis. Unde psal. CL: Laudate eum in tympano et choro, etc.

VERS. 22.-- Tulit autem Moses Israel, etc. Baptizati per mundum quasi per desertum post transitum maris Rubri ducuntur, nondum habentes promissam patriam, sed sperantes et exspectantes per patientiam, 233Adum laboriosa et periculosa tentatione fatigantur. Sed ne revertantur corde in Aegyptum, nec ibi Christus deserit: nam et illa columna non recedit.

VERS. 23, 24.-- Et venerunt, etc. (ORIG., hom. 6 in Exod.) Post transitum maris Rubri, post choros et tympana, post triumphales hymnos, venitur ad Mara, aquam scilicet amaram, quam populus bibere non poterat; venitur ad periculum sitis. Sed clamavit Moses ad Dominum, et ostendit ei lignum, quo dulcis facta est aqua, et ibi posuit Deus illi justitias et judicium; ubi scilicet amaritudo, ubi sitis, et, quod gravius est, in abundantia aquarum, alius non erat locus dignior et aptior. Praeterea quod dicit, ostendit illi lignum Deus, et misit illud in aquam, et facta est dulcis, quasi non 233Bposset sine ligno aquam facere dulcere Deus, aut Moses lignum nesciret. Sed si lex secundum litteram accipiatur, amara est et ipsa Mara. Quid enim tam amarum quam ut puer octavo die circumcisionis vulnus accipiat, et rigorem ferri tenera patiatur infantia? Amarum est enim poculum legis, populus Dei, non qui in Mose baptizatus est in nube et in mari, sed qui in spiritu et aqua non possit bibere et gustare, nec hostiarum ferre amaritudinem, nec sabbati observantiam. Sed si Deus ostendat lignum quod mittatur in hanc amaritudinem, dulcis fit aqua legis et potabilis. Prov. III: Sapientia lignum vitae est in omnibus amplectentibus eam. Si ergo lignum sapientiae Christi missum fuerit in lege, ostendens quomodo debeat intelligi circumcisio, 233Csabbatum, lex leprae et hujusmodi, amaritudo legis vertitur in dulcedinem intelligentiae spiritualis, ut populus bibere possit qui idola reliquit et ad Deum confugit. Si audiat legem de sacrificiis docentem, continuo refugit et bibere non potest. In hanc ergo amaritudinem, id est litteram legis, posuit Deus justitias et testimonia, velut scilicet in vasculo quodam sapientiae suae et scientiae thesauros concludit [condidit]. Unde: Habemus thesaurum istum in vasis fictilibus (II Cor. III). Ut ergo possit haec aqua bibi, ostendit Deus lignum quod mittatur in eam, ut qui biberit non moriatur, non sentiat amaritudinem. Si quis ergo sine ligno vitae, id est, sine mysterio crucis, sine fide Christi, sine intelligentia spirituali biberit, amaritudine morietur. Unde: Littera occidit, 233Did est, aqua Mara, si bibatur non mutata et in dulcedinem versa.

VERS. 25-27.-- Qui ostendit ei, etc. (AUG., Quaest. 57 in Exod.) Genus ligni erat istam habens vim: an quolibet ligno facere poterat Deus, qui tanta mirabilia faciebat? Videtur tamen significare quod dictum est, ostendit ei, tanquam jam tale esset; nisi forte locus erat ubi ligna omnino non inveniebantur, ut hoc ipsum esset divini adjutorii, quod ei Dominus lignum ostendit, ubi nullum erat, et per lignum aquas dulces fecit, praefigurans gloriam et gratiam crucis. Sed in tali etiam ligno quis nisi creator et demonstrator laudandus est.

Ibi constituit ei praecepta. (ORIG., hom. 7 in Exod.) 234AQuare addidit: Postquam posuit illi Deus justitias et judicia, ibi tentabat eum dicens: Si audieris vocem Domini Dei tui. Videtur exponere quare justificationes et judicia dedit, ut scilicet tentaret eos si audirent vocem Domini Dei, et mandata ejus custodirent. Nam quantum ad priorem populum, quid eis boni aut perfecti murmurantibus et contradicentibus mandaretur, qui paulo post, mirabilium Dei obliti, ad idola convertuntur? Dantur ergo praecepta in quibus tententur; unde: Dedi vobis praecepta et justificationes non bonas in quibus non vivent (Ezech. XX). Tentati enim in praeceptis non inveniuntur fideles, ideo inventum est eis mandatum esse ad mortem quod erat ad vitam. Idem enim, si servetur, vitam; si non servetur, mortem generat. Non servantibus ergo dicuntur 234Bmandata non bona, et in quibus non vivant. Sed admisto ligno crucis, in dulcedinem versa, et spiritualiter intellecta, et servata sunt mandata vitae. Cunctum languorem quem posui in Aegypto, non inducam super te. Videtur dicere quod si quis servet mandata nullam aegritudinem patiatur, id est nec febres, nec alios dolores corporeos. Sed omnino Job justissimus, et omni pietate plenus, a capite usque ad pedes repletur ulcere pessimo. Sed justi non habent illas infirmitates quas Aegyptii. Aegyptus est mundus: diligere ergo mundum et quae in mundo sunt, Aegyptius languor est; observare menses, tempora, stellarum cursibus adhaerere, servire carni, luxuriae operam dare et deliciis vacare, Aegyptius languor est.

234CVenerunt autem, etc. (ORIG., ibid.) Non ante in Elim ducitur populus, ubi erant duodecim fontes aquarum, quibus nil inerat amaritudinis, sed amoenitas plurima ex densitate palmarum: sed prius ductus est ad aquas salsas et amaras, quibus per lignum a Domino monstratum dulcoratis, venitur ad fontes. Si historiam solam sequamur, non multum nos aedificat scire ad quem locum primo venerint, ad quem secundo. Primo autem ducitur populus ad litteram legis, a qua dum permanet in amaritudine sua, recedere non potest. Cum ergo per lignum vitae fuerit indulcorata, et spiritualiter intellecta, de Veteri Testamento transit ad Novum, et ad duodecim apostolicos fontes, ubi reperiuntur arbores septuaginta palmarum. Non enim soli duodecim apostoli 234Dfidem Christi praedicaverunt, sed et alii septuaginta, per quos palmas victoriae Christi mundus agnovit. Non sufficit ergo populo Dei aquam Mara bibere, quamvis indulcoratam per lignum vitae; solum Vetus Testamentum non sufficit ad bibendum, veniendum est enim ad Novum, de quo sine scrupulo et difficultate potatur. Judaei etiam nunc apud Mara sunt, id est ad aquas amaras: nondum enim ostendit eis Deus lignum de quo dulcescunt, quia nisi crediderint, non intelligent (Isa. VII).

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- Then sang Moses, etc. (ORIG., homil. 6 in Exod.) We read many canticles in the Scriptures, 226Dbut this is the first of them all, which, when the Egyptians and Pharao were drowned, is sung after the victory. For it is the custom of the saints, when the adversary is overcome (since they know that the victory was wrought not by their own strength but by the grace of God), to offer to God a hymn of glorifying. Yet as they sing they take timbrels in their hands, as is related of Mary and Aaron. And thou therefore, if thou hast crossed the Red Sea, if thou hast seen the Egyptians drowned and Pharao cast headlong into the deep, canst thankfully sing a hymn to God, and say: Let us sing to the Lord gloriously, etc. And thou wilt say this more worthily if thou have a timbrel in thy hand, that is, if thou have crucified thy flesh with its vices and concupiscences.

For gloriously, etc. (ORIG.) It is not enough, he is glorified; 227Afor it is one thing to be glorified, another to be gloriously glorified. For when Christ took flesh for us, he was glorified, who came to seek what had perished, yet not gloriously, because he had no comeliness nor beauty; he was glorified when he came to the cross and endured death; whence John XVII: Father, the hour is come, glorify thy Son, etc. [Vulg. Jo 17:1 Pater, venit hora: clarifica Filium tuum, "Father, the hour is come, clarify thy Son"] The passion of the cross therefore was also a glory, wherein he was glorified, yet not gloriously, but humbly; whence Philip. II: He humbled himself, becoming obedient unto death, even the death of the cross. And Wisdom II: Let us condemn him to a most shameful death. In these things therefore he was glorified concerning himself humbly, not gloriously; whence Luke XXIV: Because it behoved Christ to suffer, and so to enter into his glory. [Vulg. Lc 24:26 Nonne haec oportuit pati Christum, et ita intrare in gloriam suam?, "Ought not Christ to have suffered these things, and so to enter into his glory?"] When he shall come in the glory of the Father, and of the angels, 227Bto judge the earth, and shall slay the true Pharao, that is, the devil, with the spirit of his mouth, when he shall shine again in the majesty of the Father: then shall he be gloriously glorified, when all shall honour the Son as they honour the Father.

He is magnified, the horse and the rider he hath cast into the sea, etc. In the signs of Egypt. But he was gloriously magnified, because he drowned Pharao and his army. Or he was magnified in working miracles before the passion, in preaching, in dying on the cross: but he was gloriously magnified when he rose again a conqueror, the devil conquered, hell despoiled.

The horse, etc. (ORIG.) The men who pursue us are horses; and all who are born in the flesh are figuratively horses, and have their riders. There are those whom 227Cthe Lord mounts, and they go about the whole earth; of whom it is said in Habacuc III: Thy cavalry is salvation. [Vulg. Hab 3:8 Qui ascendes super equos tuos, et quadrigae tuae salvatio, "and thy chariots are salvation"] Others the devil and his angels mount. Judas was a horse, and while he had the Lord for his rider he was of the cavalry of salvation: for, sent with the other apostles, he gave health to the sick and soundness to the languishing; but when he laid himself down under the devil, who entered into him after the morsel, being made the horse of Satan and led by his reins he rode against the Lord. All persecutors therefore are neighing horses, but they have evil angels as riders by whom they are driven, and therefore they are fierce. If thou see thy persecutor raging beyond measure, know that he is being pricked by his rider, and therefore he is savage and truculent.

VERS. 2.-- This is my God, etc. (ORIG.) He therefore 227Dshall be both my God and the God of my father. Our Father, who made us and begat us, is Christ, who said in John XVI: I go to my Father, etc. [Vulg. Jo 16:28 vado ad Patrem, without meum] If therefore I acknowledge that he is my God, I will glorify him; if also that he is my father's, I will exalt him: for it is a higher understanding, how Christ, that he may bind together and unite the truth of the one God, calls him his God whom by nature he knows as Father.

VERS. 3.-- The Lord is as a man of war, etc. (ORIG.) Another reading: The Lord shall destroy wars. Amos IX: The Lord is his name. [Vulg. Am 9:6 Dominus nomen ejus, "The Lord is his name"; the wording printed is that of Jdt 16:3] He destroys not only visible battles, but those also which are ours against principalities, and powers, and the rulers of these darknesses.--The Lord indeed is his name, and there is no 228Acreature of which he is not Lord. The chariots of Pharao and his army he cast into the sea: the chosen riders, the ternistratores, he drowned in the Red Sea. [Vulg. Ex 15:4 currus Pharaonis et exercitum ejus projecit in mare: electi principes ejus submersi sunt in mari Rubro, "his chosen princes are drowned in the Red Sea"] Pharao, as being more powerful in malice and holding the kingdom of wickedness, drives chariots: it is not enough for him to mount one horse, but he drives many at once, and compels them with a twisted lash. Those whom thou seest fouler in luxury, more savage in cruelty, more loathsome in avarice, know that they are of the chariots of Pharao, which he yokes to his car: on these he is borne and flies over the open fields of crimes, with loosened reins. There are other chosen riders, chosen, that is, unto malice.

(ORIG.) They are called ternistratores for this reason, that there is a threefold way of sinning for men. For they sin in deed, in word and in thought. Therefore they are called ternistratores 228Bone by one, because they possess these three ways of sinning in us, and are ever on the watch and work by ambushes, that either the one may draw an evil word out of the wretched man, or the other wring out an iniquitous work, or the third snatch away a most wicked thought. Finally, where the seed of the word of God falls and perishes, a threefold place is described: by the wayside, among thorns, in stony ground. Contrariwise the good earth brings forth a threefold fruit, sixtyfold, hundredfold, thirtyfold (Matth. XIII). For there is also a threefold way of doing well, since something good is done either by work, or by thought, or by word; whence the Apostle says in I Cor. III: He who buildeth upon the foundation silver, gold, precious stones, [Vulg. 1Cor 3:12 Si quis autem superaedificat super fundamentum hoc, aurum, argentum, lapides pretiosos, "gold, silver, precious stones"] indicating a threefold way of good things; and calling the way of evil things wood, hay, stubble. These ternistratores therefore are wicked angels, 228Cwho keep watch in these ways, that they may drive anyone into sin; whom the Lord will plunge into the Red Sea, and will deliver over to fiery waves in the day of judgment, and cover with a sea of punishments.

VERS. 5, 6.-- They went down into the deep like a stone. (ORIG.) Because, namely, such men were not stones out of which the children of Abraham might be raised up, but such as loved the deep and liquid element, that is, the bitter and flowing pleasure of things present. Whence it is said of them: They were sunk like lead in most mighty water. [Vulg. Ex 15:10 submersi sunt quasi plumbum in aquis vehementibus, "they were drowned like lead in the mighty waters"] Sinners are heavy, and iniquity is read to sit upon a talent of lead: hence the wicked are drowned in the deep like lead, but the saints walk upon the waters, because they are light, 228Dand are not weighed down by the burden of sin. Christ walked upon the waters, who truly did no sin: Peter also walked, but doubted a little; for he had somewhat of lead, on account of which it is said to him in Matthew XIV: O thou of little faith, why didst thou doubt? All therefore are saved by fire, that if perchance anything of the kind of lead has been admitted, the fire may boil it away and dissolve it, so that all may be made the best gold, since the gold of that land is the best which the saints are to possess; and as the furnace tries gold, so tribulation tries just men. All therefore must come to the melting-pot. For God sits and melts, and purges the sons of Juda. If any man bring many good works and little of iniquity, that little is dissolved and purged as by fire, and the whole remains pure gold. 229AIf any man bring more lead, more is burned away, that it may be the more boiled down; so that even if little, yet something of purified gold may remain; but he who comes wholly leaden is plunged like lead in most mighty water.

VERS. 7-10.-- Thou hast sent forth thy wrath, etc. (AUG. quaest. 55 in Exod.) Another reading: Thou hast sent forth thy spirit and the sea covered them, etc. Behold, now for the fifth time the Spirit of God is mentioned, etc., as far as Scripture only commanding, without the help of grace.

VERS. 11.-- Who is like to thee, etc. (ORIG., ut supra. ) He does not compare God to the images of the nations, nor to the demons who falsely claim to themselves the name of gods, but he calls those gods who are so named by grace and by participation of God, according to that of Psalm LXXXI: 229BI have said: You are gods. And in the same place: God hath stood in the assembly of gods. But these, although they are capable of God, and seem to be endowed with this name by grace, yet none is like to him in power or in nature; and as to what is said, we shall be like to him, this likeness is referred not to nature but to grace, as a picture is like to him whose image is expressed in it as to grace, but far unlike as to substance. For that is the appearance of flesh, and the comeliness of a living body; this is a dye of colours, and wax laid upon boards that lack sense. None therefore among gods is like to the Lord, invisible, namely, incorporeal, unchangeable, without beginning and without end. None is the creator of all things save the Father, creating all things through the Son, and sanctifying all things in the Holy Spirit.

229CVERS. 12, 13.-- Thou hast stretched forth, etc. (ORIG.) That moves me which he says: Thou hast stretched forth thy right hand and the earth swallowed them, [Vulg. Ex 15:12 Extendisti manum tuam, et devoravit eos terra, "Thou hast stretched forth thy hand"] as though the cause of their being swallowed by the earth was that he stretched forth his right hand. If thou consider how the Lord, lifted up on the cross, all the day stretched out his hand to a people not believing but contradicting, and how death for iniquity crushed the unbelieving people that cried out: Crucify, crucify him (Joan. XIX), thou wilt find how he stretched forth his right hand, and the earth swallowed them. Yet one must not altogether despair. For if he who has been swallowed come to himself, he can be vomited forth like Jonas. All of us the earth once swallowed and held in the inmost parts of hell: therefore Christ came down not only to the lands, but also to the lower parts of the earth: and there he found us swallowed up, and 229Dsitting in the shadow of death; and leading us out thence, not only to the earth, lest we be swallowed again, but he prepares also the kingdom of heaven.

(ORIG.) The earth swallows up the wicked even today, who always think of earth, do earthly things, speak of earth, wrangle, desire earth, place their hope in it, look not up to heaven, think not of things to come, fear not the judgment of God, nor desire his promises; when thou seest such a one, say ye that the earth has swallowed him: and if thou see any man given over to lust and to the pleasures of the body, in whom the mind avails nothing but desire possesses the whole, say thou that the earth has swallowed him.

VERS. 14, 15.-- They went up, etc. (ORIG.) The nations heard 230Aand were angry, sorrows took hold of the inhabitants of Philisthiim, etc. [Vulg. Ex 15:14 Ascenderunt populi, et irati sunt: dolores obtinuerunt habitatores Philisthiim, "The peoples went up, and were angry: sorrows laid hold of the inhabitants of Philisthiim"] As to the history, it is certain that none of these nations was present at the wonders: how then were the Philisthiim, and the Moabites, and Edom, and the remaining nations which he enumerates, terrified with terror or angered? But, spiritually, thou wilt find that the Philisthiim, that is, falling by the cup, and Edom, namely, the earthly one, tremble: and their princes run to and fro and quake, bound fast with sorrows, when they see their kingdoms, which are in hell, penetrated by him who descended into the lower parts of the earth, that he might snatch away those who were possessed by death. Hence they fear and tremble, because they have felt the greatness of his arm; hence also the inhabitants of Chanaan pined away, that is, the changeable and the moveable, when they saw their kingdoms shaken, and 230Bthe strong one bound. Let fear then come upon them, and trembling: the demons tremble and fear the cross of Christ, in which they were triumphed over, and their principalities and power were stripped from them. Fear therefore and trembling shall fall upon them, when they shall see in us the sign of the cross faithfully fixed, and the greatness of the arm which the Lord spread out upon the cross, as Isaias LXV says: All the day I have spread forth my hands to a people that believeth not, etc. [Vulg. Is 65:2 Expandi manus meas tota die ad populum incredulum, "I have spread forth my hands all the day to an unbelieving people"; the wording printed is that of Rom 10:21] They will not fear thee otherwise, nor will the trembling of thee rush upon them, unless they see in thee the cross of Christ, so that thou mayest say: But God forbid that I should glory, save in the cross of our Lord Jesus Christ (Gal. VI).

VERS. 15, 16.-- Let them become unmoveable as a stone, etc. (ORIG.) To be made as a stone is not to be a stone by nature. For nothing would be made save what it was not. This 230Cwe have said on account of those who say that Pharao or the Egyptians were of an evil nature, and were not brought into this from the freedom of choice; and who accuse God the Creator as cruel, as one who turns men into stones: he did not say, Let them become as stones, and then keep silence, but he appointed a time and a measure of the domination, namely, until thy people pass over. The former people, which was before us, was made as a hard and unbelieving stone, not that the nature of a stone should remain, but until the people pass over which the Lord has purchased. Whence Rom. XI: For blindness in part hath happened in Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles should come in, and then Israel should be saved; [Vulg. Rom 11:25-26 quia caecitas ex parte contigit in Israel, donec plenitudo gentium intraret, et sic omnis Israel salvus fieret, "and so all Israel should be saved"] which by unbelief was made as a stone, whence Matthew III: For God is able of these stones to raise up children to Abraham. But 230Dsince he is the creator of all things, how is he said to have purchased what there is no doubt is his own? For it is said in the canticle of Deuteronomy: Is not this the same Lord thy God, who made and created thee, and purchased thee? (Deut. XXXII.) [Vulg. Dt 32:6 numquid non ipse est pater tuus, qui possedit te, et fecit, et creavit te?, "Is not he thy father, that hath possessed thee, and made thee, and created thee?"] For each man seems to acquire that which was not his own. Whence the heretics say of the Saviour that those whom he purchased were not his: for at a price given he bought men whom the Creator had made, and they say it is certain that everyone buys what is not his own. For the Apostle says in I Cor. VII: You are bought with a price. But hear the Prophet: For your sins you are sold, and for your iniquities have I put away your mother (Isa. L). [Vulg. Is 50:1 Ecce in iniquitatibus vestris venditi estis, et in sceleribus vestris dimisi matrem vestram, "Behold, for your iniquities you are sold, and for your wickednesses have I put away your mother"] We are therefore all creatures of God, but each is sold by his own sins, 231Aand departs from the Creator. We are God's, therefore, according to that we were created by him: but made servants of the devil, according to that we are sold by our own sins. But Christ coming redeemed us who served the devil, to whom we had sold ourselves by sinning; and thus he seems both to have received back his own, whom he created, and to have purchased strangers, who by sinning had acquired a strange master. But he is rightly said to have redeemed us, who gave his own blood for our price. But what did the devil give, that he should buy us? Manslaying is the devil's money, for he is a murderer from the beginning: thou hast done a manslaying, thou hast received the devil's money. The same is to be said of adultery, sacrilege, theft, false witness and the like: for with this money he buys and makes his own servants all who have received anything, however much, of this 231Brevenue of his.

VERS. 17.-- Thou shalt bring them in and plant them, etc. (ORIG.) The Lord will not have us planted in Egypt, nor in low and lowly places, but in the mountain of his inheritance. Of this planting the Prophet says in Psalm LXXIX: Thou hast brought a vineyard out of Egypt, thou hast cast out the nations and planted it: thou wast the guide of the way in its sight, and thou didst plant its roots, and it filled the earth. Its shadow covered the mountains, and its branches the cedars of God. [Vulg. Ps 79:10 plantasti radices ejus, et implevit terram, "thou didst plant its roots, and it filled the earth"; Migne prints replevit] Dost thou mark how God plants, and where? He plants not in valleys, but on mountains, in high and lofty places. For those whom he leads out of Egypt, that is, out of the world unto faith, he will not have set again in lowly places, but that their conversation be lofty, and that they dwell on mountains and on the heights, not creep along 231Cthe ground. He will not have his vineyard bear its fruits any longer cast down in a low place, but that the shoots be led upward, be set on high, become layered vines: and layered not upon lowly trees, but upon lofty and most high cedars of God, namely the prophets and apostles: to whom if we be joined, and the vine which God brought out of Egypt, and our shoots be spread through their branches, and being entwined with them we be made layers, bound one to another by the bonds of charity, we shall bring forth very much fruit. For every tree that maketh not good fruit shall be cut down and cast into the fire (Matth. III).

Thy most firm dwelling place, etc. (ORIG.) In the prepared habitation which thou hast prepared, O Lord. See the goodness of the merciful Lord: he will not bring thee to labour, he will not that thou thyself make thee a dwelling place; 231Dhe will now bring thee into a habitation already prepared. Whence John IV: Others have laboured, and you have entered into their labours.

Thy sanctuary, etc. (ORIG.) Thy holy place, O Lord, etc. [Vulg. Ex 15:17 sanctuarium tuum, Domine, "thy sanctuary, O Lord"] The tabernacle of God, or the temple, is called a holy place, because it sanctifies those who approach. In Hebrews IX this is said to be made not by the hand of men, but by the hand of God: for thy sake therefore God both plants and builds; he is made a husbandman and a builder, that nothing be wanting to thee. Whence the Apostle in I Cor. III: You are God's husbandry, God's building. What then is this holy place prepared not by the hand of men, but of God. Prov.
