IX: Wisdom hath built herself a house, that is, the flesh of Christ, not by the hand of man, that is, not 232Aby human work: the temple of the flesh is built in the Virgin, but the stone cut without hands grew, and became a great mountain (Dan. II): this is the holy place of the flesh assumed, and cut both without hands and without the work of men out of the mountain of human nature and the substance of flesh.

VERS. 18.-- The Lord shall reign, etc. (ORIG.) O Lord, who reignest unto the age and unto the age and yet further. [Vulg. Ex 15:18 Dominus regnabit in aeternum et ultra, "The Lord shall reign for ever and beyond"] As often as it is said unto the age, a length of time indeed is signified, but that there is some end; and if it is said unto another age, something longer is signified, but still an end is set: and as often as ages of ages are named, perhaps some term is signified which, though unknown to us, is yet appointed by God. But that he has here added and yet further, leaves no sense of any term 232Bor of an end: for whatsoever thou hast thought, in which thou supposest the end to stand, the prophetic word always says to thee, and yet further; as if it should say to thee, Thou thinkest the Lord will reign unto the age of the age, and yet further thou thinkest unto the ages of ages: and yet further, and whatsoever thou shalt have said of the spaces of his kingdom, the prophet always says to thee, and yet further.

VERS. 19.-- But the children of Israel, etc. (ORIG.) And thou, if thou art a son of Israel, thou canst walk on dry ground in the midst of the sea. If thou shalt be in the midst of a depraved and perverse nation, going as the light of the sun in the midst of sinners, the moisture of sin shall not soak thee, and as thou passest through this world no wave of lust shall bespatter thee, no heat of covetousness shall lash thee. He who is Egypt, and follows Pharao, is drowned in the waves of vices. 232CBut he who follows Christ, and walks as he walked, to him the water becomes a wall on the right hand and on the left. He himself goes forward by the middle way, over the dry ground he turns aside neither to the right hand nor to the left, until he come out to liberty, and sing a hymn of victory to the Lord, saying: Let us sing to the Lord, etc.

VERS. 20, 21.-- A timbrel. (STRAB.) According to Jerome it is a kind of trumpet having at the top a reed or a pipe; through which it renders a sounding voice. The choir, according to the same, is a simple skin with two hemlock pipes; through the one it is blown into, through the other it renders a sound: by both therefore is signified the narrow understanding of the old law. For how brief and how small it is, is shown from this, that it is carried by the hand of a woman. According to Cassiodorus a timbrel is a skin stretched 232Dbetween two pieces of wood: by which the maceration of the flesh is signified. But a choir is a multitude singing together, and it is called chorus as it were the song of those of one age. Because therefore in a choir all the voices sound equally, by it charity is signified, whereby we are all one in Christ. Whosoever therefore renders praises to God in jubilation, let him do this in the choir, that is, in charity, and in the timbrel, that is, in the mortification of the flesh. Whence Psalm CL: Praise ye him with timbrel and choir, etc.

VERS. 22.-- And Moses took Israel, etc. The baptized are led through the world as through a desert after the passage of the Red Sea, not yet having the promised country, but hoping and awaiting it through patience, 233Awhile they are wearied by laborious and perilous temptation. But lest they turn back in heart into Egypt, neither there does Christ forsake them: for that pillar too does not withdraw.

VERS. 23, 24.-- And they came, etc. (ORIG., hom. 6 in Exod.) After the passage of the Red Sea, after the choirs and the timbrels, after the triumphal hymns, one comes to Mara, that is, to water that is bitter, which the people could not drink; one comes to the peril of thirst. But Moses cried to the Lord, and he showed him a wood, by which the water was made sweet, and there God set for him justices and judgment; where, namely, there was bitterness, where there was thirst, and, what is heavier, in an abundance of waters, there was no other place more worthy and more fitting. Besides, as to what he says, God showed him a wood, and he cast it into the water, and it was made sweet, as though God could not 233Bmake the water sweet without a wood, or as though Moses did not know the wood. But if the law be taken according to the letter, it is bitter, and is itself Mara. For what is so bitter as that a child should receive the wound of circumcision on the eighth day, and that tender infancy should suffer the hardness of the iron? For the cup of the law is bitter to the people of God, not the people that was baptized in Moses in the cloud and in the sea, but that which in spirit and in water cannot drink and taste it, nor bear the bitterness of the victims, nor the observance of the sabbath. But if God show a wood to be cast into this bitterness, the water of the law becomes sweet and drinkable. Prov. III: Wisdom is a tree of life to all that embrace her. [Vulg. Pr 3:18 Lignum vitae est his qui apprehenderint eam, "She is a tree of life to them that lay hold on her"] If therefore the wood of the wisdom of Christ shall have been cast into the law, showing how circumcision is to be understood, 233Cand the sabbath, and the law of leprosy and the like, the bitterness of the law is turned into the sweetness of spiritual understanding, so that the people can drink which has left the idols and taken refuge with God. If it hear the law teaching about sacrifices, it at once shrinks back and cannot drink. Into this bitterness therefore, that is, into the letter of the law, God set justices and testimonies, as it were, namely, into a certain little vessel he shuts up [condidit] the treasures of his wisdom and knowledge. Whence: We have this treasure in earthen vessels (II Cor. III). That this water therefore may be drunk, God shows a wood to be cast into it, that he who drinks may not die, may not feel the bitterness. If therefore any man drink without the wood of life, that is, without the mystery of the cross, without the faith of Christ, without spiritual understanding, he shall die of the bitterness. Whence: The letter killeth, 233Dthat is, the water of Mara, if it be drunk unchanged and not turned into sweetness.

VERS. 25-27.-- Who showed him, etc. (AUG., Quaest. 57 in Exod.) There was a kind of wood that had this power: or could God have done it with any wood whatever, who was doing such great wonders? Yet what is said seems to signify, he showed him, as though it were already such; unless perhaps it was a place where no wood at all was to be found, so that this very thing was of the divine help, that the Lord showed him a wood where there was none, and by the wood made the waters sweet, prefiguring the glory and the grace of the cross. But even in such a wood who is to be praised save the Creator and the Shower of it.

There he appointed for him precepts. (ORIG., hom. 7 in Exod.) 234AWhy did he add: After God set for him justices and judgments, there he tempted him, saying: If thou wilt hear the voice of the Lord thy God. [Vulg. Ex 15:25 ibi constituit ei praecepta, atque judicia, et ibi tentavit eum, "there he appointed for him precepts and judgments, and there he tempted him"] He seems to expound why he gave justifications and judgments, namely that he might tempt them whether they would hear the voice of the Lord God, and keep his commandments. For as to the former people, what good or perfect thing could be commanded to them who murmured and gainsaid, who a little after, forgetting the wonders of God, turn to idols? Precepts therefore are given in which they may be tempted; whence: I gave you precepts and justifications that are not good, in which they shall not live (Ezech. XX). [Vulg. Ez 20:25 Ergo et ego dedi eis praecepta non bona, et judicia in quibus non vivent, "Therefore I also gave them precepts that were not good, and judgments in which they shall not live"] For being tempted in the precepts they are not found faithful, and therefore it was found that what was unto life was unto death for them. For the same thing, if it be kept, begets life; if it be not kept, death. To those therefore who keep them not 234Bthe commandments are called not good, and such as they shall not live in. But with the wood of the cross mingled in, being turned into sweetness and spiritually understood, the commandments of life have also been kept. All the languor which I laid upon Egypt I will not bring upon thee. He seems to say that if a man keep the commandments he shall suffer no sickness, that is, neither fevers, nor other bodily pains. But Job, altogether most just and full of all piety, is filled from the head to the feet with the worst ulcer. But the just do not have those infirmities which the Egyptians have. Egypt is the world: to love the world therefore and the things that are in the world is the Egyptian languor; to observe months, seasons, to cling to the courses of the stars, to serve the flesh, to give labour to luxury and to be at leisure for delights, is the Egyptian languor.

234CAnd they came, etc. (ORIG., ibid.) The people is not led into Elim before, where there were twelve fountains of waters, in which there was nothing of bitterness, but very great pleasantness from the thickness of the palm trees: but first it was led to the salt and bitter waters, which being sweetened by the wood shown by the Lord, one comes to the fountains. If we follow the history alone, it does not much edify us to know to what place they came first, to what second. But first the people is led to the letter of the law, from which, while it remains in its bitterness, it cannot withdraw. When therefore it has been sweetened by the wood of life, and spiritually understood, it passes from the Old Testament to the New, and to the twelve apostolic fountains, where are found seventy trees of palms. For not the twelve apostles alone 234Dpreached the faith of Christ, but seventy others also, through whom the world knew the palms of Christ's victory. It is not enough therefore for the people of God to drink the water of Mara, although sweetened by the wood of life; the Old Testament alone does not suffice for drinking, for one must come to the New, of which one drinks without scruple and without difficulty. The Jews also are even now at Mara, that is, at the bitter waters: for God has not yet shown them the wood by which they grow sweet, because unless they shall have believed, they shall not understand (Isa. VII). [Vulg. Is 7:9 Si non credideritis, non permanebitis, "If you will not believe, you shall not continue"]

CAPUT XVI.

VERS. 2.-- Et murmuravit omnis congregatio Israel, etc. (ORIG., homil. 7 in Exod.) O ingratus 235Apopulus! desiderat Aegyptum, qui Aegyptios vidit exstinctos; et carnes Aegypti, qui Aegyptiorum carnes videt et piscibus datas et volatilibus! Murmurat adversus Mosen, imo Deum; sed primo ignoscitur, et secundo et fortassis tertio: sed si non desinant, sed persistant, audi quid sequitur, in libro Numerorum refertur (Num. XXI), et idem Apostolus retexit dicens (I Cor. X): Neque murmuraveritis, sicut quidam eorum murmuraverunt, et a serpentibus perierunt. Scripta sunt haec propter nos, in quos fines saeculorum devenerunt. Sed si non desinimus murmurare et conqueri contra Deum, incurremus simile offensionis periculum vel exemplum. Cum enim de coelesti intemperie, fructuum infecunditate, imbrium raritate, et de prosperitate aliorum et aliorum improsperitate 235Bcausamur, contra Deum murmuramus. Sed haec primo ignoscuntur, in persistentes graviter vindicantur, quia mittentur in eos serpentes, id est, immundis et veneficis spiritibus tradentur, qui occultis morsibus penetralia cordis depascantur.

(ORIG.) Propter correctionem legentium indicatur populi peccatum: quia murmuraverit et ingratus fuerit beneficiis divinis, cum coeleste manna susceperit. Secundo mense, quinta decima die mensis, quod non sine ratione scriptum est. Recordare de legibus paschae quae dicta sunt (Num. IX), et invenies ibi hoc esse tempus quod statuitur ad secundum pascha faciendum his qui immundi in anima fuerint, vel occupati negotiis peregrinis. Qui non fuerant immundi anima, vel non longe peregrinabantur, 235Cquarta decima die primi mensis fecerunt pascha; longe vero peregrini, et immundi, secundum pascha in hoc tempore faciunt, in quo manna de coelo descendit. In die quo primum pascha fit, non descendit manna, sed in quo secundum: primum pascha populi Judaici est; secundum, nostrum. Nos enim fuimus in anima immundi, qui colebamus idola; et peregrini, et longinqui fuimus, hospites et peregrini testamentorum Dei, etc. (Eph. II.) Sed datur manna de coelo, non in die quo primum pascha fit, sed quo secundum: Panis enim qui de coelo descendit (Joan. VI) non venit ad illos qui primam solemnitatem celebrabant, sed ad nos, qui suscepimus secundam. Etenim pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus, qui verus homo panis de coelo descendit (I Cor. V.) Videamus 235Dinterim quid in hac die gestum monstretur: Quarta decima die mensis secundi murmuravit populus, etc.

VERS. 3.-- Sedebamus super ollas, etc. Greges ovium habebant et armenta boum, de quibus comedere poterant; sed desiderio piscium et avium, quibus Aegyptus abundat, aestuabant.

VERS. 4.-- Ecce pluam, etc. (ORIG., homil. 8 in Exod.) Vellem primo super hanc scripturam cum Judaeis habere sermonem, quibus credita sunt eloquia Dei, quid illis videatur de eo quod dicit: Sex diebus continuis colligetis, die autem sexta duplum. Apparet ergo sextum diem nominari qui ante sabbatum ponitur; sabbatum autem septimus dies est. 236AQuaero ergo qua die coepit manna dari? et volo comparare Dominicam nostram cum sabbato Judaeorum. Ex Scriptura enim apparet quia die Dominica primum datur manna, sex diebus continuis collectum; septima, quae est sabbatum, cessatur. Quia ergo in die Dominica pluit Deus manna, et non in sabbato, intelligant Judaei jam tunc praelatam esse Dominicam nostram suo sabbato; et indicatum est quod non in ipso Dei gratia ad eos descenderit de coelo, et panis coelestis, qui est sermo Dei, ad eos non pervenerit; unde Ose. III: Diebus multis sedebunt filii Israel sine rege, sine principe, sine hostia, sine sacrificio, sine sacerdote. In nostra autem Dominica semper Dominus pluit manna, id est coelestia verba quae nobis laeta sunt. Illi infelices suspirant etiam 236Bmanna sicut patres eorum, et non merentur accipere; non enim possunt manducare quod est minutum sicut semen coriandri, et candidum sicut pruina. Nihil enim in verbo Dei minutum, subtile et spirituale sentiunt, sed totum pingue et crassum. Incrassatum est enim cor populi hujus. Interpretatio quoque nominis hujus hoc sonat. Manna enim interpretatur Quid est hoc? Ipsa vis nominis ad discendum invitat, ut cum audis legem Dei recitari, inquiras semper et dicas doctoribus, Quid est hoc? Tu ergo si vis manducare manna, et suscipere verbum Dei, scito minutum esse et valde subtile, sicut semen coriandri: habet enim aliquid oleris quo nutrire et recreare possit infirmos. Infirmus enim olus manducat (Rom. XIV). Habet enim aliquid et rigoris, et ideo ut pruina; 236Ccandoris autem et dulcedinis plurimum. Quid est candidius, splendidius eruditione divina? quid dulcius, suavius eloquiis Dei, quae sunt super mel et favum?

Panes. Manna scilicet: locutio est qua panis pro alimento ponitur; amat autem Scriptura pluraliter magis panes quam singulariter dicere panem.

Ut tentem. Tentatio ista probatio est, non ad peccatum seductio; nec ideo probantur, ut Deus non noverit: sed ipsos ipsis ostendit quo humiliores fiant ad petendum adjutorium et ad noscendam Dei gratiam.

VERS. 5.-- Die autem sexto, etc. (ORIG., hom. 7.) In die sexto duplum colligatur in repositione, quantum sufficiat etiam sabbato. Sexta dies, haec 236Dvita in qua nunc sumus, unde in sex diebus fecit Deus hunc mundum; in hac ergo die tantum reponere debemus, quantum sufficiat in futuro. Si quid enim hic boni operis acquiras, si quid justitiae, misericordiae et pietatis recommendas, hoc tibi in futuro erit cibus. Qui enim acquirit hic decem talenta (Luc. XIX), ibi accipiet decem civitates, et quae seminaverit homo, haec et metet (Gal. VI). Divites hujus saeculi ea recondunt quae in hoc saeculo, imo cum hoc saeculo corrumpuntur: bona autem recondita permanent usque in crastinum. Scriptum est autem. Qui infideles fuerunt, servaverunt de manna, et ebullierunt ex eo vermes et computruit; quod autem pro die sabbati reponebatur, permansit integrum. Et tu 237Aergo si propter praesentem vitam et amorem saeculi thesaurizas, continuo vermes ebulliunt, de quibus dicitur Isa. LXVI: Vermis eorum non moritur; hos generat avaritia et divitiarum caeca cupiditas his qui habent pecuniam, et videntes in necessitate fratres suos, claudunt viscera sua ab eis. Unde Apostolus (I Tim. VI) praecipit divitibus non sublime sapere, neque sperare in incerto divitiarum, etc.; nec aliunde fiunt vermes in nobis, nisi ex manna, id est verbo Dei. Unde Joan. XV: Nisi venissem et locutus eis fuissem, etc. Post susceptum ergo verbum Dei si quis peccat, efficitur ipsum verbum vermis, qui ejus conscientiam semper fodiat et arcana pectoris rodat.

VERS. 6.--(ORIG.) Vespere scietis, etc. Ad vesperam 237Bscietis quia ego Dominus; mane autem videbitis majestatem Domini. Respondeant mihi Judaei quomodo ad vesperam cognoscitur Dominus, et mane videatur majestas ejus? Ubi agnitus est ad vesperam, et ejus majestas visa est mane? Sed non intelligitur hoc nisi in Evangelio (Matth. XXVIII), in quo vespere sabbati qui lucescit in prima sabbati venit Maria Magdalene et altera Maria ad sepulcrum, et invenerunt lapidem revolutum a monumento, terrae motum factum, et monumenta aperta esse, et centurionem et custodes sepulcri dicentes: Vere hic Filius Dei erat. Agnitus est in hac vespera Dominus, quia ipse Dominus erat: agnitus est in virtute resurrectionis, et mane gloria ejus visa est. Cum venissent aliae mulieres prima sabbati valde mane, invenerunt angelos 237Ccum ingenti claritate sedentes ad sepulcrum, et dicentes: Non est hic, sed surrexit a mortuis. Venite et videte locum, etc. (Luc. XXIV.) Visa est ergo majestas ejus mane, quia et per angelos resurrectio nuntiata est.

VERS. 7-11.-- Nos vero quid sumus, etc. (AUG., quaest. 59 in Exod., tom. III.) Non tantum se valere quantum Deum voluerunt, etc., usque ad Spiritum sanctum Deum esse monstravit subjungens, Act. V: Non hominibus mentitus es, sed Deo.

VERS. 12-13.-- Vespere comedetis carnes (ORIG., ut supra. ) Moraliter intelligere possumus quod unicuique nostrum mane est et initium diei, tempus quo primum illuminamur et ad lucem fidei accendimur. In hoc tempore carnes verbi non possumus 237Dedere, id est, perfectae doctrinae capaces nondum sumus; sed post longa exercitia, post profectum plurimum, cum jam proximi sumus ad vesperam, id est perfectionis fidem, tunc solidioris cibi capaces efficimur. Festinemus ergo manna suscipere, quod prout vult quisque saporem reddit in ore. Unde Matth. IX: Fiat tibi secundum fidem tuam. Et tu ergo, si verbum Dei quod in Ecclesia praedicatur, tota fide et devotione suscipias, fiat tibi quodcunque desideras. Si tribularis, consolatur te, dicens: Cor contritum et humiliatum Deus non spernit (Psal. L); si laetaris pro spe futura, cumulat tibi gaudia, dicens: Laetamini in Domino et exsultate, justi (Psal. XXXI); si iracundus es, dicit: Desine ab ira, et derelinque 238Aindignationem (Psal. XXXVI); si in doloribus sis, sanat te, dicens: Dominus sanat languores tuos omnes (Matth. IV); si paupertate consumeris, consolando dicit: Dominus allevat de terra inopem, et de stercore erigit pauperem (Psal. CXII). Si quis tamen infideliter suscipit, nec comedit, sed abscondit, vermes ex eo ebulliunt. Nec turberis si ad hoc ducitur verbum Dei, ut putetur fieri vermis; ipse enim ait: Ego sum vermis, et non homo (Psal. XXI). Sicut enim ipse est qui fit aliis in ruinam, aliis autem in resurrectionem (Luc. II); ita et ipse est qui in manna fidelibus dulcedo mellis, infidelibus vermis efficitur. Ipse est enim sermo Dei qui iniquorum mentes arguit et correctionum spiculis conscientiam terebrat. Ipse est enim qui efficitur eis quibus aperit Scripturas 238Bignis, qui dicunt: Nonne cor nostrum ardens erat in nobis? etc. (Luc. XXIV.) Aliis ignis est, quibus cremat spinas de terra mala, id est, malas cogitationes in corde consumit; et ideo peccatoribus neque vermis arguens unquam moritur, neque ignis exurens exstinguitur: justis autem et fidelibus permanet dulcis et suavis. Unde dicitur psal. XXXIII: Gustate et videte, etc.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) Alia littera: Vespere manducabitis carnes, et mane replebimini panibus. Dicant Judaei quae consequentia sit, ut vespere carnes sine panibus comedant, aut mane panes sine pulmento? quid in hoc divini muneris, quid temperamenti gratiae coelestis ostenditur? Hoc est, in quo agnoscere Deum possunt, qui carnes in vespera sine panibus comedunt, 238Cet majestatem Dei apparere dicunt, si rursum panes sine carnibus comedantur? servent sibi ista, his qui acquiescentes vobis, in coturnicibus putant agnosci Deum; nos autem, quibus in fine ad vesperam mundi Verbum est caro factum, in illa tantum carne agnosci dicimus Deum, quem suscepit ex Virgine: has enim Verbi Dei carnes neque mane neque meridie quis manducavit, sed ad vesperam: adventus enim Domini in carne ad vesperam factus est, sicut et Joannes dicit: Pueri, novissima hora est (I Joan. II) .-- Mane, inquit, replebimini panibus. Nobis ergo panis Verbum Dei est: Ipse est panis vivus qui de coelo descendit, et dat vitam mundo (Joan. VI). Sed quod dicit mane dari hunc panem, cum incarnatus sit ad vesperam, sic intelligendum 238Dest quod ad vesperam mundi et prope finem Dominus venerit; sed adventu suo, cum sit sol justitiae, novum mane credentibus restauravit; novum, scilicet scientiae lumen, in quo replentur panibus, qui susceperunt praecepta ejus. Nec mireris quia verbum dicitur et caro Dei, et panis, et lac dicitur et olera, et pro mensura credentium, et possibilitate sumentium diverse nominatur. Vel post resurrectionem mane factam credentes replevit panibus, quia libros legis et prophetarum prius ignotos ad instructionem Ecclesiae concessit, et ipse fit panis in Evangelio, caeteri vero libri legis et prophetarum vel historiarum plures panes dicantur, quibus repleantur credentes ex gentibus. Unde: Ascendent in montem, et 239Abibent vinum, unguento ungentur. Sic ergo competenter carnes ad vesperam accipimus et panibus mane replemur: quia nec poteramus mane carnem edere, nondum enim erat tempus; nec meridie, vix enim angeli edunt carnes meridie et illi fortassis ordini meridianum tempus conceditur.

Et mane. Nono die quando Christus, sol justitiae, adventu suo credentibus reparavit perdita.

(ISID.) Per volucrum escas figuratur praedicatio divinitus missa, quae transcurrit per verba sonantia, quasi per aerem volatilia pennata, quibus per fidem pascuntur, qui ad patriam coelestem proficiscuntur. Vel veteris legis eloquia, quae plebem carnalem tanquam carne alebat per verba divinitus missa, quasi per volatilia. Unde et vespere datur, quia 239Bcuncta carnaliter illis concessa finem erant habitura. Manifesto autem lumine fidei datur manna, quod est Christus, panis scilicet vivus qui de coelo descendit (Joan. VI), qui per nubes evangelicas universo orbi pluitur: non jam murmuranti et tentati Synagogae, sed credenti et speranti Ecclesiae.

VERS. 14.-- In similitudinem pruinae super terram. Pruina Christum significat: manna enim Dominica die primum datum est, et Christus Dominica die resurrexit.

Tusum. Id est mundissimum. Sicut enim frumentum, vel milium, vel aliud tale in mortariolo pilo teritur et folliculis exuitur, sic corpus Christi per tribulationem passionis et pilo crucis mortalitate exutum est. Vel minutum manna subtilitas coelestis 239Cdoctrinae, quae pilo tunditur ut litteralis sensus exuatur.

VERS. 15.-- Man hu. (GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 7.) Quid per manna filiis Israel desuper datum, nisi admiratio supernae dulcedinis designatur? Dulcis enim cibus, qui de supernis accipitur; man hu vocatur, quod interpretatur Quid est hoc? quod dicimus quando cernimus quod nescientes admiramur. Supernum igitur manna anima percipit, cum per vocem compunctionis elevata novam speciem aeternae refectionis obstupescit, ut divina dulcedine repleta jure respondeat Quid est hoc? quia dum ab infima cogitatione suspenditur, ea quae de supernis conspicit et insolite miratur. Et cum per hanc vocem torporis nostri surditas rumpitur, vetustae vitae usus 239Dmutatur, ut anima, superno spiritu afflata, et in supremis appetat quod contempserat, et contemnat in infimis quod appetebat.

VERS. 16, 17.-- Juxta numerum animarum vestrarum, etc. (AUG., locutio 78.) Saepe Scriptura homines animas appellat, a parte totum: non facile autem carnes pro hominibus, sed carnem; sicut psal. LV: Non timebo quid faciat caro; quod apertius in psalmo eodem ait, quid faciat mihi homo. Non autem diceret, secundum numerum carnium vestrarum, sicut dixit, secundum numerum animarum vestrarum.

VERS. 18, 19.-- Juxta id quod edere poterant, etc. Quia in praesenti vita quilibet debet discere quantum 240Apotest, ne quid indiscussum relinquat, ne cum non possit, discere incipiat.

VERS. 20.-- Sed dimiserunt quidam ex eis usque mane, etc. Principium alterius vitae, in qua non licebit vacare doctrinae, sed manna computrescet, et vermibus scatebit, id est, poenam eis aeternam generabit.

VERS. 21.-- Cumque incaluisset sol, liquefiebat. Quia tunc omnis Scripturae intellectus patebit, quia nunc videmus per speculum in aenigmate, tunc autem videbimus facie ad faciem (I Cor. XIII).

VERS. 22.-- Cibos duplices. Necessaria scilicet animae et corpori. Nam in septima, quae mortuorum aetas intelligitur, scilicet nullus erit operandi locus.

VERS. 23-32.-- Requies sabbati, etc. Audiendo verbum Dei et faciendo, vel bene vivendo et aliis misericordiam 240Bpraestando. Quod reponebatur pro sabbato non corrumpebatur, quia bona pro futura requie facta, in futura vita manent integra. Alioquin ebulliebant vermes et computrescebat quod servabatur: qui enim propter praesentem vitam et amorem saeculi thesaurizant, illis ebullit vermis qui non morietur.

VERS. 33, 34.-- Et repone coram, etc. (AUG. quaest. 61 in Exod.) Quaeri potest ubi Aaron reponeret ante Dominum, quando nec ullum tabernaculum fuit, nec arca testamenti jam fuerat constituta. An forte de futuro, ideo dixit repone, ut intelligatur tunc ante Dominum posse reponi, quando futura arca erat? An potius ante Dominum dictum est, quod fit ipsa devotione offerendi, in quocunque loco 240Cponeretur? Ubi enim non est Deus? Sed quod adjungit: Et reposuit Aaron ante tabernaculum ad reservandum, priorem magis sensum asserit. Hoc enim modo Scriptura dixit per prolepsim, quod post factum est cum esse coepit tabernaculum testimonii.

VERS. 35, 36.-- Filii autem Israel comederunt manna quadraginta annis, etc. (AUG., ibid., quaest. 62. Alia littera: Filii autem Israel ederunt manna quadraginta annis, etc., usque ad noverat enim Deus quid desiderarent et ex quo genere carnis satiaret eos.

Comederunt manna quadraginta annis. Corpus Christi, scilicet Verbum Dei, quod percipimus nunc in aenigmate, tunc autem facie ad faciem (I Cor. 13).

Donec venirent in terram habitabilem, etc. (AUG., quaest. 63 in Exod., tom. III.) Alia littera: Quousque 240Dvenirent in partem Phoenicis. Per repetitionem exponit quam terram dixit habitari, etc., usque ad multarum enim terrarum, locorum, fluminum et urbium nomina certis causis mutavit antiquitas.

CHAPTER XVI.

VERS. 2.-- And all the congregation of Israel murmured, etc. (ORIG., homil. 7 in Exod.) O ungrateful 235Apeople! it desires Egypt, it that saw the Egyptians destroyed; and the flesh of Egypt, it that sees the flesh of the Egyptians given both to the fishes and to the birds! It murmurs against Moses, nay rather against God; but the first time pardon is granted, and the second, and perhaps the third: but if they do not cease, but persist, hear what follows, it is related in the book of Numbers (Num. XXI), and the same Apostle rehearses it, saying (I Cor. X): Neither do you murmur, as some of them murmured, and perished by the serpents. These things were written on account of us, upon whom the ends of the world are come. [Vulg. 1Cor 10:10 has et perierunt ab exterminatore, "and perished by the destroyer"; the serpents belong to v. 9, and v. 11 opens scripta sunt autem ad correptionem nostram] But if we do not cease to murmur and complain against God, we shall incur a like peril of offence, or a like example of it. For when we lay the blame upon the inclemency of the heavens, the unfruitfulness of the crops, the rarity of showers, and upon the prosperity of some and the unprosperity of others, 235Bwe murmur against God. But these things are pardoned at first, and are severely avenged upon those who persist, because serpents shall be sent upon them, that is, they shall be handed over to unclean and poisonous spirits, which by hidden bites feed upon the inner chambers of the heart.

(ORIG.) For the correction of those who read, the sin of the people is set down: that it murmured and was ungrateful for the divine benefits, when it had received the heavenly manna. In the second month, on the fifteenth day of the month, which is not written without reason. Recall the laws of the pasch which were spoken (Num. IX), and thou wilt find there that this is the time which is appointed for the keeping of a second pasch by those who have been unclean in soul, or taken up with business abroad. Those who had not been unclean in soul, or were not journeying far off, 235Ckept the pasch on the fourteenth day of the first month; but those far off on pilgrimage, and the unclean, keep the second pasch at this time, in which the manna came down from heaven. On the day on which the first pasch is kept, the manna does not come down, but on that on which the second is kept: the first pasch is that of the Jewish people; the second is ours. For we were unclean in soul, we who worshipped idols; and we were pilgrims, and far off, strangers and pilgrims from the testaments of God, etc. (Eph. II.) But manna is given from heaven, not on the day on which the first pasch is kept, but on that on which the second is: For the bread which came down from heaven (Joan. VI) did not come to those who were celebrating the first solemnity, but to us, who have received the second. For Christ our pasch is sacrificed, who as true man came down as bread from heaven (I Cor. V.) Let us see 235Dmeanwhile what is shown to have been done on this day: On the fourteenth day of the second month the people murmured, etc.

VERS. 3.-- We sat over the fleshpots, etc. They had flocks of sheep and herds of oxen, of which they could eat; but they burned with desire for the fish and the fowl in which Egypt abounds.

VERS. 4.-- Behold I will rain, etc. (ORIG., homil. 8 in Exod.) I should wish first to have a discourse upon this scripture with the Jews, to whom the oracles of God were entrusted, as to what seems to them of that which it says: For six days together you shall gather, but on the sixth day double. It appears therefore that that day is named the sixth which is set before the sabbath; but the sabbath is the seventh day. 236AI ask therefore on what day the manna began to be given? and I wish to compare our Lord's day with the sabbath of the Jews. For from Scripture it appears that on the Lord's day the manna is first given, gathered for six days together; on the seventh, which is the sabbath, there is a ceasing. Since therefore on the Lord's day God rained manna, and not on the sabbath, let the Jews understand that even then our Lord's day was preferred to their sabbath; and it was signified that on that day the grace of God did not come down from heaven to them, and that the heavenly bread, which is the word of God, did not reach them; whence Osee III: For many days the children of Israel shall sit without king, without prince, without victim, without sacrifice, without priest. [Vulg. Os 3:4 names instead sine altari, et sine ephod, et sine theraphim, "without altar, and without ephod, and without theraphim," and has no victim and no priest] But on our Lord's day the Lord always rains manna, that is, the heavenly words which are joyful to us. Those unhappy men sigh even 236Bafter manna as their fathers did, and do not deserve to receive it; for they cannot eat what is small like coriander seed, and white like hoar frost. For they perceive nothing in the word of God that is small, subtle and spiritual, but all of it fat and gross. For the heart of this people is grown gross. The interpretation also of this name sounds thus. For manna is interpreted What is this? The very force of the name invites to learning, so that when thou hearest the law of God recited, thou mayest always inquire and say to the teachers, What is this? Thou therefore, if thou wilt eat manna, and receive the word of God, know that it is small and very subtle, like coriander seed: for it has somewhat of the herb whereby it can nourish and refresh the weak. For he that is weak eateth herbs (Rom. XIV). For it has also somewhat of sharpness, and therefore is as hoar frost; 236Cbut of whiteness and sweetness very much. What is whiter, more splendid, than divine learning? what sweeter, more delightful, than the words of God, which are above honey and the honeycomb?

Loaves. The manna, namely: it is a manner of speech by which bread is put for food; and Scripture loves rather to say loaves in the plural than bread in the singular.

That I may prove. This proving is a testing, not a seducing to sin; nor are they proved for this reason, that God did not know: but he shows them to themselves, that they may become the more humble in asking help and in acknowledging the grace of God.

VERS. 5.-- But on the sixth day, etc. (ORIG., hom. 7.) On the sixth day let double be gathered into store, as much as may suffice also for the sabbath. The sixth day is this 236Dlife in which we now are, whence in six days God made this world; on this day therefore we ought to lay up as much as will suffice in the world to come. For if thou acquire here anything of good work, if thou lay up to thy credit anything of justice, of mercy and of piety, this will be food for thee in the world to come. For he who acquires here ten talents (Luc. XIX), there shall receive ten cities, and what a man shall sow, that also shall he reap (Gal. VI). The rich of this world lay up those things which are corrupted in this world, nay together with this world: but goods laid up remain until the morrow. And it is written. They who were unfaithful kept of the manna, and worms boiled forth out of it and it putrefied; but what was laid up for the day of the sabbath remained whole. And thou 237Atherefore, if for the sake of the present life and the love of the world thou lay up treasure, straightway worms boil forth, of which it is said in Isaias LXVI: Their worm dieth not; [Vulg. Is 66:24 has the future non morietur, "shall not die"; the present is the form of Mc 9:43-47] these are begotten by avarice and the blind greed of riches in those who have money, and who, seeing their brethren in need, shut up their bowels from them. Whence the Apostle (I Tim. VI) charges the rich not to be highminded, nor to trust in the uncertainty of riches, etc.; nor do worms come to be in us from any other source than from the manna, that is, the word of God. Whence John XV: If I had not come and spoken to them, etc. After the word of God has been received, therefore, if anyone sins, that very word is made a worm, which ever digs into his conscience and gnaws the secret places of his breast.

VERS. 6.--(ORIG.) In the evening you shall know, etc. Toward evening 237Byou shall know that I am the Lord; and in the morning you shall see the majesty of the Lord. Let the Jews answer me how the Lord is known toward evening, and his majesty seen in the morning? Where was he acknowledged toward evening, and his majesty seen in the morning? But this is not understood save in the Gospel (Matth. XXVIII), in which on the evening of the sabbath, which dawneth on the first day of the week, Mary Magdalen came and the other Mary to the sepulchre, and they found the stone rolled back from the monument, and an earthquake made, and the monuments opened, and the centurion and the guards of the sepulchre saying: Indeed this was the Son of God. The Lord was acknowledged in this evening, because he himself was the Lord: he was acknowledged in the power of the resurrection, and in the morning his glory was seen. When the other women had come on the first day of the week very early in the morning, they found angels 237Csitting at the sepulchre with great brightness, and saying: He is not here, but is risen from the dead. Come and see the place, etc. (Luc. XXIV.) His majesty therefore was seen in the morning, because the resurrection also was announced by angels.

VERS. 7-11.-- But what are we, etc. (AUG., quaest. 59 in Exod., tom. III.) They wished that they availed not so much as God, etc., as far as he showed the Holy Spirit to be God by adding, Acts V: Thou hast not lied to men, but to God.

VERS. 12-13.-- In the evening you shall eat flesh (ORIG., ut supra. ) Morally we can understand that for each one of us there is a morning and a beginning of the day, the time in which we are first enlightened and kindled to the light of faith. In this time we cannot eat 237Dthe flesh of the word, that is, we are not yet capable of perfect doctrine; but after long exercises, after very much progress, when we are already near to evening, that is, to the faith of perfection, then we are made capable of more solid food. Let us hasten therefore to receive the manna, which yields in the mouth what savour each one wills. Whence Matthew IX: Be it done to thee according to thy faith. [Vulg. Mt 9:29 has the plural secundum fidem vestram fiat vobis, "according to your faith be it done unto you"] And thou therefore, if thou receive with all faith and devotion the word of God which is preached in the Church, be it done to thee whatsoever thou desirest. If thou art in tribulation, it consoles thee, saying: A contrite and humbled heart God spurneth not (Psal. L) [Vulg. Ps 50:19 has non despicies, "thou wilt not despise," in the second person addressed to God]; if thou rejoicest for the hope to come, it heaps up joys for thee, saying: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye just (Psal. XXXI); if thou art wrathful, it says: Cease from anger, and leave 238Aindignation (Psal. XXXVI) [Vulg. Ps 36:8 has derelinque furorem, "leave wrath"]; if thou art in griefs, it heals thee, saying: The Lord healeth all thy diseases (Matth. IV); if thou art consumed by poverty, it says in consolation: The Lord lifteth the needy from the earth, and raiseth up the poor out of the dunghill (Psal. CXII) [Vulg. Ps 112:7 has the participles suscitans a terra inopem, et de stercore erigens pauperem, "raising up the needy from the earth, and lifting the poor out of the dunghill"]. Yet if anyone receives it unfaithfully, and does not eat it, but hides it, worms boil forth out of it. Nor be thou troubled if the word of God is brought to this, that it is thought to be made a worm; for he himself says: I am a worm, and no man (Psal. XXI). For as he himself is the one who becomes to some a ruin, but to others a resurrection (Luc. II); so also is he the one who in the manna is made to the faithful the sweetness of honey, to the unfaithful a worm. For he is the word of God which convicts the minds of the wicked and pierces the conscience with the darts of correction. For he is the one who is made fire to those to whom he opens the Scriptures 238B, who say: Was not our heart burning within us? etc. (Luc. XXIV.) To others he is fire, in whom he burns up the thorns of the evil earth, that is, consumes evil thoughts in the heart; and therefore for sinners neither does the convicting worm ever die, nor is the burning fire quenched: but for the just and the faithful he remains sweet and pleasant. Whence it is said in psalm XXXIII: Taste and see, etc.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) Another reading: In the evening you shall eat flesh, and in the morning you shall be filled with loaves. Let the Jews say what consequence there is, that in the evening they should eat flesh without loaves, or in the morning loaves without meat? what of the divine gift, what of the tempering of heavenly grace, is shown in this? Is this that wherein they can acknowledge God, who eat flesh in the evening without loaves, 238Cand do they say that the majesty of God appears, if again loaves are eaten without flesh? let them keep such things to themselves, for those who, acquiescing in you, think that God is acknowledged in quails; but we, for whom at the end, toward the evening of the world, the Word was made flesh, say that God is acknowledged in that flesh only which he took from the Virgin: for this flesh of the Word of God no one ate either in the morning or at noon, but toward evening: for the coming of the Lord in the flesh was made toward evening, as John also says: Children, it is the last hour (I Joan. II) [Vulg. 1Jo 2:18 has Filioli, "Little children"] .-- In the morning, he says, you shall be filled with loaves. To us therefore the bread is the Word of God: He is the living bread which came down from heaven, and giveth life to the world (Joan. VI). But as to its saying that this bread is given in the morning, whereas he was incarnate toward evening, it is so to be understood 238Dthat the Lord came toward the evening of the world and near its end; but by his coming, since he is the sun of justice, he restored a new morning to believers; a new light, namely, of knowledge, in which they are filled with loaves who have received his precepts. Nor wonder that the word is called both the flesh of God, and bread, and is called milk and herbs, and is named diversely according to the measure of believers and the capacity of those who take it. Or, after the morning made by the resurrection, he filled believers with loaves, because he granted the books of the law and the prophets, before unknown, for the instruction of the Church, and he himself is made bread in the Gospel, while the other books of the law and the prophets or of the histories may be called many loaves, with which believers from among the Gentiles are filled. Whence: They shall go up into the mountain, and 239Ashall drink wine, they shall be anointed with ointment. Fittingly therefore do we receive flesh toward evening and are filled with loaves in the morning: because neither could we eat flesh in the morning, for the time was not yet; nor at noon, for scarcely do the angels eat flesh at noon, and to that order perhaps the noonday time is granted.

And in the morning. On the ninth day, when Christ, the sun of justice, by his coming restored to believers the things that were lost.

(ISID.) By the food of birds is figured the preaching divinely sent, which runs through sounding words, as winged things through the air, on which they are fed by faith who set out for the heavenly fatherland. Or the utterances of the old law, which fed the carnal people as with flesh through words divinely sent, as through flying things. Whence also it is given in the evening, because 239Ball things granted to them carnally were to have an end. But in the manifest light of faith the manna is given, which is Christ, namely the living bread which came down from heaven (Joan. VI), who is rained through the evangelical clouds upon the whole world: not now upon the murmuring and tempted Synagogue, but upon the believing and hoping Church.

VERS. 14.-- In the likeness of hoar frost upon the earth. The hoar frost signifies Christ: for the manna was first given on the Lord's day, and Christ rose again on the Lord's day.

Pounded. That is, most clean. For as wheat, or millet, or some other such thing is bruised in a little mortar with a pestle and stripped of its husks, so the body of Christ was stripped of mortality through the tribulation of the passion and the pestle of the cross. Or the small manna is the subtlety of heavenly 239Cdoctrine, which is pounded with the pestle that the literal sense may be stripped away.

VERS. 15.-- Man hu. (GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 7.) What is designated by the manna given from above to the children of Israel, save the wonder of the heavenly sweetness? For sweet is the food which is received from on high; it is called man hu, which is interpreted What is this? which we say when we behold what we wonder at not knowing. The soul therefore perceives the heavenly manna, when, lifted up by the voice of compunction, it is astonished at the new appearance of the eternal refreshment, so that, filled with the divine sweetness, it may rightly answer What is this? because while it is held suspended away from the lowest thought, it beholds those things which are from on high and wonders at them as at things unwonted. And when by this voice the deafness of our torpor is broken, the practice of the old life is 239Dchanged, so that the soul, breathed upon by the heavenly Spirit, both desires in the highest things what it had despised, and despises in the lowest things what it had desired.

VERS. 16, 17.-- According to the number of your souls, etc. (AUG., locutio 78.) Often Scripture calls men souls, the whole from a part: but not readily fleshes for men, but flesh; as in psalm LV: I will not fear what flesh can do; which it says more openly in the same psalm, what man can do to me. [Vulg. Ps 55:5 has non timebo quid faciat mihi caro, "what flesh can do to me," keeping the mihi which the second quotation retains] But it would not say, according to the number of your fleshes, as it said, according to the number of your souls.

VERS. 18, 19.-- According to what they were able to eat, etc. Because in the present life everyone ought to learn as much as 240Ahe can, lest he leave anything unexamined, lest he begin to learn when he cannot.

VERS. 20.-- But some of them left of it until the morning, etc. The beginning of the other life, in which it will not be permitted to give leisure to doctrine, but the manna will putrefy, and swarm with worms, that is, it will beget for them eternal punishment.

VERS. 21.-- And when the sun grew hot, it melted. Because then all understanding of Scripture will lie open, since now we see through a glass in a dark manner, but then we shall see face to face (I Cor. XIII).

VERS. 22.-- Double food. The things necessary, namely, for soul and for body. For in the seventh, which is understood as the age of the dead, there will, namely, be no place for working.

VERS. 23-32.-- The rest of the sabbath, etc. By hearing the word of God and doing it, or by living well and showing mercy 240Bto others. What was laid up for the sabbath was not corrupted, because good things done for the rest to come remain whole in the life to come. Otherwise worms boiled forth and what was kept putrefied: for those who lay up treasure for the sake of the present life and the love of the world, for them boils forth the worm that shall not die.

VERS. 33, 34.-- And lay it up before, etc. (AUG. quaest. 61 in Exod.) It can be asked where Aaron was to lay it up before the Lord, when there was neither any tabernacle, nor had the ark of the testament yet been set up. Or was it perhaps of the future, and did he therefore say lay up, that it might be understood that it could then be laid up before the Lord, when the ark was to be? Or rather was it said 'before the Lord' of that which is done by the very devotion of him who offers, in whatsoever place 240Cit were placed? For where is God not? But what he adds: And Aaron laid it up before the tabernacle to be kept, rather asserts the former sense. For in this manner Scripture spoke by prolepsis of what was done afterwards, when the tabernacle of the testimony began to be.

VERS. 35, 36.-- And the children of Israel ate manna forty years, etc. (AUG., ibid., quaest. 62. Another reading: And the children of Israel did eat manna forty years, etc., as far as for God knew what they desired and from what kind of flesh he might satisfy them.

They ate manna forty years. The body of Christ, namely the Word of God, which we now perceive in a dark manner, but then face to face (I Cor. 13).

Until they came into a habitable land, etc. (AUG., quaest. 63 in Exod., tom. III.) Another reading: Until 240Dthey came into the region of Phoenice. By repetition he sets forth what land he said was inhabited, etc., as far as for antiquity changed the names of many lands, places, rivers and cities for certain causes.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- Igitur profecta omnis multitudo filiorum Israel, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11 in Exod.) Omnes qui volunt pie vivere in Christo persecutionem patiuntur, ab hostibus impugnantur: agensque iter hujus vitae semper debet esse paratus et stare in castris. Unde profecta est omnis synagoga filiorum Israel de deserto Sin secundum castra sua per verbum Dei. Est una quidem Synagoga Domini. sed in quatuor castris dividitur, qui describuntur in Numeris 241Acirca tabernaculum Domini posita. Armatus ergo in castris Domini militans ne implices te saecularibus negotiis, et in castris Domini semper excubias agas, ut de te dicatur quia profectus es per verbum Domini de deserto Sin, et venis in Raphidim. Sin autem tentatio interpretatur; Raphidim sanitas judicii: qui bene exit de tentatione, venit ad sanitatem judicii. In die enim judicii sanus erit. Unde Apoc. II: Qui vicerit, dabo ei de ligno vitae quod est in paradiso Dei mei. Venit ergo ad sanitatem judicii qui bene disponit sermones suos in judicio.

Sin. Hic specialiter Sin una mansio accipitur ex his quinque quae fuerunt a mari Rubro usque ad montem Sina, quas Josephus nominatim describit. Generaliter enim omne desertum illud Sin vocatur 241Bsicut et Raphidim.

Castrametati sunt in Raphidim. (HIERON. de Loc. Hebr., tom. III.) Est locus in deserto juxta montem Horeb. Sciendum est autem quod omnis eremus usque ad montem Sinai Sin vocatur, et ex tota provincia etiam locus unius mansionis nomen accepit, sicut et Moab tam urbis quam provinciae nomen est. In hac solitudine quinque sunt mansiones, Zuph, et eremus Sin, Alus, Depheca, Raphidim. Unde bene dicitur quia profecta est omnis multitudo filiorum Israel de eremo Sin per mansiones suas juxta os Domini, et venerunt in Raphidim. Unde patet plures mansiones unius regionis vocabulo demonstrari.

VERS. 3, 4.-- Sitivit ergo ibi populus, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra ). Alia littera: Sitivit, inquit, populus aquam, 241Cet murmurabat adversus Moysen. Suffecit dicere quia sitivit, quid opus fuit addere aquam? Non est superflua adjectio. Sunt ergo diversae sites, et quisque habet propriam. Qui boni sunt, esuriunt et sitiunt justitiam (Matth. V). Alii quoque dicunt: Sitivit anima mea ad te, Deus (Psal. XLI). Qui vero sunt peccatores patiuntur non sitim aquae, non famem panis, sed audiendi verbum Dei. Sitivit ergo populus aquam, qui debuit sitire justitiam. Sed Deus eruditor infantium, magister insipientium, corrigit culpas et emendat errores, dicens ad Moysen ut sumat virgam, et percutiat petram, et educat aquas. Vult enim eos jam de petra bibere et proficere, et ad interiora mysteria venire. Murmuraverunt enim adversus Moysen, ideo jubet Dominus ut ostendat eis petram 241Dde qua bibant. Si quis enim est qui legens Moysen, murmurat adversus eum, et displicet ei lex secundum litteram, quia in multis non videtur habere consequentiam, ostendit ei Moyses petram, quae est Christus, ut inde bibat et sitim reficiat. Sed haec petra non dat aquas nisi percussa, percussa vero fontes producit: percussus enim Christus, et in cruce mactatus, Novi Testamenti fontes produxit. Unde Zach. VIII: Percutiam pastorem, et dispergentur oves gregis. Nisi enim fuisset percussus, et exisset sanguis et aqua de latere ejus, omnes sitim verbi Dei pateremur. Unde: Bibebant autem de spiritali, consequente eos petra: petra autem erat Christus (I Cor. X).

(ISID.) Sitiens populus pro aqua murmuravit contra 242AMoysen: ideo jubet Deus ut ostendat eis petram ex qua bibant. Si quis autem legens Moysen murmurat adversus eum, et displicet ei littera legis, ostendit ei Moyses petram, quae est Christus, et ducit ad ipsam, unde bibat et sitim reficiat.

VERS. 5.-- Antecede populum, etc. Non solum Moyses ducit populum ad aquas, sed et seniores populi. Non enim sola lex Christum annuntiat, sed et prophetae et patriarchae et omnes majores natu.

Et virgam, etc. (AUG., quaest. 64 in Exod.) Aaron, non Moyses, legitur flumen virga percussisse. Moyses enim eadem virga mare divisit, non flumen. Quid ergo sibi vult: Accipe virgam in qua percussisti flumen? An forte mare appellavit flumen? et quaerendum est exemplum hujus locutionis? An quod Aaron 242Bfecit, Moysi potius attributum est, quia per Moysen jubebat Deus quae faceret Aaron, et in hoc auctoritas, in illo ministerium? Unde: Erit tibi ad populum, tu autem illi in his quae ad Deum.

VERS. 6, 7.-- Percutiesque petram. Christum, qui in cruce a Judaeis percussus est: cui accessit virga, id est, lignum passionis, ut fidelibus remaneret aqua.

VERS. 8.-- Venit autem Amalec, etc. (STRAB.) Amalec fuit filius Ismael, a quo Amalecitae, qui et Ismaelitae sunt: ipsi sunt Saraceni. Interpretatur autem bibens vel lambens sanguinem, et significat diabolum, qui sanctos de Aegypto exeuntes, et ad terram promissionis tendentes, semper impugnat, et animarum sanguinem bibere desiderat.

242C(ORIG., hom. II in Exod.) Describitur bellum cum Amalecitis factum. Antequam manducaret panem de coelo, et biberet aquam de petra, non dicitur pugnasse populus; sed dicitur ad eum: Dominus pugnabit pro vobis, et vos tacebitis. Est ergo quando Dominus pugnat pro nobis, nec permittit tentari supra id quod possumus, nec impares viribus sinit ad fortem venire congressum. Job quoque certamen tentationis suae jam perfectus implevit; et tu ergo cum coeperis manducare manna, panem coelestem verbi Dei, et bibere aquam de petra, cumque ad interiora doctrinae spiritualis accesseris, exspecta pugnam, praepara te ad bellum.

VERS. 9, 10.-- Dixitque Moyses ad Josue, etc. (ORIG., ibid.) Alia littera: Dixit Moyses ad Jesum: 242DElige tibi viros, etc. Hucusque beati nominis Jesu nunquam facta est mentio. Hic primum vocavit Moyses Jesum, et dixit ei: Elige viros tibi ipsi. Moyses vocat Jesum, lex invocat Christum, ut eligat sibi viros potentes de plebe: non poterat Moyses eligere, sed solus Jesus, qui ait Joan. XV: Non vos me elegistis, sed ego vos elegi (Matth. XII). Ipse est electorum dux et potentium princeps. Ipse confligit cum Amalec. Ipse enim intrat in domum fortis, et alligat fortem, et vasa ejus diripit.

Ego stabo in vertice collis. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Ascendit, inquit, Moyses non in verticem montis, sed in verticem collis. Servabatur enim ei tunc ascendere in verticem montis cum ascensurus erat 243AJesus, et cum ipso Moyses et Elias, et ibi transformandus in gloria. Nunc ergo velut nondum glorificatus de transformatione Jesu, non ascendit in verticem montis, sed in verticem collis. Et cum elevaret Moyses manus, superabat Israel. Moyses elevat manus, non extendit; Jesus, qui totum mundum, exaltatus in cruce, complexus est, dicit: Extendi manus ad populum non credentem (Isa. LXV). Elevare manus, hoc est actus levare ad Deum, ut non habeat actus dejectos et humi jacentes, sed Deo placitos et ad coelum erectos. Elevat ergo manus qui thesaurizat in coelo. Ubi enim thesaurus, ibi oculus, ibi manus. Elevat manus qui dicit: Elevatio manuum mearum sacrificium vespertinum (Psal. CXL). Si ergo eleventur actus nostri, vincitur Amalec: unde Isa. 243BXXXV: Remissas manus et genua debilia erigite. Si servat populus legem, elevat Moyses manus, et adversarius vincitur. Si non servat legem, invalescit Amalec. Quia igitur nobis pugna est adversus principes et potestates mundi hujus rectores tenebrarum, si vis vincere, eleva manus, id est, actus, conversatio tua non sit in terris, sed in coelis. Eleva et tu manus in coelum ad Deum, imple mandatum Apostoli: Sine intermissione orate (I Thess. V); et tunc fiet quod scriptum est, quia sicut ablingit vitulus in campis herbam virentem, ita ablinget populus hic populum qui super terram est; ubi inducitur quia populus Dei non tam manum et armis quam voce et lingua pugnabat, et oratione inimicos prosternebat. Eleva ergo actus, insta orationi: haec Christiani pugna 243Cest, quae superat inimicum. Puto autem per hanc figuram duos populos significari, quorum unus ex gentibus elevat manus Moysi, et in sublime tollit ea quae scripsit et intelligentiam eorum, ut sic vincat; alius vero, id est, Judaicus populus, non elevat manus Moysi a terra, nec altum aliquid nec sublime in eo intelligit, et sic ab adversariis vincitur.

Habens virgam Dei, etc. (AUG., quaest. 65.) Nunc virga Dei dicitur, quae primum dicta est Aaron, postea dicitur Moysi. Sicut dicitur spiritus Eliae qui est Spiritus Dei, cujus particeps Elias dicitur; et Dei justitia, quae nostra est. Unde: ignorantes Dei justitiam, volentes suam statuere (Rom. X), id est, 243Dtanquam a se sibi praeparatam.

VERS. 11.-- Cumque levaret Moyses manus, etc. (STRAB.) Quia cum lex mystice intelligitur, passio Christi spiritualiter pensatur, diabolus vincitur et fugatur. Cum autem remittuntur manus, id est, praecepta legis minus studiose exercentur, hostis victor insequitur.

VERS. 12, 13.-- Sumentes igitur lapidem, etc. (GREG., homil. 33 in Evang.) Moyses sedit in lapide, etc., usque ad quos etiam juste illa damnat, ipse misericorditer liberat.

VERS. 14.-- Delebo enim, etc. (RAB.) In hoc significat quod omnipotens Deus per legem (quam significat Moyses, et de Christo pronuntiat) deleat 244Amemoriam spiritualium hostium. Unde psal. XIX: Periit memoria eorum cum sonitu.

VERS. 15, 16.-- Aedificavitque Moyses altare, etc. Omnes diaboli victores in cordibus suis debent aras fidei aedificare, in qua spirituales hostias offerant.

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- All the multitude of the children of Israel therefore setting forward, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11 in Exod.) All who wish to live piously in Christ suffer persecution, and are assailed by enemies: and he who walks the road of this life ought always to be ready and to stand in the camp. Whence all the synagogue of the children of Israel set forward from the desert of Sin according to their camps by the word of God. There is indeed one Synagogue of the Lord. but it is divided into four camps, which are described in the book of Numbers as placed 241Around about the tabernacle of the Lord. Do thou therefore, armed and serving in the camp of the Lord, not entangle thyself in secular business, and keep watch always in the camp of the Lord, that it may be said of thee that thou hast set forward by the word of the Lord from the desert of Sin, and comest into Raphidim. Now Sin is interpreted temptation; Raphidim, soundness of judgment: he who comes well out of temptation comes to soundness of judgment. For in the day of judgment he will be sound. Whence Apocalypse II: To him that overcometh, I will give of the tree of life, which is in the paradise of my God. He therefore comes to soundness of judgment who orders his words well in judgment.

Sin. Here Sin is taken specially for one station out of those five which were from the Red Sea as far as mount Sina, which Josephus describes by name. For in general that whole desert is called Sin, 241Bas also Raphidim is.

They encamped in Raphidim. (HIERON. de Loc. Hebr., tom. III.) It is a place in the desert beside mount Horeb. And it is to be known that the whole wilderness as far as mount Sinai is called Sin, and that from the whole province the place of a single station also took its name, as Moab is the name both of a city and of a province. In this solitude there are five stations, Zuph, and the desert of Sin, Alus, Depheca, Raphidim. Whence it is well said that all the multitude of the children of Israel set forward from the desert of Sin by their stations according to the word of the Lord, and they came into Raphidim. Whence it is plain that many stations are denoted by the name of one region.

VERS. 3, 4.-- The people therefore thirsted there, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra ). Another reading: The people, he says, thirsted for water, 241Cand murmured against Moses. It was enough to say that he thirsted, what need was there to add water? The addition is not superfluous. There are therefore diverse thirsts, and each man has his own. They who are good hunger and thirst after justice (Matth. V). Others too say: My soul hath thirsted after thee, O God (Psal. XLI) [Vulg. Ps 41:3 has sitivit anima mea ad Deum fortem vivum, "my soul hath thirsted after the strong living God"]. But they who are sinners suffer not the thirst of water, not the hunger of bread, but of hearing the word of God. The people therefore thirsted for water, which ought to have thirsted for justice. But God, the instructor of the simple, the master of the unwise, corrects faults and amends errors, saying to Moses that he take the rod, and strike the rock, and bring forth waters. For he wills them now to drink of the rock and to make progress, and to come to the inner mysteries. For they murmured against Moses, and therefore the Lord commands that he show them the rock 241Dwhereof they may drink. For if there is anyone who, reading Moses, murmurs against him, and the law according to the letter displeases him, because in many things it seems not to hold together, Moses shows him the rock, which is Christ, that from it he may drink and repair his thirst. But this rock does not give waters unless it is struck, and when struck it brings forth fountains: for Christ, being struck and slain upon the cross, brought forth the fountains of the New Testament. Whence Zacharias VIII: I will strike the shepherd, and the sheep of the flock shall be scattered. [Vulg. Zach 13:7 has the imperative Percute pastorem, et dispergentur oves, "Strike the shepherd, and the sheep shall be scattered"; the words printed here are those of Mt 26:31] For unless he had been struck, and blood and water had gone forth from his side, we should all suffer thirst for the word of God. Whence: And they drank of the spiritual rock that followed them: and the rock was Christ (I Cor. X).

(ISID.) The people, thirsting for water, murmured against 242AMoses: therefore God commands that he show them the rock whereof they may drink. But if anyone, reading Moses, murmurs against him, and the letter of the law displeases him, Moses shows him the rock, which is Christ, and leads him to it, that from it he may drink and repair his thirst.

VERS. 5.-- Go before the people, etc. Not Moses alone leads the people to the waters, but also the elders of the people. For not the law alone announces Christ, but also the prophets and the patriarchs and all the elders by birth.

And the rod, etc. (AUG., quaest. 64 in Exod.) It is read that Aaron, not Moses, struck the river with the rod. For Moses divided the sea with that same rod, not the river. What then does this mean: Take the rod wherewith thou didst strike the river? Or did he perhaps call the sea a river? and is an example of this manner of speech to be sought? Or was what Aaron 242Bdid rather attributed to Moses, because through Moses God commanded the things which Aaron did, and in the one was the authority, in the other the ministry? Whence: He shall be to thee for the people, but thou to him in those things which are unto God.

VERS. 6, 7.-- And thou shalt strike the rock. Christ, who was struck upon the cross by the Jews: to whom the rod came near, that is, the wood of the passion, that water might remain for the faithful.

VERS. 8.-- But Amalec came, etc. (STRAB.) Amalec was the son of Ismael, from whom are the Amalecites, who are also Ismaelites: these are the Saracens. Now it is interpreted drinking or licking up blood, and it signifies the devil, who ever assails the saints going out of Egypt and making toward the land of promise, and desires to drink the blood of souls.

242C(ORIG., hom. II in Exod.) The war made with the Amalecites is described. Before the people ate bread from heaven, and drank water from the rock, it is not said to have fought; but it is said to it: The Lord will fight for you, and you shall hold your peace. There is therefore a time when the Lord fights for us, and does not permit us to be tempted above what we can bear, nor suffer those unequal in strength to come to a stout encounter. Job also, being already perfect, fulfilled the contest of his temptation; and thou therefore, when thou hast begun to eat manna, the heavenly bread of the word of God, and to drink water from the rock, and when thou hast drawn near to the inner things of spiritual doctrine, look for the fight, prepare thyself for war.

VERS. 9, 10.-- And Moses said to Josue, etc. (ORIG., ibid.) Another reading: Moses said to Jesus: 242DChoose out men for thee, etc. Up to this point mention was never made of the blessed name of Jesus. Here first Moses called Jesus, and said to him: Choose men for thine own self. Moses calls Jesus, the law invokes Christ, that he may choose for himself mighty men from the people: Moses could not choose, but Jesus alone, who says in John XV: You have not chosen me, but I have chosen you (Matth. XII). He is the leader of the elect and the prince of the mighty. He contends with Amalec. For he himself enters into the house of the strong man, and binds the strong man, and plunders his vessels.

I will stand on the top of the hill. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Moses, he says, went up not to the top of the mountain, but to the top of the hill. For it was kept for him to go up then to the top of the mountain when Jesus was to go up, 243Aand with him Moses and Elias, and there to be transfigured in glory. Now therefore, as not yet glorified by the transfiguration of Jesus, he does not go up to the top of the mountain, but to the top of the hill. And when Moses lifted up his hands, Israel prevailed. Moses lifts up his hands, he does not stretch them out; Jesus, who embraced the whole world, being lifted up upon the cross, says: I have stretched out my hands to a people that believeth not (Isa. LXV). [Vulg. Is 65:2 has expandi manus meas tota die ad populum incredulum, "I have spread forth my hands all the day to an unbelieving people"; Rom 10:21, whose wording is nearest, has expandi manus meas] To lift up the hands, this is to raise one's acts to God, that a man may not have his acts cast down and lying on the ground, but pleasing to God and raised toward heaven. He therefore lifts up his hands who lays up treasure in heaven. For where the treasure is, there is the eye, there the hand. He lifts up his hands who says: The lifting up of my hands an evening sacrifice (Psal. CXL). If therefore our acts be lifted up, Amalec is overcome: whence Isaias 243BXXXV: Lift up the hands which hang down and the feeble knees. [Vulg. Is 35:3 has confortate manus dissolutas, et genua debilia roborate, "strengthen ye the feeble hands, and confirm the weak knees"; the wording printed here is nearest Hbr 12:12] If the people keeps the law, Moses lifts up his hands, and the adversary is overcome. If it does not keep the law, Amalec grows strong. Since therefore for us the fight is against the princes and powers, the rulers of the darkness of this world, if thou wilt conquer, lift up thy hands, that is, thy acts; let thy conversation be not on earth, but in heaven. Do thou too lift up thy hands to heaven unto God, fulfil the commandment of the Apostle: Pray without ceasing (I Thess. V); and then shall come to pass what is written, that as the calf licks up the green grass in the fields, so shall this people lick up the people that is upon the earth; wherein it is brought in that the people of God fought not so much with hand and with arms as with voice and tongue, and laid its enemies low by prayer. Lift up thy acts therefore, be instant in prayer: this is the Christian's 243Cfight, which overcomes the enemy. And I think that by this figure two peoples are signified, of whom one, from the Gentiles, lifts up the hands of Moses, and raises on high the things which he wrote and the understanding of them, that so it may conquer; but the other, that is, the Jewish people, does not lift up the hands of Moses from the earth, nor understands anything high or lofty in him, and so is overcome by its adversaries.

Having the rod of God, etc. (AUG., quaest. 65.) Now it is called the rod of God, which first was called Aaron's, afterwards is called Moses'. As the spirit of Elias is spoken of, which is the Spirit of God, of which Elias is said to be partaker; and the justice of God, which is ours. Whence: not knowing the justice of God, and wishing to establish their own (Rom. X), that is, 243Das though prepared by themselves for themselves.

VERS. 11.-- And when Moses lifted up his hands, etc. (STRAB.) Because when the law is understood mystically, and the passion of Christ is spiritually weighed, the devil is overcome and put to flight. But when the hands are let down, that is, when the precepts of the law are less zealously practised, the enemy follows after as victor.

VERS. 12, 13.-- Taking therefore a stone, etc. (GREG., homil. 33 in Evang.) Moses sat upon a stone, etc., as far as those whom she justly condemns, he himself mercifully sets free.

VERS. 14.-- For I will blot out, etc. (RAB.) In this he signifies that almighty God, through the law (which Moses signifies, and which pronounces concerning Christ), blots out 244Athe memory of the spiritual enemies. Whence psalm XIX: Their memory hath perished with a noise.

VERS. 15, 16.-- And Moses built an altar, etc. All who conquer the devil ought to build in their hearts altars of faith, on which they may offer spiritual victims.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 1-4.-- Cumque audisset Jethro, etc. Quod Moyses liberaturus filios Israel dimisit uxorem et filios, quos postea in deserto recepit, significat quia praedicatores cum mittuntur ad opus praedicationis, debent se a sollicitudine temporali exonustare: cum vero postea ad episcopatum vel ad aliam dignitatem assumuntur, tunc pro sibi subjectis sollicitudinem 244Btam in spiritualibus quam temporalibus gerere debent.

VERS. 5-11.-- Venit ergo Jethro, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11 in Exod.) Alia littera: Post haec venit ad montem Dei Moyses, et Jethro sibi occurrenti exiit obviam de castris, nec duxit eum ad montem Dei, sed ad tabernaculum suum. Non enim poterat sacerdos Madian ascendere in montem Dei, sed nec in Aegyptum descendere, nec uxor Moysi, sed nunc ad eum venit cum filiis. Non enim poterat descendere in Aegyptum, et subire agones Aegyptiorum, nisi athleta probabilis, et qui in agone contendens ab omnibus se abstinet. Moyses, quia magnus et potens athleta, descendit in Aegyptum ad agones et exercitia virtutum; Abraham et Jacob quoque, qui 244Cipso nomine athleta est, cum descendisset in Aegyptum, factus est sicut stellae coeli in multitudine. Aliis in contrarium cedit descendisse in Aegyptum. Scio Hieroboam fugientem a Salomone descendisse in Aegyptum, et scidisse et corrupisse populum Dei; quia descendens in Aegyptum accepit a Susachim rege uxorem Tethimenem uxoris ejus sororem.

VERS. 12-15.-- Obtulit ergo Jethro, etc. (AUG. in Locutionibus, locut. 85.) Et sumpsit Jethro socer Moysi holocausta, etc. Sumpsit pro obtulit, posuit: an forte sensus est, non locutio, ut ea sumpsisse intelligatur obtulisse; quamvis Moyses non legatur sacrificasse, nec Aaron, nec quisquam Hebraeorum qui educti sunt de terra Aegypti: sed tamen superius dictum est quod altare constituit Moyses. Jethro autem 244Dlegimus sacerdotem fuisse Madian, id est gentis Madian. Mirum est autem si ad ejus adventum coepit sacrificare Moyses, et non potius ipse qui sacerdos erat.

Veneruntque, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Alia littera: Venerunt, inquit, presbyteri Aaron, et omnis Israel manducare panem cum socero Moysi in conspectu Dei. Non omnes manducant panem in conspectu Dei, sed qui presbyteri sunt, qui seniores, id est, qui perfecti sunt, in meritis probati, qui sive manducant, sive bibunt, ad gloriam Dei faciunt. Omne ergo quod faciunt sancti, in conspectu Dei faciunt: peccator a conspectu Dei fugit. Adam postquam peccavit, fugit a conspectu Domini, et interrogatus respondit: 245AAudivi, Domine, vocem tuam, et abscondi me, quia eram nudus (Gen. III). Cain exivit a facie Domini, quia indignus erat conspectu Domini. Propterea qui pleniorem scientiam Dei accipiunt, si malum faciunt, coram Deo faciunt. Unde psal. I: Tibi soli peccavi, etc. Qui malum coram Deo facit, continuo poenitet, et dicit, Peccavi. Qui discedit a conspectu Dei, nescit converti et peccatum poenitendo purgare. Hoc ergo interest inter malum coram Deo facere, et a conspectu Dei discedere.

(ORIG.) Non frustra venit Jethro ad Moysen, nec frustra manducavit panem cum senioribus populi in conspectu Dei; dat Moysi consilium probabile et utile, ut eligat viros, et constituat principes populi Deum colentes, potentes et odientes superbiam. Tales enim 245Boportet esse qui superbi non sint, et superbiam oderint; id est, ut non solum ipsi absque vitio sint, sed in malis oderint vitia: non homines dico, sed vitia.

Coram Deo, etc. (AUG., quaest. 66.) Quaeritur ubi, quandoquidem nec tabernaculum fuit, nec arca testamenti, quae postea sunt instituta? neque hoc de futuro dictum accipere possumus, sicut dictum est de manna, quod positum est in vase, scilicet aureo. Ergo id ante Deum debemus accipere factum, quod in honorem Domini factum est.

VERS. 16-18.-- Ostendam praecepta, etc. (ID., quaest. 67.) Quaeri potest quomodo hic Moyses dixit, etc., usque ad juste judicare non posset.

VERS. 19, 20.-- Sed audi verba mea, etc. (ID., 245Cquaest. 68.) Prima quaestio est cur Deus famulum suum, etc., usque ad quo fit tanto plenior quanto in superna et aeterna liberius extenditur.

Esto tu populo, etc. (ID., quaest. 69.) Quod vero adjungit, etc., usque ad sed unde hoc probari possit ignoro.

VERS. 21-23.-- Provide autem, etc. Ad custodiam humilitatis ab alienigena instruitur, ne, qui tot arcana Dei ab ipso Deo discebat, extolleretur, et putaret se humano consilio non egere. Praefigurabatur etiam quod doctrina gentium Judaeorum scientiam et superbiam superaret; et quae in lege deerant, fideles ex gentibus complenda suggererent, et spiritualem intellectum ad legem Dei afferrent. Sciebat quoque Moyses quia hunc populum lex audiret, et 245Dfaceret quaecunque diceret, legis quoque diminutio suggerente Evangelio fieret.

Et constitue, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11.) Ordinabis, inquit, eos tribunos et centuriones, et indicabunt populum omni hora, verumtamen, quod gravius fuerit referent ad te. Audient principes populi et presbyteri plebis, qui debent omni hora populum judicare, semper et sine intermissione sedere in judicio, dirimere lites, et reconciliare dissidentes, in gratiam revocare discordes. Discat unusquisque ex Scripturis sanctis officium suum. Moyses, inquit, ut sit in his quae ad Deum sunt, et verbum Dei edisserat populis. Caeteri vero principes, quos appellavit tribunos, quia tribui praesunt, vel centuriones, vel quinquagenarii 246Aqui praesunt minoribus judiciis, quae ad singulos pertinent dirimentes. Quam figuram etiam in futuro saeculo servandam puto. Unde Matth. XIX: Cum sederit filius hominis super thronum majestatis suae, sedebitis et vos super sedes duodecim, etc. Vides ergo quia non solum Dominus judicat, cui omne judicium dedit Pater (Joan. V), sed constituit principes qui judicent de minoribus causis; verbum autem quod gravius fuerit, referant ad ipsum. Ideo dicit Dominus de quodam, Matth. V: Quia reus erit concilii; de alio autem, Matth. XII: Reus erit gehennae ignis. Sed et de omni verbo otioso dicuntur reddere rationem. Et ideo non dixit quia Deo praestabimus rationem, sicut de perjurio. Reddes autem Domino juramenta tua. Matth. XII: Regina quoque austri 246Bsurget in judicio cum viris generationis hujus, et condemnabit eam. Ecce aliud genus judicii, Matth. XIII: Qui habet aures audiendi, audiat: haec enim omnia umbra sunt coelestium et imago futurorum.

(IDEM.) Sicut scriptum est Eccle. I: Non satiabitur oculus visu, nec auris auditu, nec nos satiari possumus considerando quae scripta sunt, quibus nos aedificet, quantis nos modis instruat. Cum enim conspicio quod Moyses propheta Deo plenus, cui Deus facie ad faciem loquebatur (Exod. XXXIII), consilium accepit a Jethro socero sacerdote Madian, admiratione nimia obstupesco. Audivit enim Moyses vocem soceri sui, et fecit quaecunque dixit ei. Non dixit, Quia Deus mihi loquitur quid agere debeam, quomodo ab homine consilium accipiam, et ab homine 246Cgentili, alieno a populo Dei? et non quis dicat, sed quid dicatur auscultat. Nos ergo si invenimus aliquid sapienter a gentilibus dictum, non cum auctoris nomine spernamus et dicta; nec quia legem Dei tenemus, convenit nobis tumere superbia et spernere prudentium verba; sed omnia probantes, quod bonum est teneamus. Qui autem hodie populis praesunt, non dico si jam eis aliqua a Deo revelata sunt: sed si in scientia legis aliquid meriti habent, consilium dedignantur inferioris, etiam sacerdotis, nedum laici, nedum gentilis: sed Moyses, qui erat mansuetus super omnes homines (Num. XII), accepti consilium inferioris, ut principibus daret formam humilitatis, et signaret imaginem futuri sacramenti. Sciebat quandoque futurum ut gentiles bonum 246Dconsilium proferrent, apud Moysen bonum et spiritualem intellectum afferentes ad legem, et audiret eos lex faciens omnia quae dicerent. Non enim potest facere lex sicut Judaei dicunt, quia infirmatur lex in carne, id est in littera, et nihil potest secundum litteram. Nihil enim ad perfectum adduxit lex (Hebr. VII). Secundum hoc autem consilium quod nos afferimus ad legem, possunt omnia spiritualiter fieri, et sacrificia offerri, quae modo carnaliter non possunt; potest lex leprae spiritualiter servari. Sicut ergo intelligimus, sentimus et consilium damus, omnia facit lex: secundum litteram vero non omnia, sed pauca.

VERS. 24.-- Moyses fecit omnia quae ille suggesserat, 247Aetc. Vir Deo familiaris, sapientissimus, non erubescens a minori et gentili doceri, formam doctoribus praebuit, ne a quibuslibet erubescant erudiri.

VERS. 26, 27.-- Quidquid autem gravius erat, etc. (AUG., loc. 88.) Alii: Verbum autem grave referebant super Moysen: sic enim habet Graecus sermo, quod Latini interpretati sunt, ad Moysen. Illa sane locutione ipsa etiam cura figuratur, qua dictum est super Moysen, velut onus significatur ei impositum. Verbum autem grave dictum est pro quaestione gravi.

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1-4.-- And when Jethro had heard, etc. That Moses, when he was about to deliver the children of Israel, sent away his wife and sons, whom afterwards he received back in the desert, signifies that preachers, when they are sent to the work of preaching, ought to unburden themselves of temporal solicitude: but when afterwards they are taken up to the episcopate or to some other dignity, then they ought to bear solicitude for those subject to them 244Bboth in spiritual and in temporal things.

VERS. 5-11.-- Jethro therefore came, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11 in Exod.) Another reading: After these things Moses came to the mount of God, and Jethro went out of the camp to meet him as he came toward him, nor did he lead him to the mount of God, but to his own tent.
