Ne nos ergo vocabulorum similitudo conturbet. Sunt ergo duo litigantes, duo disputantes et dogmatibus vel quaestionibus legis, et rixantes circa verborum pugnas; unde Apostolus, II Tim. II: Nolite contendere verbis, etc. Et ibidem: Quaestiones legis devita, sciens quod generant lites. Qui ergo istis in quaestionibus 258Dlitigant ad subversionem audientium, percutiunt mulierem praegnantem, et ejiciunt infantem formatum vel adhuc informem. Mulier praegnans dicitur anima, quae nuper concepit verbum Dei; unde: A timore tuo, Domine, in utero concepimus et peperimus (Isa. XXVI). Qui ergo concipiunt, et statim pariunt, non mulieres existimandae sunt, sed viri et perfecti; unde alibi: Si peperit terra in una die, et 259Anata est gens die una simul (Isa. LXVI). Ista est ergo generatio perfectorum, quae in die eadem concipitur et nascitur. Pariunt autem viri secundum illud Gal. IV: Filioli mei, quos iterum parturio, donec formetur in vobis Christus. Perfecti ergo et viri statim pariunt, id est, conceptum fidei verbum in opera perducunt: anima autem quae concipit, et in ventre retinet et non parit, mulier dicitur. Unde Isa. XXXVII: Dolores partus advenerunt ei: et virtus pariendi non est in ea. Haec ergo, inter se duobus litigantibus et scandala proferentibus, percutitur et scandalizatur: ut verbum fidei quod tenuiter conceperat nondum formatum abjiciat. Est enim in aliquibus formatum verbum, in aliquibus non formatum; unde: Donec formetur in vobis Christus (Gal. IV); Christus enim est Verbum Dei. 259BDamnum ergo patietur, qui excussit. De damnis ergo doctorem dicit Apostolus I Cor. III: Si cujus opus arserit damnum patietur, etc. Et ipse Dominus in Evangelio, Matth. XVI: Quid prodest homini si universum mundum lucretur, animam autem suam perdat? Unde videtur ostendi quod quaedam peccata ad damnum pertinent, non tamen ad interitum, quia qui damnum passus fuerit, salvari dicitur, licet per ignem: unde Joannes dicit quaedam peccata esse ad mortem, quaedam non (I Joan. V). Quae autem sint ad mortem, non facile discerni potest, Delicta enim quis intelligit? (Psal. XVIII.) Sed cum referuntur super quinque mnas acquisitae aliae quinque, et super duas duae (Luc. XIX), qui mercedem hujus acquisitionis perdit, damno dicitur percuti, sicut qui causam scandali infirmiori 259Cet muliebri animae praebuit. Dabit autem secundum quod indixerit vir ejus, et dabit cum honore animae discentis. Vir magister est Christus, vel qui pro Christo animarum doctor est. Patietur ergo damnum qui contendit ad subversionem audientium, pro anima quae nondum habuit infantem formatum: quod potest de scandalo catechumeni accipi. Potest etiam fieri ut qui laesit, instruat et reparet animae quae perdidit, et cum honore, cum modestia scilicet, cum mansuetudine, non cum lite, sicut cum scandalum intulit. Sed cum formatus infans fuerit, dabit animam pro anima. Formatus infans potest videri sermo Dei in corde animae quae suscepit gratiam baptismatis, vel qui evidentius et clarius verbum Dei suscepit. Reddet ergo qui percussit, animam pro 259Danima in die judicii, scilicet apud eum judicem qui potest animam et corpus perdere in gehennam. Vel ut qui sibi conscius fuerit tanti scandali, ponat animam suam pro anima scandalizati, et usque ad mortem det operam quomodo ad fidem restituatur. Ponat oculum pro oculo: si oculum animae laesit, id est, intellectum ejus turbavit, auferatur oculus ejus ab eo qui praeest Ecclesiae, id est intellectus. Si dentem laesit, qui suscipiens cibum verbi Dei comminuere vel molaribus terere solebat, ut subtilem ad ventrem animae transmitteret sensum: si hunc dentem ille vexaverit et evulserit, ut contritione ejus non possit anima subtiliter et spiritualiter suscipere verbum Dei, auferetur dens ejus, qui non bene cibos 260Averbi comminuit et dividit. Propter hoc enim fortasse de Domino dicitur: Dentes peccatorum contrivisti (Psl. III). Et alibi: Molas leonum confringet Dominus (Psal. LVII). Sic ergo per membra dicitur laedi anima et percuti. Manum quoque pro manu, et pedem pro pede deposcit. Manus animae, virtus qua tenere aliquid et cor stringere potest, ut actus ejus et fortitudo; et pes quo incedit ad bona vel ad mala. Quia ergo scandalizata anima non solum in fide sed etiam in actibus dejicitur, qui per manus significantur, manus auferuntur illius, quibus non bene operatur, et pedes, quibus non bene incedit. Recipiet etiam combusturam, quia combussit et gehennae tradidit animam. Per singula haec ostenditur, ut percussor, truncatis omnibus membris, a corpore Ecclesiae excidatur, 260But caeteri videntes timorem habeant, et non similiter faciant. Ideo Apostolus cum doctorem describit Ecclesiae, inter caetera praecipit, non percussorem (I Tim. III): ne percutiens praegnantes mulieres, id est animas insipientes, ponat animam pro anima, oculum pro oculo, dentem pro dente, etc. De qualibus dicitur Matth. XXIV: Vae praegnantibus et nutrientibus in illis diebus, in quibus scandalizabuntur, si fieri potest, etiam electi. Sciendum tamen quod non est perfectorum scandalizari, sed mulierum aut parvulorum; unde Matth. XVIII: Si quis scandalizaverit unum de pusillis istis, etc.

VERS. 29-36.-- Quod si bos cornupeta, etc. (RAB.) Hoc est, si superbum motum animi dominus, id est spiritus, non constringit, et animabus interitum, vel 260Ccorporibus ingerit incommodum, reatus redundat in custodem. Unde Proverbiorum decimo sexto capitulo: Cor hominis erudiet os ejus, et labiis illius addet gratiam.

Triginta siclos. Hoc est secundum legis mandata, per fidem et spem et charitatem emendabit.

Argenti. Nitorem divini verbi, quo in fide Trinitatis et scientia decalogi instructi erant, compensabit.

Si quis aperuerit, etc. (GREG., lib. XVII Moral., cap. 13.) Cisternam aperire est valido intellectu Scripturae sacrae arcana penetrare, etc., usque ad inde infirmitas auditorum ad ima dilabatur.

Si bos alienus, etc. (AUG.) Non hoc in solo bove servandum est, sed in omni pecore; a parte enim 260Dtotum intelligitur. Sed hoc de carnibus occisi pecoris fieri non potest, quo non vescuntur.

CHAPTER XXI.

  • 257A

VERS. 1. 6.-- These are the judgments, etc. (AUG., loc. 91 in Exod.) From now on those things are more distinctly explained which pertain to one's neighbour; the manner of speaking is to be noted, since it was said to Moses: These are the judgments which thou shalt set before them; the rest are said to the people.

If thou buy a Hebrew servant, etc. (AUG., quaest. 77 in Exod.) Lest Christians should here demand this of their masters, the apostle commands servants to be subject to their masters (I Petr. II), lest the name of the Lord and the doctrine be blasphemed: for from this it is plain that it was commanded as a mystery, because God commanded the ear of him who had refused liberty to be bored through with an awl to the door-post.

(GREG., hom. 3 in Ezech., tom. II.) It must be known that, 257Bsince the active and the contemplative life are from the grace of God, etc., as far as because that inmost rest is seen in a dark manner.

Six years. (ISID.) Serving in the six ages of this world, on the seventh day, that is, in the eternal sabbath, we shall be set free, if only we have been willing to be free while in this world we serve sin. But if we have been unwilling, the ear will be bored through in testimony of disobedience, and with our wife and our sons, whom we have preferred to liberty, that is, with the flesh and its works, we shall serve sin for ever.

VERS. 7-12.-- If a man sell his daughter, etc. (AUG., quaest. 78 in Exod.) She shall not go out as maidservants are wont to depart, etc., as far as it is understood that she shall go out with him freely, not separated from her husband.

VERS. 13-17.-- But he who lay not in wait for him, etc. (RAB., in Exod., tom. II.) For those who are homicides not of their own will, etc., as far as he shall have set of Christ, he shall be saved.

(AUG., quaest. 79 in Exod.) But if he did it unwillingly, and God delivered him into his hands, etc. This is so said, etc., as far as and therefore Judas justly betrayed him.

VERS. 18.-- If men quarrel, etc. (RAB.) It is the same thing, if a man, contending with his neighbour, has exasperated him by hardness of mind, or by niggardliness, that is, by despising him or by not taking counsel with him; and he, made weak by sadness, has languished away from the fervour of love: let the guilty man, by confession, expend upon the physicians, that is, the teachers, the labour of penance and the cost of the promised amendment, that they may raise up the sick man with the staff of consolation, and by the fomentations of preachings restore him to his former state of love. 257DIn correction discretion is necessary, lest he who ought to correct should kill, or should cast a man into despair or into hatred.

VERS. 22-28.-- If men quarrel. (ID.) If anyone, after the conceiving of the word, has injured another's soul through carelessness or through fraud, so that it cannot bring forth sound the living germ of a good work: even if that soul itself has persevered still living in the faith, it will be necessary, according to the ministry of the Gospel, that he make restitution 258Athrough penance for the loss inflicted. But if the soul itself, slain by error, after the bringing forth of the abortion, that is, of the death-bearing work, has persevered in unbelief, without doubt the seducer of it, as a true homicide, will be guilty of eternal death.

(AUG., quaest. 80 in Exod.) That he would not have an unformed birth pertain to homicide, etc., as far as and therefore not yet endowed with senses.

(ORIG., hom. 10 in Exod.) But if two men shall quarrel, and shall strike a woman with child, and her infant shall come forth not yet formed, he shall suffer detriment, as much as the woman's husband shall lay upon him, and he shall give for his own soul. But if it shall have been formed, he shall give life for life, eye for eye, etc. [Vulg. Ex 21:22-23 et abortivum quidem fecerit, sed ipsa vixerit: subjacebit damno quantum maritus mulieris expetierit, et arbitri judicaverint. Sin autem mors ejus fuerit subsecuta, reddet animam pro anima; the Vulgate has no formed/unformed distinction at all, and the whole gloss below expounds it; the reading Migne prints is the Septuagint's] Let us see first concerning him 258Bwho shall come forth not yet formed, how one of the litigants is commanded to be struck with the loss of money, whereas two are spoken of. And also this, that the woman's husband lays it upon him, and not upon them; and he shall give, and not rather they shall give. But if the infant shall come forth already formed, we easily understand the giving of life for life: that is, that the sin be avenged by death. But the things which follow it is worth while to explain. For neither does the infant, even formed, seem to have lost an eye in the womb, when struck by the heel of a litigant, and teeth it had not at all. For if we refer this to the mother, how does a woman miscarrying lose an eye, or suffer in her teeth? But be it that, struck either in the eye or in the tooth, she miscarried, and received a bruise or a wound, what shall we say also of burning for burning? Could a woman standing by men 258Cwho quarrel be burned, so that burning should be paid for burning? In Deuteronomy also these things cannot easily be explained. Yet in the Scriptures the members of the soul are named by the same words and offices by which the members of the body are used or called. Whence Matth. VII: Thou seest the mote in thy brother's eye, and the beam which is in thine own eye thou seest not. And Matth. XIII: He that hath ears to hear, let him hear. And Rom. X: How beautiful are the feet of them that preach peace! Let not, therefore, the likeness of words disturb us. There are, then, two litigants, two disputing also about the doctrines or the questions of the law, and quarrelling about strifes of words; whence the Apostle, II Tim. II: Contend not in words, etc. And in the same place: Avoid the questions of the law, knowing that they beget strifes. They, therefore, who 258Dquarrel in these questions to the subverting of the hearers, strike a woman with child, and cast out an infant formed or still unformed. The woman with child is called the soul, which has lately conceived the word of God; whence: From thy fear, O Lord, we have conceived in the womb and have brought forth (Isa. XXVI). They, therefore, who conceive and straightway bring forth, are not to be reckoned women, but men and perfect; whence elsewhere: If the earth brought forth in one day, and 259Aa nation was born in one day together (Isa. LXVI). This, then, is the generation of the perfect, which on the same day is conceived and born. And men bring forth according to that of Gal. IV: My little children, of whom I am in labour again, until Christ be formed in you. The perfect, therefore, and men bring forth straightway, that is, they carry through into works the word of faith conceived: but the soul which conceives, and holds in the womb and does not bring forth, is called a woman. Whence Isa. XXXVII: The pains of birth are come upon her: and there is not strength in her to bring forth. This soul, therefore, while two are quarrelling among themselves and putting forth scandals, is struck and scandalized: so that it casts away the word of faith which it had thinly conceived, not yet formed. For in some the word is formed, in some not formed; whence: Until Christ be formed in you (Gal. IV); for Christ is the Word of God. 259BHe, therefore, shall suffer loss who has struck it out. Concerning losses, therefore, the Apostle speaks of the teacher, I Cor. III: If any man's work burn, he shall suffer loss, etc. [Vulg. 1 Cor 3:15 detrimentum patietur, "he shall suffer detriment"; the exposition that follows is built throughout on the word damnum which Migne prints] And the Lord himself in the Gospel, Matth. XVI: What does it profit a man if he gain the whole world, but lose his own soul? [Vulg. Mt 16:26 animae vero suae detrimentum patiatur, "and suffer the loss of his own soul"] Whence it seems to be shown that certain sins pertain to loss, yet not to destruction, because he who shall have suffered loss is said to be saved, though by fire: whence John says that certain sins are unto death, certain not (I Joan. V). But which are unto death cannot easily be discerned, For who understands sins? (Psal. XVIII.) But when, upon five minas, five others gained are brought back, and upon two, two (Luc. XIX), he who loses the reward of this gain is said to be struck with loss, like him who has given cause of scandal to a weaker 259Cand womanish soul. And he shall give according to what her husband shall lay upon him, and he shall give with honour to the soul that learns. The husband is the master Christ, or he who is for Christ a teacher of souls. He, therefore, shall suffer loss who contends to the subverting of the hearers, for the soul which had not yet a formed infant: which can be taken of the scandal of a catechumen. It can also come to pass that he who has injured should instruct and repair the soul which he has lost, and that with honour, that is, with modesty, with meekness, not with strife, as when he brought in the scandal. But when the infant shall have been formed, he shall give life for life. The formed infant may be seen to be the word of God in the heart of the soul which has received the grace of baptism, or which has received the word of God more evidently and more clearly. He, therefore, who struck shall render life for 259Dlife in the day of judgment, that is, before that judge who can destroy both soul and body in hell. Or, that he who shall be conscious to himself of so great a scandal, should lay down his own soul for the soul of the man scandalized, and even unto death give his labour that he may be restored to the faith. Let him lay down eye for eye: if he has injured the eye of the soul, that is, has troubled its understanding, let his eye be taken away from him by him who presides over the Church, that is, his understanding. If he has injured a tooth, which, receiving the food of the word of God, was wont to break it small or to grind it with the molars, that it might pass on a subtle meaning to the belly of the soul: if he has vexed and plucked out this tooth, so that by the wearing away of it the soul cannot subtly and spiritually receive the word of God, his tooth shall be taken away, who does not well break small and divide the foods 260Aof the word. For on this account perhaps it is said of the Lord: Thou hast broken the teeth of sinners (Psl. III). And elsewhere: The Lord shall break the grinders of the lions (Psal. LVII). So, then, the soul is said to be injured and struck through its members. He demands also hand for hand, and foot for foot. The hand of the soul is the power by which it can hold something and grasp it to the heart, as its act and its strength; and the foot is that by which it goes to good things or to evil. Because, therefore, the soul that is scandalized is cast down not only in faith but also in acts, which are signified by the hands, his hands are taken away by which he does not work well, and his feet, by which he does not walk well. He shall also receive burning, because he burned and delivered a soul to hell. By each of these things it is shown that the striker, all his members being lopped off, is to be cut away from the body of the Church, 260Bthat the rest, seeing it, may have fear, and may not do the like. Therefore the Apostle, when he describes the teacher of the Church, among other things enjoins, not a striker (I Tim. III): lest, striking women with child, that is, unwise souls, he should lay down life for life, eye for eye, tooth for tooth, etc. Of such it is said, Matth. XXIV: Woe to them that are with child and that give suck in those days, in which they shall be scandalized, if it be possible, even the elect. [Vulg. Mt 24:19 ends at in illis diebus; the clause that follows welds on Mt 24:24 ita ut in errorem inducantur (si fieri potest) etiam electi, "so that even the elect (if it be possible) be led into error"] Yet it must be known that it does not belong to the perfect to be scandalized, but to women or to little ones; whence Matth. XVIII: If any man scandalize one of these little ones, etc.

VERS. 29-36.-- But if the ox was wont to push with his horn, etc. (RAB.) That is, if the master, that is the spirit, does not restrain the proud motion of the mind, and brings destruction upon souls, or 260Charm upon bodies, the guilt redounds upon the keeper. Whence in the sixteenth chapter of Proverbs: The heart of a man shall instruct his mouth, and shall add grace to his lips. [Vulg. Pr 16:23 Cor sapientis erudiet os ejus, "The heart of the wise shall instruct his mouth"]

Thirty shekels. That is, according to the commandments of the law, he shall make amends by faith and hope and charity.

Of silver. He shall repay the brightness of the divine word, in which they had been instructed in the faith of the Trinity and the knowledge of the decalogue.

If a man open, etc. (GREG., lib. XVII Moral., cap. 13.) To open a cistern is to penetrate the secrets of Holy Scripture with a strong understanding, etc., as far as thence the weakness of the hearers should slip down to the depths.

If another's ox, etc. (AUG.) This is to be observed not in the ox alone, but in every beast; for from the part the 260Dwhole is understood. But this cannot be done concerning the flesh of a beast that has been killed, on which they do not feed.

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 1.-- Si quis furatus fuerit, etc. (STRAB.) Secundum litteram quinque boves pro uno bove, et quatuor oves pro uno bove redduntur: quia bos quinque utilitates habet, ovis quatuor. Bos enim immolatur, arat, pascit, lactat, et corium diversis usibus ministrat; ovis vero immolatur, et pascit, lac et lanam tribuit. Allegorice vero per bovem Judaeus quilibet intelligitur, quem si quis Judaeus vendiderit, poenae legis subjacebit, quae continetur in quinque libris Moysi. Per ovem Christianus, qui Christum 261Aimitatur, de quo dicitur: Sicut ovis ad occisionem ductus est (Isa. LIII). Si quis hanc vendiderit, quatuor oves reddat, id est secundum institutionem quatuor Evangeliorum poenitentiam agat.

VERS. 2-5.-- Si effringens fur domum, sive suffodiens, etc. (AUG., quaest. 84 in Exod.) Si perfodiens inventus fuerit fur et percussus mortuus fuerit, etc., usque ad jam enim plus est quam fur.

VERS. 6-13.-- Si egressus ignis, etc. (RAB.) Ignem succendimus, cum correctionem subditis praeparamus, ut spinas vitiorum consumamus. Sed videndum est ne cum vitia persequimur, virtutes laedamus, et eis qui fructum bonorum operum proferre coeperant, ante maturitatem noceamus. Qui hoc ergo commiserit, dignam poenitentiam gerat, et quod per inertiam 261Blaesit, studiose restituat.

Spicas. Teneros auditores nondum ad maturitatem fructus pertingentes: quorum vitia qui acerbe et indiscrete reprehendit, quasi ignem in spicas immittit, nec ad maturitatem pervenire sinit.

VERS. 14-16.-- Qui a proximo, etc. A proximo mutuum postulamus, cum pro aeterna remuneratione curam Dominici gregis suscipimus. Sed si timorem Dei, quasi pecuniam exigentis, semper ante oculos cordis ponimus, quasi in praesentia ejus semper sumus. Unde II Cor. V: Scientes timorem Domini, hominibus suademus. Et alibi, II Cor. XII: Quasi coram Deo, in Christo loquimur. Mors ergo aut debilitas pecoris pastori non imputatur, si ejus curam strenue gessit, et quasi Deo praesente; aliter damnum 261Creddere compellitur. Unde Matth. V: Non exibit inde, donec reddat novissimum quadrantem.

VERS. 17.-- Si pater virginis, etc. (RAB.) Sensus sententiae historicus, de qua nascitur oratio. Si ergo historicus sensus regnum charitatis aedificat, cupiditatis dissipat, non est necesse allegorice exponi. Lector tamen sententiam dote rectae fidei honorare debet et intactam sua expositione relinquere.

VERS. 18-20.-- Maleficos non patieris vivere. Qui praestigiis magicae artis et diabolicis figmentis agunt, haereticos intellige, qui a consortio fidelium qui vere vivunt, excommunicandi sunt, donec maleficium erroris in eis moriatur

VERS. 21.-- Advenam non contristabis. (RAB.) Dilectionem proximi per misericordiae compassionem 261Dcommendat, ne furoris aut avaritiae stimulo commoti, jus naturae negligamus. Sed quae volumus ut faciant nobis homines eadem illis faciamus, et quae nolumus fieri, non faciamus.

VERS. 22-24.-- Viduae et pupillo non nocebitis. (RAB.) In vidua, sancta Ecclesia, quae in die judicii exspectat Christum, quem modo non habet praesentem in pupillo, Christianus populus, cui mortuus est mundus pater, et Babylonia mater, accipi potest: quam prohibet contristari per persecutionem vel fastum mundanum, ne forte talis insectatio ad perniciem eorum spiritualem perveniat qui eos frustra odiis et contumeliis molestant.

VERS. 25.-- Si pecunia mutuam, etc. (RAB.) Praedicatores 262AEvangelii monet ne superfluitatem appetant, sed habentes victum et vestitum, his contenti sint, secundum illud Matth. X: Gratis accepistis, gratis date.

VERS. 26, 27.-- Si pignus, etc. (AUG., loc. 99.) Genus pro specie posuit: sic enim dictum est ac si de omni veste pignus dederit, cum de illo se specialiter dicere ex consequentibus Scriptura testatur, qui non habet nisi eam vestem unde nocte operiatur.

(GREG., lib. XVI Moral., cap. 2.) Pignus, peccati confessio. Proximus enim debitor noster efficitur, etc., usque ad a nobis mox relaxatum sentiat quod deliquit.

VERS. 28.-- Diis non detrahes, etc. (AUG., quaest, 262B86 in Exod.) Quaeritur quos dixerit deos, etc., usque ad vel latriae obsequiis honorari.

VERS. 29-31.-- Decimas tuas, etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Per primitias frugum vel primogenitorum primitiva bonorum operum ostenduntur, etc., usque ad et perfectionis donatur effectus.

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 1.-- If any man steal, etc. (STRAB.) According to the letter, five oxen are rendered for one ox, and four sheep for one sheep: because an ox has five uses, a sheep four. For an ox is sacrificed, ploughs, feeds, gives milk, and furnishes its hide for various uses; but a sheep is sacrificed, and feeds, and yields milk and wool. Allegorically, however, by the ox is understood any Jew, and if any Jew sell him, he will lie under the penalty of the law, which is contained in the five books of Moses. By the sheep, the Christian, who imitates Christ, 261Aof whom it is said: He was led as a sheep to the slaughter (Isa. LIII). If any man sell this one, let him render four sheep, that is, let him do penance according to the ordinance of the four Gospels.

VERS. 2-5.-- If a thief be found breaking open a house, or undermining it, etc. (AUG., quaest. 84 in Exod.) If a thief be found breaking in, and being struck shall die, etc., as far as for now he is more than a thief.

VERS. 6-13.-- If a fire break out, etc. (RAB.) We kindle a fire when we prepare correction for our subjects, that we may consume the thorns of vices. But it must be seen to, lest, while we pursue the vices, we injure the virtues, and harm before their ripeness those who had begun to bring forth the fruit of good works. He, therefore, who shall have done this, let him bear worthy penance, and what he has injured through slothfulness 261Blet him diligently restore.

The ears of corn. Tender hearers not yet coming to the ripeness of fruit: whose vices he who sharply and indiscreetly rebukes, casts fire as it were into the ears of corn, and does not suffer them to come to ripeness.

VERS. 14-16.-- He who from his neighbour, etc. We ask a loan of our neighbour when for an eternal recompense we take up the care of the Lord's flock. But if we always set before the eyes of the heart the fear of God, as of one exacting money, we are as it were always in his presence. Whence II Cor. V: Knowing the fear of the Lord, we persuade men. And elsewhere, II Cor. XII: As before God, in Christ we speak. The death, therefore, or the maiming of the beast is not imputed to the shepherd, if he has borne its care strenuously, and as though God were present; otherwise he is compelled to render the loss. 261CWhence Matth. V: He shall not go out thence, till he repay the last farthing.

VERS. 17.-- If the father of the virgin, etc. (RAB.) The sense of the sentence is historical, out of which the discourse is born. If, therefore, the historical sense builds up the reign of charity and destroys that of cupidity, there is no need for it to be expounded allegorically. Yet the reader ought to honour the sentence with the dowry of right faith, and to leave it untouched by his own exposition.

VERS. 18-20.-- Thou shalt not suffer wizards to live. Those who work by the sleights of the magic art and by diabolical feignings, understand as heretics, who are to be excommunicated from the fellowship of the faithful who truly live, until the evil-doing of error die in them

VERS. 21.-- Thou shalt not grieve the stranger. (RAB.) He commends the love of one's neighbour through the compassion of mercy, 261Dlest, moved by the goad of fury or of avarice, we neglect the right of nature. But what we wish men to do to us, the same let us do to them, and what we do not wish to be done, let us not do.

VERS. 22-24.-- You shall not hurt the widow and the orphan. (RAB.) By the widow, holy Church, which in the day of judgment awaits Christ, whom now she has not present; by the orphan, the Christian people, to whom the world their father, and Babylon their mother, is dead, may be taken: whom he forbids to be grieved by persecution or by worldly pride, lest perchance such harrying come to the spiritual ruin of those who vex them in vain with hatreds and reproaches.

VERS. 25.-- If money on loan, etc. [Vulg. Ex 22:25 Si pecuniam mutuam dederis, "If thou lend money"; Migne's nominative pecunia does not construe with the accusative mutuam] (RAB.) He warns the preachers 262Aof the Gospel not to seek superfluity, but having food and clothing, to be content with these, according to that of Matth. X: Freely you have received, freely give.

VERS. 26, 27.-- If a pledge, etc. (AUG., loc. 99.) He put the genus for the species: for it was so said as if he had given a pledge of any garment, whereas Scripture testifies from what follows that it speaks specially of him who has no garment but that one wherewith he may be covered by night.

(GREG., lib. XVI Moral., cap. 2.) A pledge, the confession of sin. For our neighbour is made our debtor, etc., as far as let him feel that what he has done amiss is presently remitted by us.

VERS. 28.-- Thou shalt not speak ill of the gods, etc. (AUG., quaest, 262B86 in Exod.) It is asked whom he called gods, etc., as far as or to be honoured with the services of latria.

VERS. 29-31.-- Thy tithes, etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) By the firstfruits of the crops or of the firstborn are shown the first beginnings of good works, etc., as far as and the effect of perfection is granted.

CAPUT XXIII.

VERS. 3.-- Pauperis quoque non misereberis in judicio. (AUG.) Ne scilicet contra justitiam pauperi faveas causa misericordiae. Bona est misericordia, sed non debet esse contra judicium. Judicium autem dicit Scriptura, quod justum est. Ne quis autem propter hanc sententiam Deum putet prohibere misericordiam, opportune sequitur:

262CVERS. 4-6.-- Si occurreris bovi inimici tui, etc. Quasi fac misericordiam etiam erga inimicos tuos, cum potestas est a judicando libera; non enim cum reducis errantem bovem inimici et reddis, inter aliquos judex resides.

VERS. 7-9.-- Mendacium fugies. (RAB.) Veritatem et judicium amat Dominus: et ad haec exsequenda populum suum hortatur, quia non est personarum acceptor.

VERS. 10-12.-- Sex annis seminabis terram tuam. (AUG.) Quaeritur quid colligant pauperes, si in septimo anno ita parcitur terrae, etc., usque ad sex annos cum colliguntur quae seminantur.

(RAB.) In senario perfectio operum, etc., usque ad qui seminat in benedictionibus, in benedictionibus et 262Dmetet.

VERS. 13.-- Et per nomen externorum deorum, etc. Per nomen suum jurare permittit, non quod bonum sit jurare; sed quia sciebat homines pronos ad jurandum esse, non vult ut jurent per nomina exteriorum deorum, ne frequenti juramento inducantur ad cultum eorum. Sic et hostias non approbat Deus, mavult tamen sacrificari sibi quam idolis.

VERS. 14-18.-- Tribus vicibus per singulos annos, etc. Fidem scilicet sanctae Trinitatis hic exigit Deus: in Pascha enim immolatur Filius, in Pentecoste datur Spiritus sanctus, in Scenopegia paternae majestatis potentia declaratur.

Tribus vicibus per singulos annos, etc. (RAB.) Hoc 263Aest omni tempore praesentis vitae et in sanctae Trinitatis confessione, per fidem et spem et charitatem bonorum operum obsequia Deo creatori vestro exhibebitis. Pascha enim celebramus mense novorum, cum per sanguinem Agni immaculati ab Aegyptia servitute liberati, per spirituales aquas de veteri homine in novum transimus, non in vetustate litterae, sed in novitate spiritus, quo in terram promissionis per gratiam Christi pervenimus. Solemnitatem vero mensis primitivorum celebramus, cum operum nostrorum, voluntatum, et sermonum primitias Domino consecramus; solemnitatem quoque in exitu anni, quando congregatis frugibus, id est virtutum fructibus, ad finem vitae et introitum regni coelestis pervenire contendimus; in tabernaculis septem diebus 263Bcommemorantes, quia per omne tempus vitae praesentis peregrinos nos esse agnoscimus, nec requiem hic, sed in futuro requirimus, sedulo curantes ne in conspectu Dei vacui appareamus.

Solemnitatem quoque, etc. (RAB.) Maturis scilicet, frugibus, quando fructus totius anni collecti sunt, in septembri scilicet quem Hebraei Chaldaico vocabulo thisri, תשרי Macedones ὑπερβεραῖον vocant. In cujus principio Judaei feriantes amplius tuba canunt quam in caeteris mensibus, et plura offerunt, in quo dies expiationum, et dies scenopegiae quinto decimo die, qui est solemnitas Tabernaculorum.

VERS. 19.-- Non coques haedum, etc. (AUG., quaest. 90 in Exod.) Quomodo intelligatur, ad verborum proprietatem nescio utrum possit reperiri, 263Cetc., usque ad postea vero passi sunt non agni in lacte, sed arietes in grege.

(STRAB.) Moraliter. Haedus, id est peccator, non est in lacte matris coquendus, id est blanditiis dissolvendus, sed aspere increpandus et redarguendus.

VERS. 20, 21.-- Ecce ego mittam angelum, etc. (RAB.) Josue significat ducem populi, qui ideo angelus dicitur, quia ad voluntatem Domini annuntiandam mittitur; ἄγγελος enim Graece, nuntius dicitur.

VERS. 22.-- Inimicus ero, etc. (GREG.) Radix cuncti mali superbia est, de qua dicitur Eccli. X: Initium omnis peccati superbia. Sunt autem ejus soboles septem principalia vitia, inanis gloria, invidia, 263Dira, tristitia, avaritia, ventris ingluvies, luxuria. Et quia his septem superbiae vitiis nos captos doluit Redemptor noster, ad spirituale liberationis praelium Spiritu septiformis gratiae plenus venit.

VERS. 23, 24.-- Praecedetque te angelus. (RAB.) Josue populum Israeliticum in terram promissionis induxit, septem gentes ejiciens, idolaque et aras eorum destruens. Mystice verus Jesus has gentes cum simulacris suis exterminat, cum septem principalia vitia et omne idolum inimici de cordibus nostris expellit, ut naturae nostrae terram secure possideamus.

Quos ego conteram. (CHRYSOST.) Habet unumquodque vitium in corde nostro propriam mansionem, 264Aquam sibi vindicans, in animae nostrae recessu exterminat Israelem, id est rerum summarum contemplationem, et adversari non desinit: non enim possunt virtutes cum vitiis morari. Sed cum ab Israelitis, id est a virtutibus, fuerint vitia superata, locum quem concupiscentiae vel fornicationis spiritus possidebat, castitas obtinebit: quem furor ceperat, patientia vindicabit; quem tristitia mortem operans occupaverat, salutaris et plena gaudio laetitia possidebit; quem acedia vastavit, excolet fortitudo; quem superbia conculcabat, humilitas honestabit. Itaque singulis vitiis expulsis, eorum loca, id est affectus, virtutes contrariae possidebunt: quae filii Israelis, id est animae Deum videntis nuncupantur: qui cum universas cordis expulerint passiones, non tam alienas possessiones 264Brapuisse quam proprias recuperasse credendi sunt; quia secundum veterem traditionem, terram Chananaeorum filii Sem in divisione orbis sortiti sunt, quam Chananaei violenter possederunt: unde justo, judicio Dei expulsi sunt. Voluntas quoque Domini possessionem cordis nostri non vitiis sed virtutibus deputavit, quae post praevaricationem Adae insolescentibus vitiis, quasi Chananaeis, a propria regione depulsae sunt. Cum vero per Dei gratiam diligentia nostra et labore nostro restituuntur, non tam alienam terram occupant quam recipiunt propriam.

VERS. 25-27.-- Benedicam panibus tuis, etc. (AUG.) Alia littera: Benedicam panem et vinum tuum, etc. Quamvis hae promissiones possint et spiritualiter intelligi, etc., usque ad quae impii non accipient.

264CVERS. 28.-- Emittens crabrones. (AUG., quaest. 93.) Emittam vespas, etc. Hoc promittit Deus, etc., usque ad si aliquid a Christo dicitur.

Alia littera. Emittam vespas ante te, et ejiciet Amorrhaeos, etc. A plurali ad singularem numerum se convertit. Intelligitur autem ejiciet Amorrhaeos, vespa, scilicet sicut locusta, sicut rana, non quod una sit, sed per singularem intelligitur pluralis.

VERS. 29-33.-- Non ejiciam eos a facie, etc. (STRAB.) Gentes istae vitia significant, quae nec simul, nec uno tempore in sanctis exstinguuntur, ne monstra terrae multiplicentur, id est ne in superbiam erigantur et vanam gloriam et hujusmodi. Nam cum sancti contra majora vitia pugnant et vincunt, relinquunt 264Dminora, et permittuntur dominari, ne per elationem gravius corruant. Unde Paulo datus est stimulus carnis ad custodiam humilitatis (I Cor. XXII).

CHAPTER XXIII.

VERS. 3.-- Neither shalt thou have pity on a poor man in judgment. (AUG.) That is, lest against justice thou favour the poor man for mercy's sake. Mercy is good, but it ought not to be against judgment. Now Scripture calls judgment that which is just. But lest anyone, on account of this sentence, should think that God forbids mercy, there fittingly follows:

262CVERS. 4-6.-- If thou meet thy enemy's ox, etc. As if to say: show mercy even toward thine enemies, when the power is free from judging; for when thou leadest back the straying ox of thine enemy and restorest it, thou art not sitting as judge between any parties.

VERS. 7-9.-- Thou shalt fly lying. (RAB.) The Lord loves truth and judgment, and to the performing of these he exhorts his people, because he is no respecter of persons.

VERS. 10-12.-- Six years thou shalt sow thy ground. (AUG.) It is asked what the poor gather, if in the seventh year the land is thus spared, etc., as far as six years, when those things are gathered which are sown.

(RAB.) In the number six is the perfection of works, etc., as far as he that soweth in blessings, in blessings shall also 262Dreap. [Vulg. 2Cor 9:6 de benedictionibus et metet, "shall reap also of blessings"; Migne repeats the preposition of the first member]

VERS. 13.-- And by the name of strange gods, etc. He permits swearing by his own name, not because it is good to swear, but because he knew that men are prone to swearing; he does not will that they swear by the names of outward gods, lest by frequent oaths they be led into the worship of them. So too God does not approve victims, yet he had rather that sacrifice be made to himself than to idols.

VERS. 14-18.-- Three times in every year, etc. Here, that is, God requires the faith of the holy Trinity: for at the Pasch the Son is immolated, at Pentecost the Holy Spirit is given, at the Scenopegia the power of the Father's majesty is declared.

Three times in every year, etc. (RAB.) This 263Ais: at every season of the present life, and in the confession of the holy Trinity, through faith and hope and charity, you shall render the services of good works to God your creator. For we celebrate the Pasch in the month of new things, when, delivered by the blood of the unspotted Lamb from Egyptian servitude, we pass through spiritual waters out of the old man into the new, not in the oldness of the letter but in the newness of the spirit, whereby we come into the land of promise through the grace of Christ. But we celebrate the solemnity of the month of firstfruits when we consecrate to the Lord the firstfruits of our works, of our wills and of our words; the solemnity also at the going out of the year, when, the crops being gathered in, that is, the fruits of the virtues, we strive to come to the end of life and to the entrance of the heavenly kingdom; in tabernacles 263Bcommemorating for seven days, because through all the time of the present life we acknowledge that we are pilgrims, and seek rest not here but in the time to come, diligently taking care that we appear not empty in the sight of God.

The solemnity also, etc. (RAB.) When the crops, that is, are ripe, when the fruits of the whole year have been gathered: namely in September, which the Hebrews call by the Chaldee word thisri, תשרי, and the Macedonians ὑπερβεραῖον. At the beginning of it the Jews, keeping holiday, sound the trumpet more than in the other months, and offer more things; in which month is the day of expiations, and the day of the Scenopegia on the fifteenth day, which is the solemnity of Tabernacles.

VERS. 19.-- Thou shalt not boil a kid, etc. (AUG., quaest. 90 in Exod.) How it is to be understood according to the propriety of the words, I know not whether it can be found out, 263Cetc., as far as but afterwards it was not lambs in milk that suffered, but rams in the flock.

(STRAB.) Morally. The kid, that is, the sinner, is not to be boiled in his mother's milk, that is, dissolved by blandishments, but sharply rebuked and refuted.

VERS. 20, 21.-- Behold I will send an angel, etc. (RAB.) Josue signifies the leader of the people, who is therefore called an angel because he is sent to announce the will of the Lord; for ἄγγελος in Greek is called a messenger.

VERS. 22.-- I will be an enemy, etc. (GREG.) The root of all evil is pride, of which it is said in Eccli. X: Pride the beginning of all sin. Now its offspring are the seven principal vices: vainglory, envy, 263Danger, sadness, avarice, gluttony of the belly, lust. And because our Redeemer grieved that we were held captive by these seven vices of pride, he came full of the Spirit of sevenfold grace to the spiritual battle of our deliverance.

VERS. 23, 24.-- And an angel shall go before thee. (RAB.) Josue led the people of Israel into the land of promise, casting out seven nations and destroying their idols and their altars. Mystically the true Jesus exterminates these nations with their images, when he drives out the seven principal vices and every idol of the enemy from our hearts, that we may securely possess the land of our own nature.

Whom I will destroy. (CHRYSOST.) Every vice has its own dwelling in our heart, 264Aand, claiming that dwelling for itself, it exterminates Israel in the recess of our soul, that is, the contemplation of the highest things, and does not cease to oppose; for virtues cannot abide together with vices. But when the vices have been overcome by the Israelites, that is, by the virtues, the place which the spirit of concupiscence or of fornication possessed, chastity shall obtain; the place which fury had seized, patience shall claim; the place which sadness working death had occupied, saving gladness full of joy shall possess; the place which sloth laid waste, fortitude shall cultivate; the place which pride trod down, humility shall make honourable. And so, the several vices being expelled, their places, that is, the affections, shall be possessed by the contrary virtues; and these are named the sons of Israel, that is, of the soul that sees God: who, when they have driven out all the passions of the heart, are to be believed not so much to have seized 264Bother men's possessions as to have recovered their own; because, according to the ancient tradition, the sons of Sem obtained by lot, in the division of the world, the land of the Chananaeans, which the Chananaeans possessed by violence: whence by the just, judgment of God they were driven out. The will of the Lord also assigned the possession of our heart not to vices but to virtues, which after the transgression of Adam, when the vices grew insolent like Chananaeans, were driven from their proper region. But when by the grace of God they are restored by our diligence and by our labour, they do not so much occupy another's land as receive back their own.

VERS. 25-27.-- I will bless thy loaves, etc. (AUG.) Another reading: I will bless thy bread and thy wine, etc. Although these promises can be understood spiritually also, etc., as far as which the ungodly shall not receive.

264CVERS. 28.-- Sending out hornets. (AUG., quaest. 93.) I will send wasps, etc. This God promises, etc., as far as if anything is said by Christ.

Another reading. I will send wasps before thee, and it shall cast out the Amorrhites, etc. He turns himself from the plural to the singular number. And it shall cast out the Amorrhites is understood of the wasp, namely as of the locust, as of the frog: not that it is one, but by the singular the plural is understood.

VERS. 29-33.-- I will not cast them out from the face, etc. (STRAB.) These nations signify the vices, which are extinguished in the saints neither all together nor at one time, lest the monsters of the earth be multiplied, that is, lest they be lifted up into pride and vainglory and things of this kind. For when the saints fight against the greater vices and conquer, they leave 264Dthe lesser, and these are permitted to have dominion, lest through elation they fall more grievously. Whence to Paul was given a sting of the flesh for the keeping of humility (I Cor. XXII).

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- Moysi quoque dixit: Ascende ad Dominum, tu et Aaron, etc. (AUG., quaest. 85 in Exod., tom. III.) Usque ad hunc locum Scripturae justificationes intelliguntur, etc., usque ad justificationes vero Latini eas esse dicunt quae Graeci δικαιώματα dicunt.

Tu et Aaron, etc. (STRAB.) Moyses et Aaron et Nadab et Abiu. Per hos quatuor viri evangelici significantur, qui scilicet evangelica praecepta sequuntur. 265APer septuaginta vero (qui constat ex septenario decies scilicet ducto, vel ex denario septies multiplicato) significantur illi qui Spiritus sancti gratia adjuti, adimplentes denarium legis, tendunt ad contemplationem Conditoris. Vel ad litteram de quibus dicitur Num. XI: Assume tibi septuaginta viros, etc., de quibus Heldad et Meldad fuerunt.

VERS. 2.-- Solusque Moyses ascendet ad Dominum. (RAB.) Prius praecipitur ut Moyses et Aaron, Nadab et Abiu, et septuaginta seniores, ascendant ad Dominum. Et statim subjungitur quod solus Moyses ascendat ad Dominum, caeteri non appropinquent, sed adorent procul. Ubi moraliter instruimur vires nostras perpendere, nec ultra posse nostrum arcem contemplationis attingere, sed quantum divina clementia 265Bvocat, et regula Scripturae provocat.

(GREG.) Moyses ut sublimia Dei praecepta perciperet, alta conscendit; et ut interna penetret, ab externis tumultibus occultatur: quia viri sancti qui exterioribus officiis deservire necessitate coguntur, studiose semper ad cordis secreta confugiunt, ibique cogitationis intimae cacumen ascendunt, et legem quasi in monte percipiunt, dum, postpositis tumultibus actionum temporalium, in contemplationis suae vertice supernae voluntatis sententiam perscrutantur.

VERS. 3-5.-- Faciemus (AUG., quaest. 95.) Notandum quod iterum populus, etc., usque ad apprehenderunt justitiam.

VERS. 6-9.-- Tulit itaque, etc. [ID., quaest. 98.] 265CNotandum est nunc primum sacrificasse Mosem, etc., usque ad nam decalogum in tabulis lapideis fuisse scriptum postea patet.

Crateras. Cratera, sive crater, dicitur ἀπὸ τοῦ κρατεῖν, quod est capere, eo quod sit multi vini capax.

VERS. 10, 11.-- Sub pedibus. Deus non loco continetur, nec situ corporis membra ponit, sicut est nostri corporis sedere, et jacere, stare, et hujusmodi, quae tantum corporis sunt. Deus autem spiritus est. Quod ergo se ostendit specie corporali, vel signis corporaliter expressis, non substantia ejus apparet, quae est quod ipse est, sed assumptio formarum visibilium ejus omni potentiae subjacet.

VERS. 12.-- Ascende ad me, etc. (BED., lib. I de 265DTabernac., cap. 11, tom. II.) Locuturi de figura tabernaculi, etc., usque ad ut Ecclesia per orbem cunctarum gentium linguis locutura designaretur.

Daboque tibi. (ID.) Huic simile est illud: Docentes eos servare omnia quaecunque mandavi vobis (Matth. XXVIII). Non ergo Moyses, non apostoli, vel quilibet, alia debet docere quam quae Dominus docuit, et consilii decreto observanda praescripsit, in quibus vitam aeternam nos habere voluit, sicut de his praeteriri quidquam non oportet: sed omnia quae mandavit, auditoribus committere necesse est.

VERS. 13.-- Surrexerunt Moyses et Josue. (AUG., quaest. 103 in Exod.) Josue non commemoratus in illis quatuor subito cum Moyse apparet, etc., usque 266Aad quod modo memoratur nondum vocabatur Jesus, sed quando scriptum est.

(BED.) Josue Christum nomine et actione significat; qui minister Moysi dicitur, quia Christus non venit legem solvere, sed adimplere (Matth. V). Moysi vestigia in omnibus sequitur, quia in cunctis quae scripsit Moyses, typice designatur, quasi comes individuus, si bene quaeritur, invenitur. Unde Joan. V: Si crederetis Moysi, forsitan crederetis et mihi.

(BED., de Tabernac., lib. I, cap. 1, tom. II.) Aaron ( אהרן ) mons fortitudinis, Hur ( חור ) interpretatur ignis vel lumen, etc., usque ad et ad dilectionem Conditoris animus inardescit.

VERS. 15.-- Cumque ascendisset Moyses, (BEDA., ibid.) Sicut mons altitudinem perfectionis, etc., usque 266Bad coelesti benedictione protegit.

VERS. 16.-- Et habitavit gloria Domini. (ID., ibid.) Non modo sublimitas, sed et nomen montis, etc., usque ad in septimo quiescere jubemur.

Tegens illum nube. (ID., ibid.) Sex diebus nube et gloria Domini tegitur, etc., usque ad ut ad sublimiora divinae visionis et collocutionis dona perveniret.

VERS. 17.-- Erat autem species, etc. (ID., ibid.) Quasi ignis ardens species gloriae Domini apparuit, etc., usque ad tametsi plene illos sequi non valeant.

VERS. 18.-- Ingressusque Moyses, etc. (ID., ibid.) Moyses medium nebulae ingressus ascendit in montem, etc., usque ad ut per eorum observantiam ad salutem veniant aeternam.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- And to Moses he said: Come up to the Lord, thou and Aaron, etc. (AUG., quaest. 85 in Exod., tom. III.) Up to this place the justifications of Scripture are understood, etc., as far as but the Latins say that those are justifications which the Greeks call δικαιώματα.

Thou and Aaron, etc. (STRAB.) Moses and Aaron and Nadab and Abiu. By these four are signified evangelical men, that is, those who follow the evangelical precepts. 265ABut by the seventy (which number consists of the number seven taken ten times, or of the number ten multiplied seven times) are signified those who, helped by the grace of the Holy Spirit, fulfilling the ten of the law, stretch toward the contemplation of the Creator. Or, according to the letter, those of whom it is said in Num. XI: Take unto thee seventy men, etc., among whom were Heldad and Meldad. [Vulg. Nm 11:16 Congrega mihi septuaginta viros de senibus Israel, "Gather unto me seventy men of the ancients of Israel"]

VERS. 2.-- And Moses alone shall come up to the Lord. (RAB.) First it is commanded that Moses and Aaron, Nadab and Abiu, and the seventy elders should come up to the Lord. And at once it is subjoined that Moses alone should come up to the Lord, and that the rest should not draw near, but adore afar off. Where we are morally instructed to weigh our own strength, and not to reach the citadel of contemplation beyond our power, but so far as the divine clemency 265Bcalls, and the rule of Scripture invites.

(GREG.) Moses, that he might receive the sublime precepts of God, climbed on high; and that he might penetrate inward things, he is hidden from outward tumults: because holy men, who are compelled by necessity to serve outward offices, always flee back studiously to the secret places of the heart, and there ascend the summit of their inmost thought, and receive the law as it were upon a mountain, while, the tumults of temporal actions being set aside, they search out upon the height of their contemplation the sentence of the supernal will.

VERS. 3-5.-- We will do (AUG., quaest. 95.) It is to be noted that again the people, etc., as far as they laid hold of justice.

VERS. 6-9.-- He took therefore, etc. [ID., quaest. 98.] 265CIt is to be noted that Moses now sacrificed for the first time, etc., as far as for that the decalogue was written on tables of stone appears afterwards.

Bowls. A cratera, or crater, is so called ἀπὸ τοῦ κρατεῖν, which is to hold, in that it is able to hold much wine.

VERS. 10, 11.-- Under his feet. God is not contained by place, nor does he set the members of a body in a posture, as it belongs to our body to sit, and to lie, to stand, and the like, which are of the body only. But God is a spirit. Therefore that he shows himself under a bodily appearance, or by signs bodily expressed, is not the appearing of his substance, which is what he himself is; rather the assumption of visible forms lies subject to all his power.

VERS. 12.-- Come up to me, etc. (BED., lib. I de 265DTabernac., cap. 11, tom. II.) Being about to speak of the figure of the tabernacle, etc., as far as that the Church might be designated as about to speak throughout the world with the tongues of all nations.

And I will give thee. (ID.) Like to this is that: Teaching them to observe all things whatsoever I have commanded you (Matth. XXVIII). Therefore neither Moses, nor the apostles, nor anyone whatever, ought to teach other things than those which the Lord taught and prescribed by the decree of his counsel to be observed, in which he willed that we should have eternal life; and just as nothing of these ought to be passed over, so it is needful to commit to the hearers all things which he commanded.

VERS. 13.-- Moses and Josue rose up. (AUG., quaest. 103 in Exod.) Josue, not mentioned among those four, suddenly appears with Moses, etc., as far 266Aas that at the time now recorded he was not yet called Jesus, but when it was written.

(BED.) Josue signifies Christ in name and in action; who is called the minister of Moses, because Christ came not to destroy the law, but to fulfil (Matth. V). He follows the footsteps of Moses in all things, because in all that Moses wrote he is typically designated, and, as an inseparable companion, is found if he be rightly sought. Whence John V: If you did believe Moses, you would perhaps believe me also.

(BED., de Tabernac., lib. I, cap. 1, tom. II.) Aaron ( אהרן ) is interpreted the mountain of strength, Hur ( חור ) fire or light, etc., as far as and the mind burns toward the love of the Creator.

VERS. 15.-- And when Moses was gone up, (BEDA., ibid.) As the mountain the height of perfection, etc., as far 266Bas he protects with heavenly blessing.

VERS. 16.-- And the glory of the Lord dwelt. (ID., ibid.) Not the loftiness only, but the very name of the mountain, etc., as far as on the seventh we are bidden to rest.

Covering it with a cloud. (ID., ibid.) For six days he is covered with the cloud and with the glory of the Lord, etc., as far as that he might come through to the loftier gifts of the divine vision and converse.

VERS. 17.-- And the appearance was, etc. (ID., ibid.) The appearance of the glory of the Lord appeared as a burning fire, etc., as far as although they are not able to follow them fully.

VERS. 18.-- And Moses entering in, etc. (ID., ibid.) Moses, having entered the midst of the cloud, went up into the mountain, etc., as far as that by the observance of them they may come to eternal salvation.

CAPUT XXV.

  • 266C

VERS. 2.-- Loquere filiis Israel ut tollant mihi primitias, etc. (BED., de Tabernac., lib. I, cap. III, tom. II.) Primitias bonorum Deo offerimus, cum bonum quod agimus, totum divinae gratiae tribuentes, et toto corde profitentes quod nec initium bonae actionis vel cogitationis nisi a Domino possumus habere; mala vero semper a nobis diabolo instigante et coepta et consummata, nec nisi Domino donante laxanda.

VERS. 3-6.-- Haec sunt autem quae accipere debetis, etc. (ID., ibid.) Cuncta quae sibi Dominus ad faciendum sanctuarium materialiter offerri praecepit, nos quoque, qui spirituales filii Israel, id est imitatores 266DDeum videntis populi esse cupimus, spiritualiter offerre debemus: ut per has oblationes voluntarias ipsi sanctuarium in nobis faciamus, ut ipse habitare in nostro corde et sibi mansionem consecrare dignetur.

VERS. 7.-- Lapides onychinos, etc. (ID., ibid.) Haec offerimus, etc., usque ad addita bonis operibus martyrii merita signantur.

VERS. 8.-- Facientque mihi sanctuarium. (ID., ibid.) Ostendit Dominus Moysi tabernaculum in monte, etc., usque ad praeclaram multitudinem sanctarum animarum recepit.

VERS. 9-11.-- Juxta omnem similitudinem, etc. (ID., ibid.) Imitari debemus angelos diligendo Deum 267Aet proximum, etc., usque ad et post hanc vitam in illud tabernaculum, quod imitaris, introducat.

Omnium vasorum. (BED.) Vasa tabernaculi, singulae personae angelorum, ex quibus Jerusalem, mater omnium nostrum, in vera pace et unitate consistit.

Sicque facietis illud. Arcam, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 4.) Arca fieri praecipitur, etc., usque ad sed in similitudine carnis peccati venit.

Cujus longitudo (ID.) Quaeritur cujus quantitatis sit iste cubitus, etc., usque ad unde patet quia illum designat cubitum quem duo palmi faciunt.

Latitudo. (ID., ibid.) Unius cubiti latitudo pro dispensatione Dominicae charitatis, etc., usque ad ubi eum toto corde diligamus, intremus.

267B(ID.) Mystice. Longitudo arcae longanimitatem Christi, etc., usque ad quia tarditatem discipulorum longanimiter ferebat.

Altitudo, cubitum, etc. (ID., ibid.) Habet arca cubitum in altitudine, etc., et usque ad hoc tantillum non nisi ab illo habemus.

VERS. 12-15.-- Et quatuor circulos, etc. (GREG. Past., cap. 11, tom. III.) Arca, Ecclesia, etc., usque ad quae in vobis est, etc.

Duo circuli. (BEDA, ut supra. ) Quia in figura quatuor animalium, etc., usque ad quae ad futurae vitae gloriam pertinent expresserunt.

VERS. 16.-- Testificationem quam dabo. (ID., ibid.) De qua Apostolus, etc., usque ad judicem omnium docuit saeculorum.

267CVERS. 17.-- Propitiatorium. (ID.) Propitiatorium tabula erat aurea ejusdem mensurae cujus arca; sic dicta quia ibi Deus Moysi apparens, et cum eo loquens, propitiabatur populo, et misericordiam significat, unde bene super arcam erat, quia misericordia ejus super omnia opera ejus.

VERS. 18-21.-- Duos quoque cherubim, etc. (ID.) Propter societatem angelicae pacis, quae minus quam inter duos haberi non potest. Cherubim autem angelicam militiam significat: angeli vero summa concordia et quiete Christum contemplantur; unde versis ad invicem vultibus propitiatorium respiciunt, quia illam beatitudinem se habere Dei gratia intelligunt.

(GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.) Duo cherubim, qui plenitudo 267Dscientiae dicuntur, etc., usque ad si aliud promitteret quod aliud negaret.

(BEDA, ibid.) Numero singulari cherub, כרוב plurali autem cherubim כרובים dicitur, etc., usque ad bene expansis alis ad volandum aptatis designantur.

VERS. 22.-- Inde praecipiam, etc., super propitiatorium. (ID., ibid.) Super propitiatorio, etc., usque ad unde Habacuc ait: In medio duorum animalium innotesces (Habac. III).

(BED., ibid.) Potest in arca Ecclesia figurari, etc., usque ad quo ipsa in fundamento summae veritatis stabilita consistunt.

(BED., ibid.) Mensa de lignis Setim, etc., usque ad 268Aunde Cant. II: Introduxit me rex in cellam vinariam, et ordinavit in me charitatem.

VERS. 23.-- In altitudine cubitum, etc. (BED., ibid.) Quod altitudo cubitum et semissem habet, etc., usque ad non per plenum cubitum figuratur.

VERS. 23.-- Faciesque illi labium., etc. (BED., ibid.) Labium aureum fit in mensa per circumitum etc., usque ad pro diversitate meritorum, gloria singulorum.

VERS. 24.-- Coronam, etc., et super illam alteram coronam. (BED., ibid.) Corona aurea additur, cum per Evangelium his qui mandata custodiunt, etc., usque ad quod ei sublimior gloria in receptione corporum servetur.

VERS. 26.-- Per singulos, etc. (BED., ibid.) Mensa 268Btabernaculi quatuor pedes habet, etc., usque ad intrent in regnum coelorum.

VERS. 27.-- Ut mittantur vectes per eos. (ID., ibid.) Per circulos mittuntur vectes ad subvehendam mensam, etc., usque ad Domini mensam cum panibus et vasis in quatuor circulis portant.

VERS. 29.-- Parabis et acetabula ac phialas. (ID., ibid.) Varia vasa quae fiunt ad offerenda libamina, etc., usque ad unde: Quis putas est fidelis dispensator et prudens (Luc. XII)?

VERS. 29.-- Parabis acetabula, etc. (GREG., hom. 6 in Evang., tom. II.) Quid per phialas et cyathos nisi mensura praedicationis accipitur? etc., usque ad sed etiam parva et subtilia, quae quasi per gustum notitiam praestent.

268C(IDEM.) Alius doctrina veritatis plenus auditores inebriat, et quasi phialam porrigit: Alius explere quod sentit non valet, sed etiam quia utcumque denuntiat, per cyathum gustum praebet. Qui ergo per doctrinam sapientiae ministrare phialas non possunt, quantum pro divina largitate sufficiunt, propinent proximis cyathos verbi.

VERS. 30.-- Et pones super mensam panes propositionis in conspectu meo semper. Josephus dicit duodecim panes ex simila fieri, singulos ex duabus decimis, qui Sabbato super mensam sex hinc et sex inde ponebantur: super quemque patena aurea cum pugillo thuris, et sic servabantur usque ad aliud Sabbatum, in quo thus cremabatur; panes vero sacerdotes comedebant calidos cum thure reponentes.

268DEt pones super mensam, etc. (BED. ubi supra. ) Bene panes propositionis vocantur, quia semper debet patere sermo salutis cunctis fidelibus, nec unquam deesse oportet in ecclesia verbum solatii: quod Dominus per praecones veritatis mundo proponens in conspectu suo apparere, et usque in finem his qui esuriunt et sitiunt justitiam incessanter abundare voluit.

VERS. 31.-- Facies et candelabrum, etc. (GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.) Candelabrum Christus est in quo natura humanitatis fulsit lumine deitatis, etc., usque ad scyphi ergo et sphaerulae ad laborem pertinent, lilia ad retributionem.

Facies et candelabrum ductile de auro mundissimo. 269A(BEDA, ibid.) Candelabrum tabernaculi, etc., usque ad et claritatem verbi omnibus praedicando et faciendo ostendit.

Et calamos, scyphos et sphaerulas. (ID., ibid.) Scilicet praedicatores de hastili procedentes, etc., usque ad quae animabus sanctis, id est, floribus vernat aeternis.

VERS. 32.-- Sex calami, etc. (ID., ibid.) Senarius in quo factus est mundus, etc., usque ad unde Paulus: Gratia Domini nostri Jesu Christi et charitas Dei, et communicatio sancti Spiritus, sit semper cum omnibus vobis.

Tres ex uno. (ID., ibid.) Tres calami ex uno latere, etc., usque ad visione ejus fruetur.

(ID., ubi supra ). Fiunt scyphi in candelabro ad nucis modum, etc., usque ad quia continentes illius 269Btemporis tres ordines sanctorum eadem distinctione temporum habuerunt.

VERS. 33.-- In nucis. (ID.) Nux Ecclesiam significat, unde: Descendit in hortum nucum. Nux enim dulcem fructum habet intus, qui nisi fracta testa non ostenditur foris: sic sanctorum vita suavitatem gratiae in corde conservat, quae non cognoscitur exterius nisi, soluto carnis domicilio animae, in coelesti luce sese conspiciant.

(ID.) Tertius similiter calamus tres scyphos et sphaerulas, et tria habet lilia: quia multi conjugati ante legem, sub lege et sub prophetis erant, qui verbum Domini audire, et viam ejus currere et bonorum operum praemia exspectare gauderent.

(ID.) Item ex alio latere primus calamus, secundus 269Cet tertius, tres scyphos et sphaerulas; tria quoque habebat lilia: quia in utroque Testamento in doctoribus, in continentibus, et in conjugatis Deo devotis tres fuerunt ordines temporum distantia. In primitiva scilicet ecclesia de Israel, in nostra electione de gentibus, in ultima collectione reliquiarum Israel, qui omnes suo tempore verbo vitae inebriati, in via pacis properare, candorem perpetuae lucis videre desiderant.

VERS. 34.-- In ipso autem candelabro, etc. (ID., ubi supra. ) Diximus ipsum candelabrum, etc., usque ad sed innovata per spiritum corda capiunt.

VERS. 35.-- Sphaerulaeque per singulos et lilia. Sphaerulae sub duobus calamis, etc.

(ID., ibid.) Sunt per singulos scyphos sphaerulae 269Dsimul et lilia, etc., usque ad unde: Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera ejus amplexabitur me.

(ID., ibid.) Scyphi, sphaerulae et lilia sub duobus calamis sunt, etc., usque ad omnis plenitudo divinitatis corporaliter.

VERS. 36.-- Et sphaerulae igitur et calami. (ID., ibid.) Omne opus candelabri, etc., usque ad unde: In tribulatione dilatasti mihi, etc.

VERS. 37.-- Facies. (ID.) Lucernae septem, septem sunt dona Spiritus sancti, qui in Christo semper manserunt, et fidelibus secundum voluntatem ejus distributa sunt: hae super candelabrum ponuntur, quia requievit super eum Spiritus sapientiae et intellectus (Isai. XI).

270AUt luceant, etc. (ID., ibid.) Lucernae candelabri ex adverso tabernaculum illustrabant, cum Christus plenus gratia et veritate omnibus tribuebat de sua plenitudine; mansuetis et pauperibus verbum Evangelii committens; poenitentibus medelam indulgentiae conferens. Nunc tempus esse parcendi in futuro universalis judicii tempus affirmans.

VERS. 38.-- Emunctoria quoque, et ubi quae. (ID., ibid.) Divina verba, quibus manifeste signatur in plurimis non legis litteram, sed spiritualem sensum esse servandum: unde: Si circumcidimini, Christus nihil vobis proderit.

(ID., ibid.) Emunctorium, et vasa, ubi quae emuncta sunt exstinguuntur, etc., usque ad dicens: Sive prophetiae evacuabuntur, sive linguae cessabunt, etc.

270BEmunctoria quoque, etc. (ID., ibid.) Sunt quaedam in Scripturis praecepta, etc., usque ad et alibi: Ecce nova facio omnia.

VERS. 39.-- Omne. (ID., ibid.) Hoc est corpus Christi cum ipso capite, scilicet omnes electi, etc., usque ad quia merito scelerum in abyssum gravantis poenae decidunt.

VERS. 40.-- Inspice. (ID., ibid.) Ostensum est Mosi in monte exemplar candelabri quod faceret, etc., usque ad exemplar auditoribus exsecutione operis et verbo doctrinae ostendit.

CHAPTER XXV.

  • 266C

VERS. 2.-- Speak to the children of Israel, that they bring me firstfruits, etc. (BED., de Tabernac., lib. I, cap. III, tom. II.) We offer to God the firstfruits of our goods when, ascribing wholly to divine grace the good that we do, we profess with our whole heart that we cannot have even the beginning of a good action or thought save from the Lord; but that evils are always both begun and consummated by us at the instigation of the devil, and are not to be loosed save by the Lord's gift.

VERS. 3-6.-- And these are the things you must take, etc. (ID., ibid.) All the things which the Lord commanded to be offered materially for the making of the sanctuary, we also, who desire to be spiritual children of Israel, that is, imitators 266Dof the people that sees God, ought to offer spiritually: that by these voluntary oblations we may ourselves make a sanctuary within us, so that he may vouchsafe to dwell in our heart and to consecrate a habitation to himself.

VERS. 7.-- Onyx stones, etc. (ID., ibid.) These we offer, etc., as far as are signified the merits of martyrdom added to good works.

VERS. 8.-- And they shall make me a sanctuary. (ID., ibid.) The Lord showed Moses the tabernacle on the mountain, etc., as far as it received the glorious multitude of holy souls.

VERS. 9-11.-- According to all the likeness, etc. (ID., ibid.) We ought to imitate the angels by loving God 267Aand our neighbour, etc., as far as and after this life may bring thee into that tabernacle which thou imitatest.

Of all the vessels. (BED.) The vessels of the tabernacle are the several persons of the angels, of whom Jerusalem, the mother of us all, consists in true peace and unity.

And thus you shall make it. An ark, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 4.) The ark is commanded to be made, etc., as far as but he came in the likeness of sinful flesh.

The length whereof (ID.) It is asked of what size this cubit is, etc., as far as whence it appears that it designates that cubit which two palms make.

The breadth. (ID., ibid.) The breadth of one cubit for the dispensation of the Lord's charity, etc., as far as where we may love him with our whole heart, let us enter.

267B(ID.) Mystically. The length of the ark the long-suffering of Christ, etc., as far as because he bore with long-suffering the slowness of the disciples.

The height, a cubit, etc. (ID., ibid.) The ark has a cubit in height, etc., and as far as this little we have not save from him.

VERS. 12-15.-- And four rings, etc. (GREG. Past., cap. 11, tom. III.) The ark, the Church, etc., as far as which is in you, etc.

Two rings. (BEDA, ut supra. ) Because in the figure of the four living creatures, etc., as far as they expressed the things which pertain to the glory of the life to come.

VERS. 16.-- The testimony which I will give. (ID., ibid.) Of which the Apostle, etc., as far as he taught the judge of all ages.

267CVERS. 17.-- A propitiatory. (ID.) The propitiatory was a golden board of the same measure as the ark; so called because there God, appearing to Moses and speaking with him, was made propitious to the people; and it signifies mercy, whence it was fittingly above the ark, because his mercy is above all his works.

VERS. 18-21.-- Two cherubim also, etc. (ID.) Because of the fellowship of angelic peace, which cannot be had among fewer than two. Now the cherubim signify the angelic host; and the angels contemplate Christ in the highest concord and quiet; whence, with faces turned toward one another, they look upon the propitiatory, because they understand that they have that beatitude by the grace of God.

(GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.) The two cherubim, which are called the fulness 267Dof knowledge, etc., as far as if he promised one thing while he denied another.

(BEDA, ibid.) In the singular number it is called cherub, כרוב but in the plural cherubim, כרובים etc., as far as they are well designated by wings spread out and fitted for flying.

VERS. 22.-- Thence will I give orders, etc., over the propitiatory. (ID., ibid.) Over the propitiatory, etc., as far as whence Habacuc says: In the midst of two living creatures thou shalt be made known (Habac. III). [Vulg. Hab 3:2 in medio annorum notum facies, "in the midst of the years thou shalt make it known"; the whole gloss rests on the two living creatures]

(BED., ibid.) In the ark the Church can be figured, etc., as far as wherein they themselves stand established upon the foundation of the highest truth.

(BED., ibid.) The table of setim wood, etc., as far as 268Awhence Cant. II: The king brought me into the wine cellar, and he set in order charity in me. [Vulg. Ct 2:4 Introduxit me in cellam vinariam, ordinavit in me caritatem, without rex, "he brought me in"]

VERS. 23.-- In height a cubit, etc. (BED., ibid.) That the height has a cubit and a half, etc., as far as it is not figured by a full cubit.

VERS. 23.-- And thou shalt make for it a lip., etc. (BED., ibid.) A golden lip is made on the table round about, etc., as far as according to the diversity of merits, the glory of each. [Vulg. these words stand at Ex 25:24; Migne's address repeats VERS. 23]

VERS. 24.-- A crown, etc., and over it another crown. (BED., ibid.) A golden crown is added, since through the Gospel to those who keep the commandments, etc., as far as that a loftier glory is kept for it in the receiving back of their bodies.

VERS. 26.-- For every one, etc. (BED., ibid.) The table 268Bof the tabernacle has four feet, etc., as far as they may enter into the kingdom of heaven.

VERS. 27.-- That the bars may be put through them. (ID., ibid.) Through the rings the bars are put for carrying the table, etc., as far as they carry the table of the Lord with the loaves and the vessels in four rings.

VERS. 29.-- Thou shalt prepare also dishes and bowls. (ID., ibid.) The various vessels which are made for offering libations, etc., as far as whence: Who, thinkest thou, is the faithful and prudent steward (Luc. XII)?

VERS. 29.-- Thou shalt prepare dishes, etc. (GREG., hom. 6 in Evang., tom. II.) What is understood by the bowls and the cups but the measure of preaching? etc., as far as but also small and subtle things, which afford knowledge as it were by a taste.

268C(IDEM.) One man, full of the doctrine of truth, inebriates his hearers, and as it were holds out a bowl; another is not strong enough to fill out what he perceives, yet, because he does declare it after some fashion, affords by a cup a taste. Those therefore who cannot minister bowls through the doctrine of wisdom, let them, so far as they suffice by the divine bounty, pour out to their neighbours cups of the word.

VERS. 30.-- And thou shalt set upon the table the loaves of proposition in my sight always. Josephus says that twelve loaves were made of fine flour, each of two tenths, which on the Sabbath were set upon the table, six on this side and six on that: upon each one a golden dish with a handful of incense, and thus they were kept until another Sabbath, on which the incense was burned; but the priests ate the loaves warm, putting back the incense.

268DAnd thou shalt set upon the table, etc. (BED. ubi supra. ) They are rightly called the loaves of proposition, because the word of salvation ought always to lie open to all the faithful, nor ought the word of comfort ever to be wanting in the Church: which word the Lord, proposing it to the world through the heralds of truth, willed to appear in his sight and to abound unceasingly, even unto the end, for those who hunger and thirst after justice.

VERS. 31.-- Thou shalt make also a candlestick, etc. (GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.) The candlestick is Christ, in whom the nature of humanity shone with the light of the Godhead, etc., as far as the goblets therefore and the little spheres pertain to labour, the lilies to reward.

Thou shalt make also a candlestick of beaten work of the purest gold. 269A(BEDA, ibid.) The candlestick of the tabernacle, etc., as far as and by preaching and by doing he showed the brightness of the word to all.

And the branches, the goblets and the little spheres. (ID., ibid.) Namely, preachers proceeding from the shaft, etc., as far as which blooms for holy souls, that is, for eternal flowers.

VERS. 32.-- Six branches, etc. (ID., ibid.) The number six, in which the world was made, etc., as far as whence Paul: The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ, and the charity of God, and the communication of the Holy Spirit, be always with you all. [Vulg. 2Cor 13:13 sit cum omnibus vobis, without semper, "be with you all"]

Three out of one. (ID., ibid.) Three branches out of one side, etc., as far as he shall enjoy the vision of him.

(ID., ubi supra ). The goblets are made on the candlestick after the manner of a nut, etc., as far as because the continent of that 269Btime had three orders of saints by the same distinction of times.

VERS. 33.-- In the manner of a nut. (ID.) The nut signifies the Church, whence: He went down into the garden of nuts. [Vulg. Ct 6:10 Descendi in hortum nucum, "I went down into the garden of nuts"; Migne's third person moves the words out of the Bride's mouth] For the nut has a sweet fruit within, which is not shown without unless the shell be broken: so the life of the saints keeps the sweetness of grace in the heart, which is not known outwardly, save when, the dwelling of the flesh being loosed, they behold themselves in the heavenly light.

(ID.) The third branch likewise has three goblets and little spheres, and three lilies: because many married persons were before the law, under the law and under the prophets, who rejoiced to hear the word of the Lord, and to run his way, and to look for the rewards of good works.

(ID.) Likewise from the other side the first branch, the second 269Cand the third, three goblets and little spheres; three lilies also it had: because in both Testaments, among the doctors, among the continent, and among the married that are devoted to God, there were three orders by a distance of times. Namely in the primitive Church out of Israel, in our election out of the Gentiles, in the last gathering of the remnants of Israel: all of whom, inebriated in their own time with the word of life, desire to hasten in the way of peace, and to see the whiteness of perpetual light.

VERS. 34.-- And in the candlestick itself, etc. (ID., ubi supra. ) We have said that the candlestick itself, etc., as far as but hearts renewed through the Spirit receive them.

VERS. 35.-- And little spheres under every one, and lilies. Little spheres under two branches, etc.

(ID., ibid.) There are under the several goblets little spheres 269Dtogether with lilies, etc., as far as whence: His left hand under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me.

(ID., ibid.) The goblets, the little spheres and the lilies are under two branches, etc., as far as all the fulness of the Godhead corporeally.

VERS. 36.-- The little spheres therefore and the branches. (ID., ibid.) The whole work of the candlestick, etc., as far as whence: In tribulation thou hast enlarged me, etc.

VERS. 37.-- Thou shalt make. (ID.) The seven lamps are the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, which always remained in Christ, and are distributed to the faithful according to his will: these are set upon the candlestick, because the Spirit of wisdom and understanding rested upon him (Isai. XI).

270AThat they may give light, etc. (ID., ibid.) The lamps of the candlestick lighted up the tabernacle over against them, when Christ, full of grace and truth, bestowed on all out of his fulness; committing the word of the Gospel to the meek and the poor, conferring on the penitent the remedy of pardon, affirming that now is the time of sparing, in the time to come the time of the universal judgment.

VERS. 38.-- Snuffers also, and where the things. (ID., ibid.) The divine words, by which it is manifestly signified that in very many places not the letter of the law but the spiritual sense is to be kept: whence: If you be circumcised, Christ shall profit you nothing.

(ID., ibid.) The snuffers, and the vessels where the things that are snuffed off are extinguished, etc., as far as saying: Whether prophecies shall be made void, or tongues shall cease, etc.

270BSnuffers also, etc. (ID., ibid.) There are certain precepts in the Scriptures, etc., as far as and elsewhere: Behold, I make all things new.

VERS. 39.-- The whole. (ID., ibid.) This is the body of Christ together with the head itself, that is, all the elect, etc., as far as because by the desert of their crimes they fall down into the abyss of oppressing punishment.

VERS. 40.-- Look. (ID., ibid.) There was shown to Moses on the mountain the pattern of the candlestick which he should make, etc., as far as he showed the pattern to his hearers by the performance of the work and by the word of doctrine.

CAPUT XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- Tabernaculum vero ita et fiet, etc. (BED., lib. II de Tabernac., cap. 3, tom. II.) Tabernaculum, 270Cquod fecit Moyses in solitudine, et templum quod fecit Salomon in Jerusalem, etc., usque ad et copula divinae et propriae dilectionis jungantur.

(ID., ubi supra. ) Potest figura utriusque sanctuarii ita distingui, etc., usque ad mirabili figurarum varietate depingit.

De bysso retorta et hyacintho ac purpura coccoque bis tincto variatas. (ID., ubi supra, cap. 2.) In his quatuor cortinarum coloribus, etc., usque ad purpura sanguinei coloris et de conchyliorum sanguine tincta devota Deo corda figurat, et pro Deo mori parata.

VERS. 2.-- Longitudo cortinae unius habebit viginti octo cubitos, latitudo quatuor, etc. (ID., ibid.) 270DSi ab uno usque ad septem omnes in ordine numeros pleno ordine numeraveris, usque ad ut per fidem et opera bona ad requiem tendat.

Unius mensurae, etc. (ID., ibid.) Etsi variante pictura cortinae differebant, una tamen erant omnes longitudinis et latitudinis mensura temperatae, quia etsi donationes habent electi differentes, unus tamen Dominus, una fides, unum baptisma, unus Deus et Pater omnium.

(ID., ubi supra. ) Narrat Josephus, etc., usque ad quasi una decem cortinarum connexione tabernaculum decoratur.

VERS. 4.-- Ansulas hyacinthinas in lateribus, etc. (ID., ibid.) Ansulis hyacinthinis cortinae invicem copulantur, 271Aetc., usque ad et in ea se perpetuo mansuros confidunt.

(BEDA, ibid.) Praeceptum est quoque fieri fimbrias per angulos palliorum, etc., usque ad a quatuor ventis collecti, indivisibili societate conjungemur.

VERS. 5.-- Quinquagenas, etc. (ID., ibid.) In quinquagesimo anno, etc., usque ad et in cujus perceptione veraciter quiescitur et gaudetur, recte figuratur.

(ID., ibid.) Quinquaginta ansulas habent cortinae, quia dono Spiritu sancti agitur, ut in societate pacis, quae est vinculum perfectionis, sibi invicem copulentur electi: futurae quoque spes et memoria societatis et pacis, quae in hac vita habetur, temporibus vel locis discretos una facit pietate concordes.

271B(ID., ibid.) Cortinae in utraque parte habent ansas, etc., usque ad cum justi concordi virtutum qualitate foederantur.

Facies et quinquaginta circulos. (ID., ibid.) Hoc plenius explicatur, cum dicitur, etc., usque ad imo ex omnibus electis Christi Ecclesia perficiatur.
