VERS. 7.-- Facies et saga, etc. (ID., ibid.) Hoc in sequentibus ita reperitur: Fecit et saga undecim de pilis caprarum, etc. Saga, quibus operitur tabernaculum, etc., usque ad dum quasi saga foris tempestates tolerant.

Saga cilicina grossas hominum mentes significant, quae occulto Dei judicio quamvis dure praeferuntur, quia servire curis temporalibus non timent, ut 271Ctentationum ventos et pluvias portent.

Facies et saga, etc. (ID., ibid.) Cilicium poenitentium est, etc., usque ad quorum tamen corda quantae sint perfectionis declaratur, cum dicitur: Longitudo sagi unius, etc.

(ID., ibid.) Saga cilicina, vel de pilis caprarum, etc., usque ad nec apostolicam gratiam percipiant.

(AUG., quaest. 108 in Exod.) Undecim sunt vela capillacea, etc., usque ad et quorum tecta sunt peccata.

Ad operiendum, scilicet ne interiora tenera clara qualibet adversitate turbentur, alioquin nec byssus fulget, nec coccus coruscat, nec hyacinthus caeruleo colore decoratur: non debent ergo qui in Ecclesiae 271Dsinu proficiunt praepositos despicere, si vacare eos rebus exterioribus vident.

VERS. 8.-- Longitudo sagi unius. (BEDA, lib. II de Tabernac., cap. 3, tom. II.) Longitudo habet denarium numerum, etc., usque ad qui tamen secundum humanae perfectionis modum inter homines sublimantur.

Aequa erit mensura. (ID., ibid.) Quia una est fides in qua tota salvatur Ecclesia, etc., usque ad ubi solida parietis firmitas aberat.

(ID., ibid.) Intuere etiam mensuram cortinarum, etc., usque ad Haec de cortinis.

(ID., ibid.) Saga tricenos habet cubitos longitudinis, etc., usque ad eo quod sint duces sequentium domini gregum.

272AVERS. 9.-- Sextum sagum in fronte. Mystice vero in fronte tecti sextum sagum duplicatur, cum omnes, qui sanctam intrant Ecclesiam, ita fide et sacramento Dominicae passionis initiantur, ut hujus quoque imitatione semper sibi vivendum esse intelligant. Quasi enim in ingressu sextum sagum duplicatur, ut Dominicae passionis sacramenta consequamur et exemplis instruamur. De sacramentis ait Petrus: Qui regeneravit nos in spem vivam per resurrectionem Jesu Christi ex mortuis. Dicit et de imitatione passionis: Christo igitur passo in carne, et vos eadem cogitatione armamini.

VERS. 10.-- Facies et quinquaginta ansas in ora sagi unius. (BEDA, ibid.) Ut haec supra in cortinis exposuimus, etc., usque ad tanto auxilium proximi, 272Bquo confortetur, inquirit.

VERS. 11.-- Aeneas quibus jungantur ansae, ut unum ex omnibus operimentum, etc. (ID.) Bene autem fibulae sunt aeneae, de vocali, scilicet metallo factae, quia magnam vocem habet apud Deum humilis conscientia justorum. Unde pauper Davidicus, cum anxiatus esset et non in auribus hominum, sed in conspectu Dei orationem suam effunderet: Domine, (inquit) exaudi orationem meam, et clamor meus ad te veniat.

VERS. 12.-- Quod autem superfuerit, etc. (ID., ibid.) Haec ut plenius intelligantur, etc., usque ad decem rursus descensionis juxta orientalis parietis tabulas.

VERS. 13.-- Et cubitus. Quia cortinas uno cubito 272Ctransibant saga ab australi latere domus, altero a septentrionali, ideoque ad terram perveniebant, quia haec triginta cubitos longitudinis, illae duos minus habebant.

VERS. 14.-- Facies et operimentum. (STRAB.) Primum erat operimentum cortinarum ex bysso, purpura et cocco; secundum sagorum undecim; tertium ex rubricatis pellibus arietum; postremum erat hyacinthinarum pellium. Cortinae autem ita operiebant, ut ad latera descenderent, ad terram tamen non pertingerent; saga autem tectum et latera operiebant, et ad terram pertingebant. Pelles vero rubricatae vel ianthinae, tectum tantum operiebant, nec ad altera declinabant.

De pellibus, etc. (BED., ibid., cap. 4.) Arietes sunt 272Ddoctores, gregis Dominici duces. Unde: Afferte Domino, filii Dei, afferte, etc. Quasi, o angeli, huic officio mancipati, afferte Domino in coelos imitatores apostolorum. Hinc quoque sexta mansio populi de Aegypto egressi helin, id est arietes, nominatur, in qua erant duodecim fontes et septuaginta palmae, ut scilicet nomine et specie figuram apostolorum teneret.

Rubricatis. (ID., ibid.) Rubricantur pelles arietum ad operiendum tectum tabernaculi, etc., usque ad ne inter pressuras deficiant, muniunt

De ianthinis pellibus. (ID., ibid.) Hyacinthus coelestis est coloris, etc., usque ad id est coeli vitam imitantes.

VERS. 15.-- De lignis setim. (ID., ibid.) Hae tabulae 273Aapostolos et successores, per quorum sermonem Ecclesia dilatata est, significant, etc., usque ad nec translatum est nomen Hebraeum, quia vix apud alios invenitur hoc lignum.

VERS. 16.-- Singulae denos cubitos habent in longitudine, in latitudine cubitum ac semissem. (BEDA, ibid.) Longitudo tabularum altitudo est quae decem cubitos habebat, etc., usque ad: et ostendam illi salutare meum (Psal. XC).

VERS. 17.-- In lateribus. (ID., ibid.) Incastratur in lateribus tabularum, etc., usque ad sinu amoris suscipimus, et nos ab illis suscipi credimus.

VERS. 18.-- Quarum viginti erunt in latere meridiano. (ID., ubi supra, cap. 6.) Quanta fuerit longitudo tabernaculi, etc., usque ad ne quis sibi virtutes 273Bsine opere putet sufficere.

VERS. 19.-- Quibus quadraginta bases argenteas, etc. (ID., ibid.) Bases, quibus tabulae sustentabantur, verba sunt et libri legis et prophetarum, quibus Apostoli et evangelistae, quae scribere et praedicare volebant, vera esse et divina probabant. Unde: Tunc adimpletum est quod dictum est per prophetas. Et alibi: Hoc autem totum factum est, ut adimplerentur scripturae prophetarum (Matth. XXI). Et Petrus ait: Habemus firmiorem propheticum sermonem (II Petr. I).

(BED., GREG.) Per tabularum bases argenteas ordo prophetarum significatur: qui dum primi de Domini incarnatione locuti sunt, quasi bases a fundamento surrexerunt, et superpositae fabricae pondera 273Csustinuerunt, et tabulas, id est apostolicam praedicationem in mundo dilatatam, quasi firmi et fusiles sustinent: quia apostolorum vita praedicatione eorum instruitur, et auctoritate solidatur. Unde bases binae conjunctae singulis tabulis supponuntur: quia dum prophetae in verbis suis de Christi incarnatione concordant, sequentes praedicatores aedificant, ut quo a semetipsis non discrepant, illos robustius figant. Ex argento fundi jubentur. Argenti enim claritas ex usu servatur, sine usu in nigredinem vertitur; prophetarum vero dicta ante Christi adventum in usu spiritualis intelligentiae non erant, dum conspici per obscuritatem non poterant, et ideo quasi nigra permanebant: sed postquam Christus veniens ea ante oculos nostros incarnationis 273Dmanu tersit, quidquid lucis in eis latebat claruit, sensusque patrum praecedentium in usum dedit, quia verba rebus exposuit.

(BEDA.) Bene singulis tabulis binae bases supponuntur, ut consensus propheticae attestationis, in omnibus quae Apostoli dixere, monstretur. Vel binae per duos angulos subjiciuntur, ut bene suffultis angulis tota recte possit consistere tabula, quia omnis apostolici et evangelici sermonis in propheticis libris initium praesignatur, et finis omnis apostolorum et successorum vita ab initio fidei usque ad finem vitae praesentis mysticis veteris testamenti inserta est tabulis.

VERS. 20.-- In latere quoque secundo. Latus meridianum, quod vergit ad austrum, antiquam Dei 274Aplebem significat, quae dudum lucem scientiae legis accipiens, amore Dei fervebat. Latus secundum quod vergit ad Aquilonem, gentes significat, quae usque ad tempus incarnationis tenebris et frigore infidelitatis torpebant, de quarum vocatione dicitur: Dicam Aquiloni, Da: et Austro, Noli prohibere (Isa. XLIII). Quasi, dicam populo gentium diu sine fide algenti: De filiis tuis veniant ad fidem meae confessionis et amoris. Quin et plebi Israeliticae quae jam meae cognitionis lumine fruitur: Noli prohibere gentes in sortem electionis recipi.

Viginti tabulae. (BEDA.) Unius autem numeri, et mensurarum, et facturae, tabula utrumque latus habuit: quia una et eadem fides, spes et charitas, utrique populo per apostolos praedicatur, et ad terram 274Buterque regni coelestis promissam vocatur. De utroque generale praeceptum accipiunt: Euntes in mundum universum praedicate Evangelium omni creaturae (Marc. XVI), id est praeputio et circumcisioni. Et absque ulla differentia subinfertur: Qui crediderit et baptizatus fuerit salvus erit.

VERS. 21.-- Binae bases. Basis est fundamentum a Graeco βασεία, id est fundo. Per bases prophetae significantur, quorum auctoritati apostoli et omnes doctores innituntur. Binae autem dicuntur propter concordiam prophetarum, qui omnes unum senserunt.

VERS. 22.-- Ad occidentalem, etc. (BEDA, ibid.) Occidentalis plaga, etc., usque ad usque ad finem saeculi sunt in Ecclesia permansuri.

274CAd occidentalem vero plagam, etc. (ID., ibid.) Bene in occidentali plaga tabernaculum consummatur, etc., usque ad sed etiam pro malis a quibus liberavit.

(ID.) Bene plaga occidentalis, quae mare respicere dicta est, sex tabulis constat: quia in senario boni operis consummatio notatur: quia in illo mundi ornatum Dominus consummavit; in illo hominem in principio creavit; in illo genus humanum sua passione restauravit. Vel quia sunt sex aetates saeculi, quibus oportet nos bonis operibus perfici, ut ad aeternam et resurrectionis gloriam perveniamus.

VERS. 23.-- Et rursum alias duas. (ID.) Quia rursum aliae duae tabulae, exceptis sex primis, in angulum jubentur erigi post tergum tabernaculi, 274Dquae venientem ab orientali plaga parietem excipiunt, et occidentalis plagae parieti conjunguntur, ad remunerationem futurae vitae pertinet, quae post labores et tempora unius vitae secura vel securata est, divisa in sabbatismum, id est, requiem animarum post absolutionem corporum, et gloriam resurrectionis utrique populo communis nullo fine terminandam.

VERS. 24.-- Eruntque sibi conjunctae. (ID., ibid.) Quia omnis electorum vita una fide et charitate ad coelestia tendit, et in unum finem visionis divinae pervenit, et in una voce recti dogmatis omnis sanctorum praedicatorum sermo convenit. Recederet enim ab invicem compago tabularum si quod unus propheta aut Apostolus dixisset, alius negaret

275ADuabus quoque tabulis, etc. (BEDA, ibid.) Angulares tabulae tabulis parietum per omnia sunt conjunctae, etc., usque ad prophetae consona voce futura esse praedixerunt.

(ID., ibid.) Hic non dicitur mensura basium, etc., usque ad sed immobile secura quiete servaretur.

Facies et vectes de lignis, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 7.) Quinque vectes qui tabulas tabernaculi continent, etc., usque ad nec alia quam legis mandata subdidit.

VERS. 29.-- Ipsas quoque tabulas deaurabis (ID., ibid.) Tabulae deauratae tabernaculi praefulgent, etc., usque ad sed etiam supernae retributionis lucem legunt.

Quos operies laminis. (ID., ibid.) Laminis aureis vectes operiuntur, etc., usque ad cum spiritualis 275Bintelligentia mysteriis plena ostenditur.

VERS. 30.-- Eriges tabernaculum. (ID., ibid.) Exemplum tabernaculi, etc., usque ad opus animumque componunt.

VERS. 31.-- Facies et velum. (ID., ibid., cap. 8). Etiam juxta litteram pulchritudini coelestis visionis hoc congruit. Si enim decorem flammasque astrorum, multifariam nubium speciem et ipsam irim consideras, mille trahentem et varios averso sole colores, multo plures et pulchriores quam in velo tabernaculi in coelo picturas notabis.

Facies et velum, etc. (ID., ibid.) Velum hoc, usque ad ut appareat vultui Dei pro nobis.

Facies et velum de hyacintho et purpura coccoque bis tincto. (ID., ibid.) Hactenus de pariete meridiano, 275Cetc., usque ad averso sole colores.

VERS. 32.-- Quod appendes ante quatuor, etc. (ID., ibid.) Quatuor autem columnae, etc., usque ad hoc ibi non erit ubi nihil omnino mali.

Quae ipsae quidem deauratae. (ID., ibid.) Deauratae sunt columnae ante quas appenditur velum, quia positae intra velum coeli virtutes angelorum summae claritatis gratia vestitae sunt. Capita habent aurea, quia mentem, qua reguntur, praesentia divinae cognitionis et visionis habent illustratam.

(ID., ibid.) Habent columnae bases argenteas, etc., usque ad argento verborum nitor ostenditur.

(GREG., Moral. lib. XXVIII, cap. 6). Quatuor columnarum bases jubet esse argenteas, etc., usque ad evangelistarum dicta ore et opere portant.

275DVERS. 33.-- Inseretur autem velum, etc. (BED., ubi supra. ) Quia in aeterno consilio Divinitatis erat, etc., usque ad: in die judicii.

Intra quod pones arcam testimonii, etc. Intra hoc velum posita est arca testamenti, quia Christus paternorum arcanorum conscius, post passionem et resurrectionem coelos coelorum ascendens, sedet ad dexteram Patris.

Dividentur. Quia Ecclesia, quae ex angelis et hominibus constat, partim peregrinatur in infimis, partim regnat in supernis, adhuc cives suos, dirimente velo coeli, habet ab invicem segregatos.

VERS. 35.-- Mensamque extra velum, etc. (ID. ibid.) Mensa et candelabrum tabernaculi temporalia 276ADei beneficia significant, etc., usque ad testimonium perhibens ei.

VERS. 36.-- Facies et tentorium, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 9). Exposita narratione de velo, etc., usque ad quia continentia carnis et animi gaudebat.

(ID.) Erat ad litteram tabernaculum domus Deo consecrata, etc., usque ad latitudinis quinquaginta.

VERS. 37.-- Et quinque columnas. (ID., ibid.) Columnae quibus tentorium suspenditur, etc., usque ad nondum patefacta evangelica Scriptura.

Lignorum setim. (ID., ibid.) Columnae de lignis, etc., usque ad: sed Jesum Christum.

Quarum erunt capita aurea, etc., (ID., ibid.) Aurea capita Christum significant, etc., usque ad electorum 276Banimabus corruptione carentibus aedificatur.

CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- And the tabernacle shall so be made, etc. (BED., lib. II de Tabernac., cap. 3, tom. II.) [Vulg. Ex 26:1 Tabernaculum vero ita facies, "the tabernacle thou shalt make thus"; Migne's impersonal passive drops the command addressed to Moses] The tabernacle 270Cwhich Moses made in the wilderness, and the temple which Solomon made in Jerusalem, etc., as far as and they may be joined by the bond of divine and of proper love.

(ID., ubi supra. ) The figure of both sanctuaries can be distinguished thus, etc., as far as he depicts with a wonderful variety of figures.

Of twisted fine linen, and of hyacinth and purple, and scarlet twice dyed, diversified. (ID., ubi supra, cap. 2.) In these four colours of the curtains, etc., as far as the purple, of a bloody colour and dyed with the blood of shellfish, figures hearts devoted to God and ready to die for God.

VERS. 2.-- The length of one curtain shall have twenty-eight cubits, the breadth four, etc. (ID., ibid.) 270DIf from one up to seven thou number all the numbers in order, by a full order, as far as that through faith and good works it may tend toward rest.

Of one measure, etc. (ID., ibid.) Although the curtains differed by a varying embroidery, yet they were all tempered to one measure of length and breadth, because although the elect have differing gifts, yet there is one Lord, one faith, one baptism, one God and Father of all.

(ID., ubi supra. ) Josephus relates, etc., as far as the tabernacle is adorned as it were by the one connecting of ten curtains.

VERS. 4.-- Loops of hyacinth in the sides, etc. (ID., ibid.) By loops of hyacinth the curtains are coupled to one another, 271Aetc., as far as and they trust that they shall abide in it for ever.

(BEDA, ibid.) It was commanded also that fringes should be made at the corners of the cloaks, etc., as far as being gathered from the four winds, we shall be joined in an indivisible fellowship.

VERS. 5.-- Fifty each, etc. (ID., ibid.) In the fiftieth year, etc., as far as and in the receiving of which there is truly rest and joy, it is rightly figured.

(ID., ibid.) The curtains have fifty loops, because it is brought about by the gift of the Holy Spirit that the elect are coupled to one another in the fellowship of peace, which is the bond of perfection: and the hope also and the memory of that fellowship and peace to come, which is had in this life, makes those who are separated by times or by places to be of one accord in the same piety.

271B(ID., ibid.) The curtains have loops on either part, etc., as far as when the just are leagued together by a harmonious quality of virtues.

Thou shalt make also fifty rings. (ID., ibid.) This is more fully explained when it is said, etc., as far as nay rather, that out of all the elect the Church of Christ may be perfected.

VERS. 7.-- Thou shalt make also tent-cloths, etc. (ID., ibid.) This is found thus in what follows: He made also eleven tent-cloths of goats' hair, etc. The tent-cloths, with which the tabernacle is covered, etc., as far as while like tent-cloths they bear the storms without.

The tent-cloths of haircloth signify the coarse minds of men, which by the hidden judgment of God are preferred, however harshly, because they fear not to serve temporal cares, that they may 271Cbear the winds and rains of temptations.

Thou shalt make also tent-cloths, etc. (ID., ibid.) Haircloth belongs to penitents, etc., as far as yet how great is the perfection of their hearts is declared when it is said: The length of one tent-cloth, etc.

(ID., ibid.) The tent-cloths of haircloth, or of goats' hair, etc., as far as nor do they receive the apostolic grace.

(AUG., quaest. 108 in Exod.) There are eleven hairy veils, etc., as far as and whose sins are covered.

For a covering, namely lest the inner things, tender and bright, be troubled by any adversity whatever; for otherwise neither does the fine linen shine, nor the scarlet glitter, nor is the hyacinth adorned with its blue colour: those therefore who advance in the bosom of the Church 271Dought not to despise their superiors, if they see them taken up with outward things.

VERS. 8.-- The length of one tent-cloth. (BEDA, lib. II de Tabernac., cap. 3, tom. II.) The length has the number ten, etc., as far as who nevertheless, according to the measure of human perfection, are exalted among men.

The measure shall be equal. (ID., ibid.) Because there is one faith in which the whole Church is saved, etc., as far as where the solid firmness of a wall was wanting.

(ID., ibid.) Consider also the measure of the curtains, etc., as far as So much for the curtains.

(ID., ibid.) The tent-cloth has thirty cubits of length, etc., as far as in that they are leaders of the flocks that follow their lords.

272AVERS. 9.-- The sixth tent-cloth in the front. Mystically, however, in the front of the roof the sixth tent-cloth is doubled, when all who enter the holy Church are so initiated by faith and by the sacrament of the Lord's passion that they understand that they must always live by the imitation of it as well. For as it were at the entrance the sixth tent-cloth is doubled, that we may both obtain the sacraments of the Lord's passion and be instructed by his examples. Of the sacraments Peter says: Who hath regenerated us unto a lively hope, by the resurrection of Jesus Christ from the dead. And he says also of the imitation of the passion: Christ therefore having suffered in the flesh, be you also armed with the same thought.

VERS. 10.-- Thou shalt make also fifty loops in the edge of one tent-cloth. (BEDA, ibid.) As we have expounded these things above concerning the curtains, etc., as far as so much the more does he seek the help of his neighbour, 272Bwhereby he may be strengthened.

VERS. 11.-- Of brass, wherewith the loops may be joined, that of all there may be made one covering, etc. (ID.) Now the buckles are rightly of brass, made, that is, of a sounding metal, because the humble conscience of the just has a great voice with God. Whence the poor man of David, when he was in anguish and was pouring out his prayer not into the ears of men but in the sight of God: O Lord, (he says) hear my prayer, and let my cry come to thee.

VERS. 12.-- But that which shall remain over, etc. (ID., ibid.) That these things may be more fully understood, etc., as far as ten again of descent along the boards of the eastern wall.

VERS. 13.-- And a cubit. Because the tent-cloths passed beyond the curtains by one cubit 272Con the southern side of the house, and by another on the northern, and therefore reached to the ground, since these had thirty cubits of length and those two less.

VERS. 14.-- Thou shalt make also a covering. (STRAB.) The first covering was that of the curtains of fine linen, purple and scarlet; the second that of the eleven tent-cloths; the third of the reddened skins of rams; the last was of hyacinth-coloured skins. Now the curtains so covered that they came down at the sides, yet did not reach the ground; but the tent-cloths covered both roof and sides, and did reach the ground. But the reddened or violet skins covered the roof only, nor did they slope down to the other parts.

Of skins, etc. (BED., ibid., cap. 4.) The rams are 272Dthe doctors, the leaders of the Lord's flock. Whence: Bring to the Lord, O ye children of God, bring, etc. As if to say: O angels, appointed to this office, bring to the Lord into the heavens the imitators of the apostles. Hence also the sixth station of the people that went out of Egypt is named helin, that is, rams, in which there were twelve fountains and seventy palm trees, that so by name and by kind it might hold the figure of the apostles.

Reddened. (ID., ibid.) The skins of rams are reddened for covering the roof of the tabernacle, etc., as far as lest they fail amid oppressions, they fortify

Of violet skins. (ID., ibid.) The hyacinth is of a heavenly colour, etc., as far as that is, imitating the life of heaven.

VERS. 15.-- Of setim wood. (ID., ibid.) These boards 273Asignify the apostles and their successors, through whose discourse the Church was enlarged, etc., as far as nor was the Hebrew name translated, because this wood is scarcely found among other peoples.

VERS. 16.-- Every one shall have ten cubits in length, in breadth a cubit and a half. (BEDA, ibid.) The length of the boards is the height, which had ten cubits, etc., as far as: and I will show him my salvation (Psal. XC).

VERS. 17.-- In the sides. (ID., ibid.) It is mortised in the sides of the boards, etc., as far as we receive in the bosom of love, and believe that we are received by them.

VERS. 18.-- Of which twenty shall be on the south side. (ID., ubi supra, cap. 6.) How great the length of the tabernacle was, etc., as far as lest anyone think that virtues 273Bwithout work suffice him.

VERS. 19.-- For which thou shalt cast forty sockets of silver, etc. (ID., ibid.) The bases by which the boards were held up are the words and the books of the law and of the prophets, by which the apostles and evangelists proved that the things they willed to write and to preach were true and divine. Whence: Then was fulfilled that which was spoken by the prophets. And elsewhere: Now all this was done, that the scriptures of the prophets might be fulfilled (Matth. XXI). [Vulg. these words stand at Mt 26:56; the address Migne prints points to Mt 21:4, which reads ut adimpleretur quod dictum est per prophetam dicentem] And Peter says: We have the more firm prophetical word (II Petr. I).

(BED., GREG.) By the silver bases of the boards the order of the prophets is signified: who, when they first spoke of the Lord's incarnation, rose as it were as bases from the foundation, and sustained the weights of the fabric set above them, 273Cand they sustain the boards, that is, the apostolic preaching spread abroad in the world, as firm and molten bases: because the life of the apostles is instructed by their preaching, and is made solid by their authority. Whence two bases joined together are set under each board: because while the prophets agree in their words concerning the incarnation of Christ, they build up the preachers that follow, so that, in the point where they do not differ among themselves, they may fix those others the more strongly. They are commanded to be cast of silver. For the brightness of silver is preserved by use, and without use turns to blackness; but the sayings of the prophets, before the coming of Christ, were not in the use of spiritual understanding, since they could not be beheld through their obscurity, and therefore they remained as it were black: but after Christ, coming, wiped them before our eyes with the hand of his incarnation, 273Dwhatever of light lay hidden in them shone forth, and he gave the understanding of the preceding fathers into use, because he expounded the words by the things.

(BEDA.) Rightly are two bases set under each board, that the consent of the prophetic attestation in all the things which the apostles said may be shown. Or they are put beneath, two by two, at the two corners, that, the corners being well propped, the whole board may be able to stand upright: because the beginning of all apostolic and evangelical discourse is prefigured in the prophetic books, and the end of the whole life of the apostles and of their successors, from the beginning of faith unto the end of the present life, is inserted in the mystical boards of the old testament.

VERS. 20.-- On the second side also. The southern side, which looks toward the south wind, signifies the ancient people of God, 274Awhich, long ago receiving the light of the knowledge of the law, glowed with the love of God. The second side, which looks toward the North, signifies the Gentiles, who until the time of the incarnation were torpid in the darkness and cold of unbelief; of whose calling it is said: I will say to the North: Give up; and to the South: Keep not back (Isa. XLIII). As if to say: I will say to the people of the Gentiles, long benumbed without faith: Let there come of thy sons to the faith of my confession and of my love. Yea and to the people of Israel, which already enjoys the light of my knowledge: Keep not back the Gentiles from being received into the lot of election.

Twenty boards. (BEDA.) Now the board on either side was of one number, and of one measure, and of one making: because one and the same faith, hope and charity is preached to either people through the apostles, and to the promised land 274Bof the heavenly kingdom either people is called. Of both they receive a general precept: Going into the whole world, preach the Gospel to every creature (Marc. XVI), that is, to uncircumcision and to circumcision. And without any difference it is subjoined: He that believeth and is baptized shall be saved.

VERS. 21.-- Two sockets. A base is a foundation, from the Greek βασεία, that is, I found. By the bases the prophets are signified, upon whose authority the apostles and all doctors lean. And they are called two because of the concord of the prophets, who all thought one thing.

VERS. 22.-- Toward the west, etc. (BEDA, ibid.) The western quarter, etc., as far as they are to remain in the Church unto the end of the world.

274CBut toward the western quarter, etc. (ID., ibid.) Rightly is the tabernacle completed in the western quarter, etc., as far as but also for the evils from which he delivered them.

(ID.) Rightly does the western quarter, which is said to look toward the sea, consist of six boards: because in the number six the consummation of a good work is marked; because in it the Lord consummated the adorning of the world; in it he created man in the beginning; in it he restored the human race by his passion. Or because there are six ages of the world, in which we must be perfected by good works, that we may come to the eternal glory and to the glory of the resurrection.

VERS. 23.-- And again two others. (ID.) That again two other boards, besides the first six, are commanded to be raised up at the corner behind the back of the tabernacle, 274Dwhich receive the wall coming from the eastern quarter and are joined to the wall of the western quarter, pertains to the reward of the life to come, which after the labours and the times of one life is secure, or made secure, being divided into a sabbath-rest, that is, the rest of souls after they are released from bodies, and the glory of the resurrection common to either people, to be ended by no end.

VERS. 24.-- And they shall be joined to one another. (ID., ibid.) [Vulg. Ex 26:24 Eruntque conjunctae a deorsum usque sursum, "and they shall be joined together from beneath unto the top"; Migne's sibi makes the joining mutual] Because the whole life of the elect tends by one faith and charity toward the things of heaven, and comes to the one end of the divine vision, and in one voice of right doctrine all the discourse of the holy preachers agrees. For the framing of the boards would fall apart from one another, if what one prophet or Apostle had said, another denied

275AAnd to the two boards also, etc. (BEDA, ibid.) The corner boards are joined in all things to the boards of the walls, etc., as far as the prophets foretold with a consonant voice that these things should be.

(ID., ibid.) Here the measure of the bases is not stated, etc., as far as but that it might be kept unmoved in secure quiet.

Thou shalt make also bars of wood, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 7.) The five bars which hold together the boards of the tabernacle, etc., as far as nor did he lay upon them other things than the commandments of the law.

VERS. 29.-- The boards themselves also thou shalt overlay with gold (ID., ibid.) The gilded boards of the tabernacle shine forth, etc., as far as but they gather also the light of the supernal recompense.

Which thou shalt cover with plates. (ID., ibid.) The bars are covered with plates of gold, etc., as far as when the spiritual 275Bunderstanding is shown full of mysteries.

VERS. 30.-- Thou shalt set up the tabernacle. (ID., ibid.) The pattern of the tabernacle, etc., as far as they compose both their work and their mind.

VERS. 31.-- Thou shalt make also a veil. (ID., ibid., cap. 8). This agrees, even according to the letter, with the beauty of the heavenly vision. For if thou consider the comeliness and the flames of the stars, the manifold appearance of the clouds and the rainbow itself, drawing out a thousand and diverse colours when the sun is turned away, thou shalt mark in heaven many more and fairer paintings than in the veil of the tabernacle.

Thou shalt make also a veil, etc. (ID., ibid.) This veil, as far as that he may appear before the face of God for us.

Thou shalt make also a veil of hyacinth and purple and scarlet twice dyed. (ID., ibid.) Thus far concerning the southern wall, 275Cetc., as far as colours when the sun is turned away.

VERS. 32.-- Which thou shalt hang before four, etc. (ID., ibid.) Now the four pillars, etc., as far as this shall not be there where there is nothing at all of evil.

Which indeed shall be gilded. (ID., ibid.) The pillars before which the veil is hung are gilded, because the powers of the angels, placed within the veil of heaven, are clothed with the grace of the highest brightness. They have heads of gold, because they have the mind by which they are ruled made bright by the presence of divine knowledge and vision.

(ID., ibid.) The pillars have bases of silver, etc., as far as the brightness of words is shown by the silver.

(GREG., Moral. lib. XXVIII, cap. 6). He commands the bases of the four pillars to be of silver, etc., as far as they carry the sayings of the evangelists by mouth and by work.

275DVERS. 33.-- And the veil shall be inserted, etc. (BED., ubi supra. ) Because it was in the eternal counsel of the Godhead, etc., as far as: in the day of judgment.

Within which thou shalt put the ark of the testimony, etc. Within this veil the ark of the testament was placed, because Christ, conscious of the Father's secrets, after his passion and resurrection, ascending the heaven of heavens, sits at the right hand of the Father.

They shall be divided. Because the Church, which consists of angels and of men, partly sojourns in the lowest places, partly reigns in the highest, has her citizens still separated one from another, the veil of heaven parting them.

VERS. 35.-- And the table without the veil, etc. (ID. ibid.) The table and the candlestick of the tabernacle signify the temporal 276Abenefits of God, etc., as far as bearing witness to him.

VERS. 36.-- Thou shalt make also a hanging, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 9). The narrative concerning the veil being set forth, etc., as far as because it rejoiced in the continence of flesh and of mind.

(ID.) According to the letter the tabernacle was a house consecrated to God, etc., as far as of breadth fifty.

VERS. 37.-- And five pillars. (ID., ibid.) The pillars by which the hanging is suspended, etc., as far as the evangelical Scripture not yet laid open.

Of setim wood. (ID., ibid.) The pillars of wood, etc., as far as: but Jesus Christ.

The heads whereof shall be of gold, etc., (ID., ibid.) The golden heads signify Christ, etc., as far as it is built up of the souls of the elect 276Bthat lack corruption.

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS 1.-- Facies et altare de lignis. (BED., de Tabernac., lib. II, cap. 11, tom. II.) Scilicet quia munda et incorrupta decet esse corda in quibus Spiritus sanctus habitat.

Et tres cubitos. (ID., ibid.) Tanta fere est statura hominis, etc., usque ad ad altare non pertinet.

VERS. 2.-- Cornua autem, etc. (ID., Ibid.) Quatuor angulos habet altare holocausti, etc., usque ad inviolabilis, imo fortior, persistit.

VERS. 3.-- Faciesque in usus ejus lebetes. (ID., ibid.) Vasa altaris diversa, etc., usque ad charitas nunquam exstinguenda flagrabit.

276CForcipes atque. (ID., Ibid.) Forcipes sunt praedicatores, etc., usque ad et lucere praecipiunt.

Atque fuscinulas, et ignium, etc. (ID., ibid.) Fuscinulae, quae Graece vocantur κρεάγραι, etc., usque ad nostrae vero capacitatis mensuram transcendant.

Ignium receptacula. Ad hoc deputata sunt, ut per haec ignis sanctus ab altari holocausti ad altare thymiamatis vespere et mane, ad incensum ponendum, deferretur; quia doctores tanquam ignem de altari holocaustorum ad altare thymiamatis deferunt, cum auditores de virtute in virtutem provehunt docendo, et meritis crescentibus, ad altiora et divinae visionis arcana interiora. Qui etiam ferventia pietate corda proximorum imitantur, receptacula ignium fiunt, quia flammam coelestis sacrificii, 276Dquam in fraternis mentibus conspiciunt, in suis accendere satagunt.

VERS. 4.-- Craticulumque in modum, etc. (BED., ubi supra, cap. 18.) Altare totum concavum fieri praeceptum est, etc., usque ad a Judaeorum doctoribus didicisse putamus.

VERS. 5.-- Subter arulam, etc. (ID., ibid.) Habet altare arulam, etc., usque ad et ad finem perfectionis perducantur.

(ID.) Si quem movet juxta litteram, etc., usque ad tanto mundius inveniatur.

VERS. 6.-- Facies et vectes. Doctores scilicet qui Judaeam et Gentilitatem in unam fidei gratiam consono ore et opere vocent. Vel ex utroque latere altaris 277Aad portandum erunt vectes, cum praedicatores mentes fidelium in prosperis et in adversis constantiam virtutum tenere docebunt.

VERS. 8.-- Sicuttibi in monte. (BEDA, ibid.) Omnia quae nobis mystice, etc., usque ad semper agere jubemur.

VERS. 9.-- Facies et atrium. (ID., ubi supra, cap. 13.) Sicut Sancta sanctorum, etc., usque ad sed ejus memoria ab intimo corde eradicatur.

Centum cubitos. (ID., ibid.) Constat centenarium, etc., usque ad et continentiae temporalis assumant.

VERS. 10.-- Et columnas viginti. (ID., ibid.) Columnae quibus tentoria suspenduntur, etc., usque ad quia non aliud quam verbum Dei sonant.

277BCum basibus, etc. (ID., ibid.) Basibus columnarum, etc., usque ad nunquam fructu mercedis carebunt.

Argentea. (ID.) Capita non argentata, sed tota argentea: quia doctores totos se divinae Scripturae subjiciunt, verba divina meditando et praedicando mancipant animam et corpus. Sicut enim membra sine capite vivere nequeunt, sic illi eloquia divina vitam suam existimantes haec nunquam obediendo portare, et se humiliando exaltare desistunt.

VERS. 11.-- Similiter et in latere. (ID., ibid.) Eadem de latere Aquilonis repetuntur, etc., usque ad in uno tabernaculi latere fieri praecipiuntur.

VERS. 12.-- In latitudine vero, etc. (ID., ibid.) Latitudo atrii quod respicit ad occasum solis, usque 277Cad decem columnae sublevantes extollunt.

VERS. 13.-- In ea quoque atrii. (ID., ibid.) Latitudo atrii orientem respiciens, etc., usque ad: et vitam futuri saeculi.

Quinquaginta cubiti erunt. (ID.) Quinquaginta cubitis plagae orientalis generaliter initia credentium signantur, quia in remissione peccatorum, et spe futurae beatitudinis celebrantur, quorum specialis distinctio subinfertur, quae ad eumdem finem bene considerata refertur. Sequitur enim.

VERS. 14.-- In quibus quindecim cubitorum tentoria lateri uno deputabuntur columnaeque tres, etc. (ID., ibid.) Quindecim namque, etc., usque ad et corpus sine querela in diem Domini custodire.

VERS. 16.-- Columnasque habebit, etc. (ID., ibid.) 277DIn introitu atrii quatuor sunt columnae, etc., usque ad omnes corporis sensus in obedientia charitatis expendunt.

Ex hyacintho, etc. (ID., ibid.) Recte in hoc introitu atrii tentorum, etc., usque ad tentorium decorant pulchra colorum varietate contextum.

VERS. 17.-- Omnes columnae, etc. (ID.) Quae dixerat, diligentius inculcat, ut sedulo lectorem admoneat, neminem inter doctores veritatis, columnas scilicet atrii coelestis, posse computari, qui non instar aeris invincibilem inter adversa patientiam habere didicerit, nisi sese coelestium observantia et annuntiatione verborum, velut argenti laminis undique muniendo exornaverit, nisi se omni subjectione 278Apraeceptis divinis, quasi argenteo capiti, supposuerit, et timore Dei velut aenea basi stabilierit.

VERS. 18.-- In longitudine, etc. (ID.) Centum cubitos longitudinis, etc., usque ad fietque de bysso retorta, etc.

VERS. 19.-- Cuncta vasa, etc. (ID., ibid.) Haec vasa homines sunt in Ecclesia, etc., usque ad atria Domini.

VERS. 20.-- Praecipe filiis Israel. (ID., lib. III de Tabern., cap. 1, tom. II.) Disposito omni ornatu tabernaculi, etc., usque ad lumen bonae actionis ostendat.

De arboribus olivarum. (ID., ibid.) Notandum quod filii Israel jubentur offerre oleum, etc., usque ad ad 278Bincendenda thymiamata orationum.

VERS. 21.-- Et collocabunt. (ID., ibid.) Non est enim omnium sacramenta fidei populo praedicare, etc., usque ad qualibus Jacobus dicit. Nolite plures magistri fieri, fratres mei, etc. (Jac. III).

Perpetuus erit, etc. (ID., ibid.) Haec clausula, sicut quae tabernaculo, etc., usque ad cum sit Deus omnia in omnibus.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS 1.-- Thou shalt make also an altar of wood. (BED., de Tabernac., lib. II, cap. 11, tom. II.) Namely because clean and uncorrupted ought those hearts to be in which the Holy Spirit dwells.

And three cubits. (ID., ibid.) Such, well nigh, is the stature of a man, etc., as far as it does not pertain to the altar.

VERS. 2.-- And the horns, etc. (ID., Ibid.) The altar of holocaust has four corners, etc., as far as it abides inviolable, nay rather the stronger.

VERS. 3.-- And thou shalt make for the uses thereof cauldrons. (ID., ibid.) The divers vessels of the altar, etc., as far as charity, never to be quenched, shall burn.

276CAnd tongs. (ID., Ibid.) The tongs are the preachers, etc., as far as they bid also to shine.

And fleshhooks, and firepans, etc. (ID., ibid.) The fleshhooks, which in Greek are called κρεάγραι, etc., as far as they should go beyond the measure of our capacity.

Receptacles of fire. For this were they appointed, that by them the holy fire might be carried from the altar of holocaust to the altar of incense, evening and morning, for the setting on of the incense; because the teachers carry fire as it were from the altar of holocausts to the altar of incense, when by teaching they advance their hearers from virtue to virtue, and, as their merits grow, to higher things and to the more inward secrets of the divine vision. And they who imitate the hearts of their neighbours fervent with piety become receptacles of fires, because the flame of the heavenly sacrifice, 276Dwhich they behold in their brethren's minds, they are busy to kindle in their own.

VERS. 4.-- And a gridiron after the manner, etc. (BED., ubi supra, cap. 18.) The whole altar was commanded to be made hollow, etc., as far as we think they learned it from the teachers of the Jews.

VERS. 5.-- Beneath the hearth, etc. (ID., ibid.) The altar has a hearth, etc., as far as they may be brought to the end of perfection.

(ID.) If it moves anyone according to the letter, etc., as far as it may be found the cleaner.

VERS. 6.-- Thou shalt make also bars. The teachers, namely, who may call the Jewry and the Gentile world into one grace of faith with harmonious mouth and work. Or, on either side of the altar 277Athere shall be bars for carrying it, when the preachers shall teach the minds of the faithful to hold the constancy of the virtues in prosperity and in adversity.

VERS. 8.-- As to thee Sicuttibi in the mount. (BEDA, ibid.) All the things which for us mystically, etc., as far as we are bidden always to do.

VERS. 9.-- Thou shalt make also a court. (ID., ubi supra, cap. 13.) As the Holy of holies, etc., as far as but the memory of it is rooted out from the inmost heart.

A hundred cubits. (ID., ibid.) It is agreed that the number of a hundred, etc., as far as and they may take up the continence of things temporal.

VERS. 10.-- And twenty pillars. (ID., ibid.) The pillars on which the hangings are hung, etc., as far as because they sound forth nothing other than the word of God.

277BWith bases, etc. (ID., ibid.) By the bases of the pillars, etc., as far as they shall never lack the fruit of a reward.

Of silver. (ID.) The heads not silvered over, but wholly of silver: because the teachers wholly subject themselves to the divine Scripture, and by meditating and preaching the divine words make over to them soul and body. For as the members cannot live without the head, so they, accounting the divine oracles their own life, never cease to bear these by obeying, and to exalt themselves by humbling themselves.

VERS. 11.-- In like manner also on the side. (ID., ibid.) The same things are repeated concerning the side of the North, etc., as far as they are commanded to be made on one side of the tabernacle.

VERS. 12.-- But in the breadth, etc. (ID., ibid.) The breadth of the court which looks toward the setting of the sun, as far 277Cas ten pillars lift up and bear it.

VERS. 13.-- In that breadth also of the court. (ID., ibid.) The breadth of the court looking toward the east, etc., as far as: and the life of the world to come.

Fifty cubits shall be. (ID.) By the fifty cubits of the eastern side are signified in general the beginnings of them that believe, because they are kept in the remission of sins and in the hope of future blessedness; and the particular distinction of these is subjoined, which, being well considered, is referred to the same end. For there follows.

VERS. 14.-- In which there shall be hangings of fifteen cubits allotted to one side, and three pillars, etc. (ID., ibid.) For fifteen, etc., as far as and to keep the body without complaint unto the day of the Lord.

VERS. 16.-- And it shall have pillars, etc. (ID., ibid.) 277DIn the entrance of the court there are four pillars, etc., as far as they spend all the senses of the body in the obedience of charity.

Of hyacinth, etc. (ID., ibid.) Rightly in this entrance of the court of the hangings, etc., as far as they adorn the hanging woven with a fair variety of colours.

VERS. 17.-- All the pillars, etc. (ID.) What he had said he inculcates the more diligently, that he may earnestly admonish the reader that none can be reckoned among the teachers of truth, that is, the pillars of the heavenly court, who shall not have learned to have, after the likeness of brass, an unconquered patience amid adversities; unless he shall have adorned himself by fencing himself about on every side, as with plates of silver, with the observance and the announcing of heavenly words; unless he shall have put himself under the divine 278Aprecepts by every subjection, as under a head of silver, and shall have made himself steadfast in the fear of God as upon a base of brass.

VERS. 18.-- In length, etc. (ID.) A hundred cubits of length, etc., as far as and it shall be made of twisted fine linen, etc.

VERS. 19.-- All the vessels, etc. (ID., ibid.) These vessels are men in the Church, etc., as far as the courts of the Lord.

VERS. 20.-- Command the children of Israel. (ID., lib. III de Tabern., cap. 1, tom. II.) All the adornment of the tabernacle being disposed, etc., as far as it may show the light of good action.

Of the trees of the olives. (ID., ibid.) It is to be noted that the children of Israel are bidden to offer oil, etc., as far as for 278Bthe burning of the incense of prayers.

VERS. 21.-- And they shall set. (ID., ibid.) For it belongs not to all to preach the mysteries of the faith to the people, etc., as far as to such as James says: Be ye not many masters, my brethren, etc. (Jac. III).

It shall be perpetual, etc. (ID., ibid.) This clause, like those which to the tabernacle, etc., as far as since God is all in all.

CAPUT XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Applica quoque ad te, etc. (BEDA, lib. III de Tabernac., cap. 1, tom. II.) Quomodo tabernaculum, etc., usque ad et cunctos Ecclesiae magistros.

(ID.) Applica quoque, etc. Descripta factura tabernaculi, 278Cetc., usque ad igne supernae ultionis absumuntur.

VERS. 2.-- Faciesque vestem sanctam. (ID., ibid.) Vestes sanctae Aaron, etc., usque ad et Dei verbi dispensatorem.

VERS. 4.-- Haec autem. (ID., ibid.) Quomodo haec singula facta sint, etc., usque ad praeclara et interni arbitri conspectui oportet esse gloriosa.

(GREG. Past., lib. II, cap. 3, tom. III.) Jubetur sacerdos in utroque humero, etc., usque ad quasi in diversa superhumeralis specie byssus torta candescit.

VERS. 6.-- Facient autem superhumerale. (BED., lib. III de Tabernac., cap. 4.) Quia in humeris onera portamus, etc., usque ad cum per abstinentiam caro 278Dfatigatur.

Coccoque bis tincto et bysso retorta, opere polymito. (ID., ibid.) Coccus ignis specie refulget, etc., usque ad in suo habitu coccum demonstrans.

VERS. 7.-- Duas oras junctas habebit in utroque latere summitatum, ut in unum redeant. (ID., ibid.) De hoc in sequentibus dicitur, etc., usque ad quam vitae invisibilis praemia requirimus.

VERS. 8.-- Ipsa quoque textura et cuncta operis varietas erit ex auro et hyacintho ac purpura, coccoque bis tincto, et bysso retorta. Sumesque. (ID., ibid.) Non in una parte aurum, etc., usque ad sacerdos debet esse nitidus.

VERS. 9.-- Duos lapides, etc. Ut sacerdos fidem 279Aet vitam patriarcharum imitetur et duodecim tribuum in sacrificiis et orationibus memor sit et populus intuens non aberret a patriarcharum meritis.

VERS. 11.-- Inclusos auro, etc. (BED., ibid.) Apte lapides includi auro atque circumdari jubentur, etc., usque ad custodia mentis valeant prolabi.

VERS. 12.-- Portabitque Aaron nomina eorum coram Domino super utrumque humerum ob recordationem. Quia sacerdos diligenter omni hora praecedentium sanctorum vitam perpendens in adversis et prosperis virtutum ornamento debet muniri, ut per arma justitae a dextris et a sinistris gradiens in nullo delectationis infimae latere flectatur.

(BED. ibid.) Tribus causis Aaron nomina, etc., 279Busque ad sive patrum exempla quae sequantur proponat.

VERS. 13, 14.-- Facies et uncinos ex auro, et duas catenulas auri purissimi sibi invicem cohaerentes quas inseres uncinis. (ID., ibid.) Uncini isti in supremis erant angulis, etc., usque ad per charitatem et bonam intentionem sunt nectenda.

(GREG., Past., part. II, cap. 2, tom. III.) Praecipitur ut in pectore Aaron rationale judicii vittis ligantibus imprimatur, etc., usque ad non sine magno timore regantur subjecti.

VERS. 15.-- Rationale quoque judicii facies opere, etc. (BED., ubi supra, cap. 5.) Sicut in superhumerali operum perfectio, etc., usque ad congruant studiose cogitare.

279CVERS. 16.-- Duplex. (ID., ibid.) Factum est rationale, etc., usque ad ut comprehendat bravium supernae vocationis Dei.

VERS. 17.-- Ponesque in eo. (ID., ibid.) Haec positio diversarum gemmarum in rationali, etc., usque ad ut et illi membra summi sacerdotis mereantur effici.

(ID.) Possumus in vario decore lapidum, etc., usque ad nihil illi prodest.

VERS. 19.-- Onychinus. (ID.) Recte autem duo lapides onychini, etc., usque ad ipse ad meliora proficiat.

VERS. 22.-- Facies in rationali, etc. (ID.) Ordo operis, etc., usque ad et a se invicem moverentur.

(ID., ibid.) Superhumerale consummationem bonorum 279Doperum, etc., usque ad si non eum accelerans bonae actionis uncinis contineat.

(ID.) Quod superhumerale strictum erat balteo, etc., usque ad per virtutum operationem.

VERS. 28.-- Vitta hyacinthina, etc. (ID., ibid.) Vittae hyacinthinae quibus uterque sacerdotis habitus copulatur, ligaturae sunt desiderii coelestis, quae annulis aureis innexae superhumerale cum rationali stringunt, cum agnita luce perennis patriae in illam gloriam suspiramus, et ut intrare mereamur concordi fide, et vita, et opere, et professione, in tabernaculo praesentis ecclesiae servire studemus.

VERS. 29.-- Portabitque Aaron nomina filiorum Israel in rationali judicii super, etc. Nomina filiorum 280AIsrael Aaron super pectus memoriale coram Domino portat in aeternum, quando praesul nunquam subditorum curam intermittit, sed vitam eorum consolando, increpando, exhortando, confortat, et tutandam Domino crebris orationibus satagit commendare.

(GREG., lib. XXVIII Moral., cap. 9.) Sacerdos quando tabernaculum ingreditur, duodecim lapides in pectore portare jubetur sculptos nominibus filiorum Israel: hic est redemptor noster, qui semetipsum pro nobis sacrificium offerens, dum fortes in exordio praedicatores habuit, duodecim lapides sub capite in prima sui corporis parte portavit.

Oportet sacerdotem memoriam patrum ferre in pectore, maxime cum ministrat altari, ut in quo 280Bsacratius est ministerium, solertius se toto studio, tota industria mentis, exornet, et corporis, exempla sanctorum respiciens, ut eorum sedulus imitator aptum se divinis praesentet aspectibus.

VERS. 30.-- Pones autem in rationali judicii, etc. (BED., ubi supra. ) Ut scilicet meminerit sacerdos, etc., usque ad in eo quod divina vice dispensat.

Pones autem in rationali, etc. (AUG., Quaest. 117 in Exod.) In quali re vel metallo ponatur demonstratio et veritas super rationale, etc., usque ad impositam super λογίον.

VERS. 31.-- Facies et tunicam, etc. (BED., ubi supra. ) Tunica talari tota hyacinthina vestitur sacerdos, etc., usque ad recte additur:

VERS. 32.-- In cujus medio supra erit capitium 280Cet ora per gyrum ejus textilis: sicut fieri, etc. Capitium tunica hyacinthina firmissimum habet, et oram ex sese textam, ne facile rumpatur, cum primordium boni operis forti radice timoris Domini subnixum et contra omnes insidias antiqui hostis probatur esse munitum. Talis ora collum sacerdotis per gyrum vestit et ornat, quando rectori fiduciam praedicandi coelestia praebet: hoc quod non solum in vitae cursu recte vixerit, sed exordium quoque a rectitudine coeperit, sicut Samuel, Jeremias, Joannes, qui ab ipsa infantia spirituali gratia repleti, et in doctorum ordine sunt separati. Quia enim vox in collo est, per collum recte loquendi usus exprimitur, sicut fieri solet in extremis vestium partibus: sicut 280Dvestibus opera, ita extremis vestium partibus operum consummatio figuratur. Vel extremae vestium partes, ultimae sunt nostrae sollicitudines, quibus fideles, cum finire vitam coguntur, intentius ab omni labe malorum se expurgare contendunt, cum metu et pavore curantes, ne ante districtum judicem producti pro sordido vitiorum habitu expellantur in tenebras exteriores, sed appareant induti, sicut electi Dei, viscera misericordiae, benignitatem, modestiam, patientiam et charitatem, quod est vinculum perfectionis (Coloss. III). Tunica ergo hyacinthina habet oram in capitio textilem, sicut in extremis vestium partibus solet fieri: cum doctor egregius a tanta perfectione opus virtutum inchoat, ad quantam alius longo labore vix aliquando per 281Aveniat; et cum tanto timore famulatus omni hora cum Deo suo ambulat, quantum alius vix moriturus, et ad judicium ultimum ingressurus habere sufficiat; sed quia tota perfectio sacerdotis in operibus et doctrina veritatis consistit, secundum illud: Coepit Jesus facere et docere (Act. I), recte subjungitur: Deorsum vero ad pedes ejusdem tunicae (Exod. XXVIII).

Facies et tunicam, etc. (BEDA, ut supra. ) Tunica superhumeralis dicitur, etc., usque ad a capite usque ad pedes gratia virtutum contecta esse debere.

VERS. 33.-- Deorsum vero, etc. (GREG., lib. I Regist., c. 29.) Cum rector se ad loquendum praeparat, etc., usque ad unitatem fidei cauta observatione teneatis.

(BEDA, ut supra. ) Deorsum ad pedes tunicae fiunt, 281Betc., usque ad discreto sermonis sonitu produnt.

(ID.) Possumus in duodenario gemmarum rationalis intelligere, etc., usque ad De eodem anno et David dicit: Benedices coronae anni benignitatis tuae (Psal. LXIV).

(ID.) Vel sicut supra dictum est, etc., usque ad in variarum splendore virtutum.

VERS. 35.-- Ut audiatur, etc. (GREG., ubi supra. ) Sacerdos ingrediens vel regrediens, etc., usque ad ipsa quoque opera sacerdotis clament.

(BED., ubi supra. ) Quod si Josephi verbis intendere volumus, etc., usque ad quadrati orbis partibus praedicaretur.

VERS. 36.-- Facies et laminam, etc. (ID., ibid., c. 7.) Lamina aurea in fronte pontificis, etc., usque ad ut 281Ceamdem resurrexisse fateamur.

Sanctum Domino, etc. (ID.) Quod in lamina sculpi jubetur, etc., usque ad quam reparavit sua passione.

(ID.) Sanctum Domini auro sculptum in fronte portamus, etc., usque ad qui omnibus debent exemplum virtutis ostendere.

VERS. 37.-- Ligabisque eam. Ligatur lamina, quae sanctum Domini continet, vitta hyacinthina cum tiara pontificis, cum spe coelestium praemiorum, quam hyacinthus significat, in fide confortamur, et eo arctius sacramenta nostrae redemptionis, vel imaginem ac similitudinem conditoris ac redemptoris nostri intemeratam servamus, quo nullum aliud esse salutis iter cognovimus. De tiara suo loco 281Ddicetur.

VERS. 38.-- Portabitque Aaron. Portat scilicet sacerdos iniquitates in muneribus et donariis quae obtulerunt Domino, et sanctificaverunt filii Israel: cum pro fructibus dignae poenitentiae, id est eleemosynis, caeterisque operibus justitiae, quae faciunt poenitentes, a reatu scelerum absolvit. Haec quidem ita gerenda institutio divina in habitu pontificis figuravit et verbis manifeste docuit. Proh dolor! autem, quidam donaria accipiunt, imo exigunt, et iniquitates populi non asportant nec castigant, cum ob hoc solum debeant sumere carnalia, quia ministrant spiritualia. Sed quia pro subditis potest efficaciter laborare, praedicare, et Domino supplicare cujus 282Aanimus divini nominis memoria semper est praeditus, apte subjungitur: Erit autem lamina semper in fronte ejus, etc.

Erit autem lamina, etc. (BED., ubi supra, cap. 8.) Si lamina nomine Dei scripta semper fuerit in fronte Aaron, etc., usque ad promereri festinant.

VERS. 39.-- Stringesque tunicam. (ID., ibid.) Quare linea dicta sit stricta, etc., usque ad cum dicitur: Et tiaram byssinam facies, etc.

(BED., ibid.) Qualiter haec facta sit, etc., usque ad auditoribus asperum reddat et durum.

Et tiaram, etc. (ID., ibid.) Tiara quae cidaris et mitra vocatur, etc., usque ad timore Dei perficeret.

(ID.) De factura baltei, etc., usque ad et caetera indumenta, cum dicitur:

282BVERS. 40.-- Porro filiis Aaron tunicas parabis lineas, etc. (Ibid.) Dicendum est autem de balteo pontificis, etc., usque ad et fides cinctorium renum ejus.

Tunicas parabis, etc. (ID., ibid.) Tunicas habent sacerdotes lineas, etc., usque ad et olfactus digne Deo custodiantur.

VERS. 41.-- Vestiesque his, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 9.) Omnibus his vestiendus erat Aaron et filii ejus, sed distincte, ut ipse quidem omnibus his uteretur; filii autem ultimis tribus, quae eis proprie ascripta sunt, cum dicitur: Porro filiis Aaron tunicas parabis lineas, etc.

(ID.) Feminalia quae ad operiendam corporis vel carnis turpitudinem fiunt, etc., usque ad feminalibus, 282Cscilicet linea stricta, cingulo, et tiara.

CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Take also unto thee, etc. (BEDA, lib. III de Tabernac., cap. 1, tom. II.) How the tabernacle, etc., as far as and all the masters of the Church.

(ID.) Take also, etc. The making of the tabernacle being described, 278Cetc., as far as they are consumed by the fire of the vengeance from above.

VERS. 2.-- And thou shalt make a holy vesture. (ID., ibid.) The holy vestments of Aaron, etc., as far as and a dispenser of the word of God.

VERS. 4.-- And these. (ID., ibid.) How each of these was made, etc., as far as they ought to be excellent and glorious to the sight of the inward judge.

(GREG. Past., lib. II, cap. 3, tom. III.) The priest is bidden on either shoulder, etc., as far as as in the diverse fashion of the superhumeral the twisted fine linen shines white.

VERS. 6.-- And they shall make the superhumeral. (BED., lib. III de Tabernac., cap. 4.) Because on the shoulders we bear burdens, etc., as far as when through abstinence the flesh 278Dis wearied.

And scarlet twice dyed, and twisted fine linen, of embroidered work. (ID., ibid.) The scarlet shines with the appearance of fire, etc., as far as showing the scarlet in his own habit.

VERS. 7.-- It shall have two edges joined at the top on both sides, that they may be closed into one. (ID., ibid.) Of this it is said in what follows, etc., as far as than that we seek the rewards of the invisible life.

VERS. 8.-- The very texture also and all the variety of the work shall be of gold and hyacinth and purple, and scarlet twice dyed, and twisted fine linen. And thou shalt take. (ID., ibid.) Not gold in one part, etc., as far as the priest ought to be bright.

VERS. 9.-- Two stones, etc. That the priest may imitate the faith 279Aand life of the patriarchs, and be mindful of the twelve tribes in his sacrifices and prayers, and that the people, beholding, may not stray from the merits of the patriarchs.

VERS. 11.-- Set in gold, etc. (BED., ibid.) Fitly are the stones bidden to be set in gold and to be encompassed about, etc., as far as they may be able to slip away from the keeping of the mind.

VERS. 12.-- And Aaron shall bear their names before the Lord upon both shoulders for a memorial. Because the priest, diligently weighing at every hour the life of the saints that went before, ought in adversity and in prosperity to be fortified with the ornament of the virtues, so that, walking by the arms of justice on the right hand and on the left, he may be bent aside on no side of the lowest delight.

(BED. ibid.) For three causes the names of Aaron, etc., 279Bas far as or may set forth the examples of the fathers which they are to follow.

VERS. 13, 14.-- Thou shalt make also hooks of gold, and two little chains of purest gold, linked one to another, which thou shalt put into the hooks. (ID., ibid.) These hooks were in the topmost corners, etc., as far as they are to be knit together by charity and by good intention.

(GREG., Past., part. II, cap. 2, tom. III.) It is commanded that upon Aaron's breast the rational of judgment be fastened with binding fillets, etc., as far as subjects are not ruled without great fear.

VERS. 15.-- Thou shalt make also the rational of judgment with work, etc. (BED., ubi supra, cap. 5.) As in the superhumeral the perfection of works, etc., as far as they may study earnestly to think what befits them.

279CVERS. 16.-- Double. (ID., ibid.) The rational was made, etc., as far as that he may lay hold of the prize of the supernal vocation of God.

VERS. 17.-- And thou shalt set in it. (ID., ibid.) This setting of divers gems in the rational, etc., as far as that they too may deserve to be made members of the highest priest.

(ID.) We can in the varied comeliness of the stones, etc., as far as it profits him nothing.

VERS. 19.-- The onyx. (ID.) Rightly moreover the two onyx stones, etc., as far as he himself may advance to better things.

VERS. 22.-- Thou shalt make in the rational, etc. (ID.) The order of the work, etc., as far as and they would be moved apart one from another.

(ID., ibid.) The superhumeral the consummation of good 279Dworks, etc., as far as if he does not, hastening, hold it in with the hooks of good action.

(ID.) That the superhumeral was drawn tight with a belt, etc., as far as through the working of the virtues.

VERS. 28.-- A hyacinth fillet, etc. (ID., ibid.) The hyacinth fillets by which both habits of the priest are coupled are the bindings of heavenly desire, which, knit into the golden rings, draw the superhumeral together with the rational, when, the light of the everlasting fatherland being known, we sigh after that glory, and, that we may deserve to enter in, are earnest to serve in the tabernacle of the present Church with concordant faith, and life, and work, and profession.

VERS. 29.-- And Aaron shall bear the names of the children of Israel in the rational of judgment upon, etc. The names of the children 280Aof Israel Aaron bears upon his breast as a memorial before the Lord for ever, when the prelate never intermits the care of his subjects, but strengthens their life by consoling, rebuking, exhorting, and is busy to commend it to the Lord to be guarded with frequent prayers.

(GREG., lib. XXVIII Moral., cap. 9.) The priest, when he enters the tabernacle, is bidden to bear on his breast twelve stones graven with the names of the children of Israel: this is our Redeemer, who, offering himself a sacrifice for us, while he had strong preachers at the beginning, bore twelve stones under the head, in the first part of his body.

It behoves the priest to bear the memory of the fathers on his breast, chiefly when he ministers at the altar, that in the ministry which is 280Bthe more sacred he may the more skilfully adorn himself with his whole study, with all the industry of mind and of body, looking upon the examples of the saints, that as their earnest imitator he may present himself fitted to the divine gaze.

VERS. 30.-- And thou shalt put in the rational of judgment, etc. (BED., ubi supra. ) That the priest, namely, may remember, etc., as far as in that which he dispenses in God's stead.

And thou shalt put in the rational, etc. (AUG., Quaest. 117 in Exod.) In what thing or metal the doctrine and the truth are set upon the rational, etc., as far as set upon the λογίον.

VERS. 31.-- Thou shalt make also a tunic, etc. (BED., ubi supra. ) The priest is clothed with a tunic reaching to the feet, wholly of hyacinth, etc., as far as it is rightly added:

VERS. 32.-- In the midst whereof above there shall be a head-opening 280Cand a woven border round about it: as it is wont to be made, etc. The hyacinth tunic has a most firm head-opening, and a border woven out of itself, lest it be easily rent, when the beginning of a good work, propped by the strong root of the fear of the Lord, is proved to be fortified also against all the snares of the ancient enemy. Such a border clothes and adorns the priest's neck round about, when it affords to the ruler confidence in preaching heavenly things: this, that he has not only lived rightly in the course of his life, but has also begun his beginning from rectitude, like Samuel, Jeremias, John, who from their very infancy, filled with spiritual grace, were also set apart in the order of teachers. For because the voice is in the neck, by the neck is expressed the practice of right speaking, as it is wont to be done in the outermost parts of garments: as 280Dby the garments the works, so by the outermost parts of the garments the consummation of the works is figured. Or, the outermost parts of the garments are our last anxieties, in which the faithful, when they are compelled to end their life, strive the more intently to purge themselves of every stain of evils, taking heed with fear and trembling lest, being brought before the strict judge, they be cast out into the outer darkness for the filthy habit of vices, but may appear clothed, as the elect of God, with bowels of mercy, benignity, modesty, patience and charity, which is the bond of perfection (Coloss. III). The hyacinth tunic, therefore, has a border woven in the head-opening, as it is wont to be made in the outermost parts of garments: when an excellent teacher begins the work of the virtues from so great a perfection as another scarcely at any time arrives at by 281Along labour; and when with so great fear of service he walks at every hour with his God, as another, about to die and to enter upon the last judgment, scarcely suffices to have. But because the whole perfection of the priest consists in works and in the doctrine of truth, according to that: Jesus began to do and to teach (Act. I), rightly is it subjoined: And beneath at the feet of the same tunic (Exod. XXVIII).

Thou shalt make also a tunic, etc. (BEDA, ut supra. ) The tunic is called of the superhumeral, etc., as far as that from the head even to the feet it ought to be covered by the grace of the virtues.

VERS. 33.-- And beneath, etc. (GREG., lib. I Regist., c. 29.) When the ruler prepares himself to speak, etc., as far as you may hold the unity of the faith with wary observance.

(BEDA, ut supra. ) Beneath at the feet of the tunic there are made, 281Betc., as far as they disclose by the distinct sound of speech.

(ID.) We can understand in the twelvefold number of the gems of the rational, etc., as far as Of the same year David also says: Thou shalt bless the crown of the year of thy goodness (Psal. LXIV).

(ID.) Or as was said above, etc., as far as in the splendour of divers virtues.

VERS. 35.-- That it may be heard, etc. (GREG., ubi supra. ) The priest going in or coming back, etc., as far as the very works of the priest may cry out.

(BED., ubi supra. ) But if we will attend to the words of Josephus, etc., as far as it might be preached to the parts of the foursquare world.

VERS. 36.-- Thou shalt make also a plate, etc. (ID., ibid., c. 7.) The golden plate on the forehead of the high priest, etc., as far as that 281Cwe may confess that she has risen again.

Holy to the Lord, etc. (ID.) That which is bidden to be graven on the plate, etc., as far as which he repaired by his passion.

(ID.) We bear on the forehead the Holy of the Lord graven in gold, etc., as far as who ought to show to all an example of virtue.

VERS. 37.-- And thou shalt tie it. The plate which contains the Holy of the Lord is tied with a hyacinth fillet to the tiara of the high priest, when by the hope of heavenly rewards, which the hyacinth signifies, we are strengthened in faith, and keep the more closely undefiled the mysteries of our redemption, or the image and likeness of our maker and redeemer, the more we have known that there is no other way of salvation. Of the tiara it shall be said in its own place. 281D

VERS. 38.-- And Aaron shall bear. The priest bears, namely, the iniquities in the gifts and offerings which the children of Israel have offered and sanctified to the Lord: when for the fruits of worthy penance, that is, for alms and the other works of justice which the penitent do, he absolves them from the guilt of their crimes. These things indeed the divine ordinance figured in the habit of the high priest as to be so performed, and taught manifestly in words. But, alas for the grief! certain men receive offerings, nay rather exact them, and do not carry away nor chastise the iniquities of the people, when for this alone ought they to take carnal things, because they minister spiritual ones. But because he can labour effectually for his subjects, and preach, and beseech the Lord, whose 282Amind is always furnished with the memory of the divine name, it is fitly subjoined: And the plate shall be always on his forehead, etc.

And the plate shall be, etc. (BED., ubi supra, cap. 8.) If the plate written with the name of God shall have been always on Aaron's forehead, etc., as far as they hasten to earn.

VERS. 39.-- And thou shalt gird the tunic. (ID., ibid.) Why the linen one is called drawn tight, etc., as far as when it is said: And thou shalt make a tiara of fine linen, etc.

(BED., ibid.) In what manner this was made, etc., as far as it may render it harsh and hard to the hearers.

And the tiara, etc. (ID., ibid.) The tiara, which is called cidaris and mitre, etc., as far as he might accomplish by the fear of God.

(ID.) Of the making of the belt, etc., as far as and the other garments, when it is said:

282BVERS. 40.-- But for the sons of Aaron thou shalt prepare linen tunics, etc. (Ibid.) But it must be said of the belt of the high priest, etc., as far as and faith the girdle of his reins.

Thou shalt prepare tunics, etc. (ID., ibid.) The priests have linen tunics, etc., as far as and the smell may be worthily kept for God.

VERS. 41.-- And thou shalt vest with these, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 9.) With all these were Aaron and his sons to be vested, but distinctly, so that he indeed should use all of them; but the sons the last three, which are properly ascribed to them, when it is said: But for the sons of Aaron thou shalt prepare linen tunics, etc.

(ID.) The breeches, which are made for the covering of the shame of the body or of the flesh, etc., as far as with the breeches, 282Cnamely, the linen drawn tight, the girdle, and the tiara.

CAPUT XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- Sed et hoc facies, etc. (BEDA, ubi supra. ) Hactenus Aaron et filiorum ejus habitus qualis esse debeat, etc., usque ad figurate demonstrant.

Tolle vitulum. (STRAB.) Quisquis pontifex vel sacerdos taurum debet offerre, id est, passionem Christi firmiter credere et imitari; et duos arietes, ut credat eumdem Christum ex corpore et anima constare; et panes azymos, id est, munditiam vitae; et oleo conspersos, ut inter omnes virtutes non negligat opera misericordiae vel, non a se, sed a dono gratiae se credat habere.

VERS. 2.-- Et crustulam. (BEDA, ubi supra. ) Crustulam 282Doleo conspersam ad consecrationem nostram Domino offerimus, cum omnia, quae facimus, per internam Spiritus gratiam in cordis devotione pinguescunt. Lagana oleo lita, cum foris spiritualia esse, quae agimus, indubitanter in exemplum vivendi ostendimus; quibus oblationibus consecratio nostra perficitur, dum per bona opera et cogitationes puras meritum nobis sanctimoniae, Deo donante, acquirimus.

Cumque laveris patrem, etc. (ID.) Prius lavari jubentur, deinde ornari vestibus, quia quicunque sacerdotii officium assumit, unda lacrymarum se abluere debet, ut ad ministerium divinum dignus approximet.

283A(BEDA.) Posset in hoc lavacro baptisma sacri fontis intelligi, si consequeretur ut quis in sacerdotium electus, tunc primum aqua baptismatis in remissionem peccatorum ablueretur, et non contradiceret Apostolus dicens: Non neophytum in superbiam elatus, in judicium incidat diaboli (I Tim. III).

VERS. 5.-- Indues Aaron, etc. (ID.) Notandum quod cum in Exodo Aaron octo vestibus induendus asseveretur, etc., usque ad quia venit in nomine Patris dicens: Ego in patre et pater in me est: et qui videt me, videt et patrem (Joan. IV).

VERS. 9.-- Postquam, etc. (AUG., quaest. 25 in Exod.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad sanctificari a Moyse.

VERS. 10.-- Applicabis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 126.) 283BAlia littera, etc., usque ad super vitulum immolandum.

VERS. 18.-- Oblatio est Domino. (AUG.) Odor suavitatis Domino, etc., usque ad odorem corporeis naribus ducit.

VERS. 22.-- Armumque dextrum. (GREG., Past., part. II, c. 3.) Sacerdos divina lege, etc., usque ad ad sublimia armo operis invitet.

VERS. 30.-- Et qui ingredietur. (AUG., quaest. 129.) Quomodo ergo proprium dicit hujus unius esse, etc., usque ad et utrobique servaretur quod scriptum est: Superexaltat misericordia judicium (Jac. II).

CHAPTER XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- But this also thou shalt do, etc. (BEDA, ubi supra. ) Thus far of what sort the habit of Aaron and of his sons ought to be, etc., as far as they show figuratively.

Take a calf. (STRAB.) Whosoever is high priest or priest ought to offer a bullock, that is, firmly to believe and to imitate the passion of Christ; and two rams, that he may believe that the same Christ consists of body and soul; and unleavened loaves, that is, cleanness of life; and sprinkled with oil, that among all the virtues he may not neglect the works of mercy, or, not of himself, but of the gift of grace, may believe that he has them.

VERS. 2.-- And a wafer. (BEDA, ubi supra. ) A wafer 282Dsprinkled with oil we offer to the Lord for our consecration, when all the things which we do grow fat through the inward grace of the Spirit in the devotion of the heart. Cakes anointed with oil, when we show without all doubt that the things we do are spiritual, for an example of living; by which oblations our consecration is perfected, while through good works and pure thoughts we acquire for ourselves, by God's gift, the merit of holiness.

And when thou hast washed the father, etc. (ID.) They are bidden first to be washed, then to be adorned with vestments, because whosoever undertakes the office of the priesthood ought to wash himself with the wave of tears, that he may approach worthy to the divine ministry.

283A(BEDA.) There might be understood in this washing the baptism of the sacred font, if it followed that one chosen to the priesthood should then first be washed with the water of baptism unto the remission of sins, and if the Apostle did not contradict, saying: Not a neophyte, lifted up into pride, he fall into the judgment of the devil (I Tim. III). [Vulg. 1Tim 3:6 Non neophytum: ne in superbiam elatus, in judicium incidat diaboli, "Not a neophyte: lest, being lifted up with pride, he fall into the judgment of the devil"; Migne prints no ne, so the purpose clause has lost its conjunction]

VERS. 5.-- Thou shalt clothe Aaron, etc. (ID.) It is to be noted that whereas in Exodus Aaron is asserted to be clothed with eight vestments, etc., as far as because he came in the name of the Father, saying: I am in the Father and the Father is in me: and he that sees me, sees the Father also (Joan. IV). [Vulg. Jn 14:10-11 ego in Patre, et Pater in me est joined to Jn 14:9 qui videt me, videt et Patrem; Migne's own citation names Joan. IV]

VERS. 9.-- After that, etc. (AUG., quaest. 25 in Exod.) Another reading, etc., as far as to be sanctified by Moses.

VERS. 10.-- Thou shalt bring near, etc. (AUG., quaest. 126.) 283BAnother reading, etc., as far as upon the calf to be immolated.

VERS. 18.-- It is an oblation to the Lord. (AUG.) An odour of sweetness to the Lord, etc., as far as draws in the odour with bodily nostrils.

VERS. 22.-- And the right shoulder. (GREG., Past., part. II, c. 3.) The priest by the divine law, etc., as far as he may invite to lofty things by the shoulder of work.

VERS. 30.-- And he that shall enter in. (AUG., quaest. 129.) How then does he say that it is proper to this one alone, etc., as far as and on both sides that would be kept which is written: Mercy exalteth itself above judgment (Jac. II).

CAPUT XXX.

VERS. 1.-- Facies quoque altare, etc. (BEDA, ubi 283Csnpra, cap. 11.) Expletis mandatis consecrationis, etc., usque ad etiam in corpore appropinquant.

Altare. (ID., ibid.) Apte etiam altare thymiamatis quantum metalli fulgore praecellebat, tanto quantitate minus erat: quanto enim sanctiores, tanto pauciores.

De lignis setim. (ID., ibid.) Apte de lignis setim albae spinae similibus et incorruptibilibus, etc., usque ad varia donationum gratia vivificat.

VERS. 2.-- Habens cubitum. (ID., ibid.) Unius cubiti est longitudo altaris et latitudo, etc., usque ad officium pietatis impendat.

Quadrangulum. (ID., ibid.) Recte altare thymiamatis, etc., usque ad laetitia sempiterna erit eis.

Cornua. (ID., ibid.) Cornua eminentiam fidei et 283Dvirtutis significant, etc., usque ad speciem pietatis habentes, virtutem ejus negant.

VERS. 3.-- Vestiesque illud, etc. (ID., ibid.) Apte vero illud aere, etc., usque ad quanta dulcedine Dei in abscondito vultus reficiantur.

Tam craticulam, etc. (ID., ibid.) Bene craticula altaris, etc., usque ad recte subditur:

Facies quoque ei coronam, etc. (ID., ibid.) Altare incensi coronam habet per gyrum: in his qui minores sunt meritis, nec palam protestari audent boni certaminis et servitutis et fidei sibi repositam esse mercedem: cuncta tamen agunt intentione bene placendi Deo, sed spe percipiendae mercedis.

VERS. 4.-- Et duos annulos, etc. (ID., ibid.) Possunt 284Ain his annulis quatuor Evangelii libri accipi, etc., usque ad sequentia demonstrant: Ponesque altare, etc.

VERS. 7.-- Et adolebit incensum, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 12.) Incensum vel thymiama, etc., usque ad gratias reddamus.

(ID.) Et adolebit incensum, etc. Mane orto sole clare omnia, etc., usque ad in caliginoso loco.

Mane quando componet. (ID., ibid.) Mane Aaron incensum, etc., usque ad et precibus vacare delectantur.

VERS. 9.-- Non offeretis. (ID., ibid.) In sequentibus, etc., usque ad stratum suum per singulas noctes irrigant.

(ID.) Possumus haec altaria interpretari, etc., 284Buque ad potest accipi.

VERS. 10.-- Et deprecabitur, etc. (ID., ibid.) Quia Christus sacerdos noster, etc., usque ad recte subjungit:

Et placabit, etc. (ID.) Placat Aaron super altare incensi, etc., usque ad Isaac et Israel, etc.

VERS. 11.-- Locutusque, etc. Descripto altari incensi, restat descriptio labii aenei, in quo lavarent manus et pedes ingressuri tabernaculum sacerdotes, sed unum mandatum praemittitur.

VERS. 12.-- Quando tuleris. (BEDA, ubi supra, cap. 13.) Hujus praecepti oblitus est David, etc., usque ad propriae divitiae suae.

VERS. 13.-- Dimidium sicli. (ID., ibid.) Id est decem obolos, etc., usque ad nulla potest infidelium 284Cperturbatione corrumpi.

VERS. 14.-- Qui habetur in numero, etc. (ID., ibid.) Vicenarius, etc., usque ad exspectat in coelis.

VERS. 15.-- Dives non addet, etc. (ID., ibid.) Quia sive magnus est quis meritis et perfectus, seu parvus, et in provectu virtutum positus, cunctis aeque lex decalogi, qua Deum et proximum diligant, imponitur

VERS. 16.-- Susceptamque pecuniam, etc. (ID., ibid.) Hoc fit cum quidquid agimus boni, etc., usque ad plaga districtae ultionis ferietur.

VERS. 17.-- Locutusque est Dominus, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 14.) Potest in hoc labio vel labro, etc., usque ad quae excubabant ad ingressum tabernaculi.

VERS. 18.-- Cum basi sua. (ID., ibid.) Basis in 284Dqua labrum erat positum, etc., usque ad: ut exhibeatis corpora vestra, etc. (Rom. XII).

Lavandum, etc. Christus quoque hujus labri aqua est lotus, priusquam ad altare oblaturus intraret, et thymiama sacri corporis pro salute nostra in ara crucis incenderet: pro nostro enim amore lacrymas fudit, sicut in resurrectione Lazari.

VERS. 21.-- Ne forte moriantur. (BED., ibid.) Mors enim timenda est aeterna, etc., usque ad: si manducat et bibit, etc. (I Cor. XI.)

VERS. 23.-- Sume tibi (STRAB.) σμύρνα Graece, Latine dicitur myrrha, qui est arbor in Arabia quinque cubitorum, cujus virgulta Arabes exurunt, ut melius pullulent et fructificent. Ex quorum nidore 285Aincurabiles morbos contraherent, nisi fumo storacis occurrerent. Hujus arboris gummi proprie stacte vocatur, abusive etiam myrrha.

Calami. Cinnamomi est virgultum, et in India vel Aethiopia nascitur duorum tantum cubitorum. Dicitur autem cinnamomum, quia in modum cannae subtiles et replicatos habet calamos, cinerei vel nigri coloris; qui fracti visibile spiramentum reddunt, et subtile quidem charum; grossum vero, vile et despectum. Calamus aromatica species juxta Libanum. Myrrhae, cinnamomi, calami, cassiae. Hae quatuor species significant quatuor principales virtutes, quibus oleo unctionis, id est gratia misericordiae, Dei Ecclesia dedicanda est et sanctificanda.

VERS. 29.-- Et erunt, etc. (AUG., quaest 135 in 285BExod.) Cum scilicet uncta fuerint. Quid ergo distabit jam inter illa interiora quae velo teguntur?

Qui tetigerit.
