Similiter de altari sacrificiorum superius dictum est, quod sic intelligendum est, ut liceat populo tangere tabernaculum, quando offerebant hostias, vel quaelibet dona, et tangendo sanctificabantur, propter illud oleum quo cuncta peruncta sunt: non sicut sacerdotes, qui etiam, ut aeterno sacerdotio fungerentur, ungebantur ex illo.

VERS. 34.-- Onychen. Onyche ostreola fertur esse parvula suave redolens, magnitudine humani unguis: unde et onyche Graece dicitur, quia ὄνυξ, onyx, Graece dicitur unguis.

Galbanum. Galbanum succus ferulae esse dicitur, et nascitur in Syria optimum: cartilaginosum est, 285Cet minus lignosum, cujus odore fugantur serpentes.

Et thus. Thus Graece, libanum Latine dicitur, a Libano monte Arabiae: alius enim Libanus est Phoenicis, in quo sunt cedri.

Thus optimum, scilicet quod masculinum dicitur, quia in modum humani testis rotundum, cujus arbor nascitur in Arabia ad aeris qualitatem perplexo et in torto vimine.

Mystice autem hae quatuor species significant sanctarum virtutum species.

VERS. 35.-- Faciesque thymiama, etc. (GREG., lib. I Moral., cap. 39, tom. I.) Thymiama ex aromatibus compositum facimus, etc., usque ad odorem de se subtilius non aspergit.

(ISID.) Incensum, quod ex quatuor odoratissimis generibus in maximam subtilitatem comminutis, id est, stacte, onycha, galbano, et thure componitur; orationes fidelium significat, quamvis quatuor elementa haec quatuor videant significare: ut thus, quod lucidum est, aeri comparetur, stactes aquis, galbanus et onyx terrae et igni, ut per haec omnium quae in coelo et infra coelum, et in terra, et in aquis, sunt placitum Deo incensum sit

Opere, etc. (AUG., quaest. 136.) Non debemus putare unguentum fieri, sed thymiama vel incensum, quod apponitur illi altari incensi ubi non licebat sacrificari, et erat intra sancta sanctorum.

VERS. 37.-- Talem, etc. (RAB.) Qui scilicet precibus et laudibus divinis non debemus uti in adulationibus 286Ahumanis. Soli enim Deo ita serviendum est, unde supra prohibetur, ne caro hominis ungatur ex eo.

CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 1.-- Thou shalt make also an altar, etc. (BEDA, ubi 283Csnpra, cap. 11.) The commands of the consecration being fulfilled, etc., as far as they draw near even in the body.

An altar. (ID., ibid.) Fitly also the altar of incense, by as much as it excelled in the brightness of its metal, by so much was it less in size: for the holier, the fewer.

Of setim wood. (ID., ibid.) Fitly of setim wood, like to the white thorn and incorruptible, etc., as far as it quickens with the varied grace of gifts.

VERS. 2.-- Having a cubit. (ID., ibid.) Of one cubit is the length of the altar and the breadth, etc., as far as he may spend the office of piety.

Foursquare. (ID., ibid.) Rightly the altar of incense, etc., as far as everlasting gladness shall be theirs.

Horns. (ID., ibid.) The horns signify the eminence of faith and 283Dof virtue, etc., as far as having the appearance of piety, they deny the power thereof.

VERS. 3.-- And thou shalt overlay it, etc. (ID., ibid.) Fitly moreover it with brass, etc., as far as with how great sweetness of God they are refreshed in the hiding of his face.

Both the gridiron, etc. (ID., ibid.) Well the gridiron of the altar, etc., as far as it is rightly subjoined:

Thou shalt make also to it a crown, etc. (ID., ibid.) The altar of incense has a crown round about: in those who are lesser in merits, and dare not openly protest that the reward of the good fight and of servitude and of faith is laid up for them: yet they do all things with the intention of pleasing God well, but with the hope of receiving the reward.

VERS. 4.-- And two rings, etc. (ID., ibid.) There can 284Abe taken in these rings the four books of the Gospel, etc., as far as the things that follow show: And thou shalt set the altar, etc.

VERS. 7.-- And he shall burn incense, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 12.) Incense, or thymiama, etc., as far as let us render thanks.

(ID.) And he shall burn incense, etc. In the morning, the sun being risen, all things clearly, etc., as far as in a dark place.

In the morning when he shall dress. (ID., ibid.) In the morning Aaron the incense, etc., as far as and they delight to have leisure for prayers.

VERS. 9.-- You shall not offer. (ID., ibid.) In what follows, etc., as far as they water their couch every night.

(ID.) We can interpret these altars, etc., 284Bas far as uque ad it can be taken.

VERS. 10.-- And he shall pray, etc. (ID., ibid.) Because Christ our priest, etc., as far as he rightly subjoins:

And he shall appease, etc. (ID.) Aaron appeases upon the altar of incense, etc., as far as Isaac and Israel, etc.

VERS. 11.-- And the Lord spoke, etc. The altar of incense being described, there remains the description of the brazen laver, in which the priests about to enter the tabernacle should wash their hands and feet; but one command is set before it.

VERS. 12.-- When thou shalt take. (BEDA, ubi supra, cap. 13.) Of this precept David was forgetful, etc., as far as his own riches.

VERS. 13.-- Half a sicle. (ID., ibid.) That is, ten obols, etc., as far as it can by no 284Cdisturbance of the unbelieving be corrupted.

VERS. 14.-- He that is counted in the number, etc. (ID., ibid.) The number twenty, etc., as far as awaits in heaven.

VERS. 15.-- The rich man shall not add, etc. (ID., ibid.) Because whether a man be great in merits and perfect, or little, and set in the advancement of the virtues, upon all alike is laid the law of the decalogue, by which they should love God and their neighbour

VERS. 16.-- And the money received, etc. (ID., ibid.) This is done when whatsoever good we do, etc., as far as it shall be smitten with the stroke of strict vengeance.

VERS. 17.-- And the Lord spoke, etc. (ID., ibid., cap. 14.) There can in this laver or basin, etc., as far as which watched at the entrance of the tabernacle.

VERS. 18.-- With its base. (ID., ibid.) The base on 284Dwhich the basin was set, etc., as far as: that you present your bodies, etc. (Rom. XII).

To be washed, etc. Christ also was washed with the water of this laver, before he entered in to offer at the altar, and burned the incense of his sacred body for our salvation on the altar of the cross: for out of love for us he shed tears, as at the raising of Lazarus.

VERS. 21.-- Lest perhaps they die. (BED., ibid.) For the death to be feared is the eternal one, etc., as far as: if he eats and drinks, etc. (I Cor. XI.)

VERS. 23.-- Take to thee (STRAB.) σμύρνα in Greek, in Latin is called myrrh, which is a tree in Arabia of five cubits, whose twigs the Arabians burn up, that they may sprout and bear fruit the better. From the reek of which 285Athey would contract incurable diseases, did they not meet it with the smoke of storax. The gum of this tree is properly called stacte, and by misuse also myrrh.

Of cane. It is the shoot of the cinnamon, and it grows in India or Ethiopia, of two cubits only. And it is called cinnamon, because after the manner of a reed it has slender and folded canes, of an ashen or black colour; which, being broken, give back a visible breath, and the slender indeed is precious; but the thick, cheap and despised. The cane is an aromatic kind near Libanus. Of myrrh, of cinnamon, of cane, of cassia. These four kinds signify the four principal virtues, by which with the oil of unction, that is, with the grace of mercy, the Church of God is to be dedicated and sanctified.

VERS. 29.-- And they shall be, etc. (AUG., quaest 135 in 285BExod.) When, namely, they shall have been anointed. What then shall now be the difference between those inner things which are covered by the veil?

He that shall touch. In like manner it was said above of the altar of sacrifices, which is so to be understood that it was lawful for the people to touch the tabernacle, when they offered victims, or any gifts whatsoever, and by touching they were sanctified, on account of that oil with which all things were anointed: not as the priests, who also, that they might discharge an eternal priesthood, were anointed from it.

VERS. 34.-- Onycha. Onycha is said to be a very small shellfish of sweet smell, of the size of a human nail: whence also it is called onycha in Greek, because ὄνυξ, onyx, in Greek is called a nail.

Galbanum. Galbanum is said to be the juice of a fennel-plant, and the best grows in Syria: it is gristly, 285Cand less woody, and by its smell serpents are put to flight.

And frankincense. Thus in Greek, libanum in Latin it is called, from Libanus a mountain of Arabia: for there is another Libanus, of Phoenicia, in which are the cedars.

Frankincense the best, namely that which is called masculine, because after the manner of a human testicle it is round, whose tree grows in Arabia, in a withe tangled and twisted according to the quality of the air.

Mystically moreover these four kinds signify the kinds of the holy virtues.

VERS. 35.-- And thou shalt make incense, etc. (GREG., lib. I Moral., cap. 39, tom. I.) We make incense compounded of spices, etc., as far as it does not scatter from itself an odour more subtle.

(ISID.) Incense, which is compounded of four most odorous kinds ground to the greatest fineness, that is, of stacte, onycha, galbanum, and frankincense, signifies the prayers of the faithful; although these four may see the four elements to signify: so that frankincense, which is bright, may be compared to air, stacte to the waters, galbanum and onyx to earth and to fire, so that by these there may be an incense pleasing to God of all things which are in heaven and beneath heaven, and in the earth, and in the waters

With work, etc. (AUG., quaest. 136.) We ought not to think that an ointment is made, but incense or frankincense, which is set upon that altar of incense where it was not lawful to sacrifice, and it was within the holy of holies.

VERS. 37.-- Such, etc. (RAB.) We, namely, who ought not to use prayers and divine praises in the flatteries 286Aof men. For God alone is so to be served, whence above it is forbidden that the flesh of man be anointed from it.

CAPUT XXXI.

VERS. 2.-- Beseleel, etc., qui interpretatur in umbra Dei. q. d. Dominus illuminatio mea et salus mea quem (Psal. LXII). Ab hoc dicitur David duxisse originem, et polymitarii nomen, quia Beseleel opere polymito tabernaculum construxit.

VERS. 3.-- Et implevi (AUG., quaest. 128.) Utrum Spiritus sancti dono opera ista tribuenda sunt, quae pertinere videntur ad opificium? an hoc etiam significative dictum est, ut ea pertineant ad divinum Spiritum sapientiae et intellectus et scientiae, quae his 286Brebus significantur? Tum etiam hic cum Spiritu repletus dicatur, iste divino sapientiae et scientiae, nondum legitur Spiritus sanctus.

VERS. VI.-- Oliab, qui interpretatur protectio mea, tanquam nomine dicat, Dominus protector vitae meae; a quo trepidabo (Psal. XXVI)? Bene autem tabernaculum construunt, qui tam constanter in Domino confidunt.

(STRAB.) Notandum quod in constructione tabernaculi mysterium Trinitatis ostenditur, sicut in baptismo et in transfiguratione. In baptismo enim in voce sonuit Pater: et Filius baptizatus est; in specie columbae Spiritus apparuit. Ooliab quoque, qui interpretatur protectio mea pater, Patris personam: Beseleel quoque, qui in umbra Dei significat, personam 286CSpiritus sancti: de quo dicitur. Et virtus Altissimi obumbrabit tibi. Virtus enim Altissimi Spiritus sanctus intelligitur. Unde Dominus dicit: Ego novi virtutem de me exisse (Luc. VIII). Jussio autem, id est verbum, quo tabernaculum fieri jussum est, Filius est, per quem Pater in virtute Spiritus sancti omnia operatur.

VERS. 13.-- Videte, etc. Frustra gloriantur Judaei de legali observatione. Aperte enim dicitur quia signum est futurae, scilicet quietis, et beatitudinis; vel quietis sanctorum in praesenti, quia quiescunt a tumultu mundialium operum et vitiorum.

VERS. 18.-- Duas tabulas (AUG., quaest. 140.) Cum multa locutus sit Deus, duae tantum tabulae dantur Mosi lapideae, quae dicuntur tabulae testimonii; 286Dquia caetera omnia, quae praecepit Deus, ex illis decem praeceptis, quae in duabus scripta sunt tabulis, pendere intelliguntur, si diligenter quaerantur, et bene intelligantur; sicut ipsa decem ex duobus, dilectione, scilicet, Dei et proximi: in quibus scilicet tota lex pendet et prophetae (Matth. XXII).

CHAPTER XXXI.

VERS. 2.-- Beseleel, etc., who is interpreted in the shadow of God. As if he should say, The Lord is my light and my salvation, whom (Psal. LXII). [Vulg. Ps 26:1 Dominus illuminatio mea et salus mea: quem timebo?, the very verse Migne's own next citation names as Psal. XXVI; his Psal. LXII points elsewhere] From him David is said to have drawn his origin, and the name of embroiderer, because Beseleel built the tabernacle with embroidered work.

VERS. 3.-- And I have filled (AUG., quaest. 128.) Whether those works are to be attributed to the gift of the Holy Spirit, which seem to pertain to handicraft? or whether this too was said significatively, so that they pertain to the divine Spirit of wisdom and of understanding and of knowledge, which are signified by these 286Bthings? Then also, whereas here he is said to be filled with the Spirit, that man with the divine Spirit of wisdom and of knowledge, the Holy Spirit is not yet read of.

VERS. VI.-- Oliab, who is interpreted my protection, as though he should say by his name, The Lord is the protector of my life; of whom shall I be afraid (Psal. XXVI)? And well do they build the tabernacle who so constantly trust in the Lord.

(STRAB.) It is to be noted that in the construction of the tabernacle the mystery of the Trinity is shown, as in the baptism and in the transfiguration. For in the baptism the Father sounded forth in the voice: and the Son was baptized; in the appearance of a dove the Spirit appeared. Ooliab also, who is interpreted my protection, father, the person of the Father: Beseleel also, who signifies in the shadow of God, the person 286Cof the Holy Spirit: of whom it is said. And the power of the Most High shall overshadow thee. For by the power of the Most High the Holy Spirit is understood. Whence the Lord says: I know that power has gone out from me (Luc. VIII). But the bidding, that is, the word by which the tabernacle was bidden to be made, is the Son, through whom the Father works all things in the power of the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 13.-- See, etc. In vain do the Jews glory in the legal observance. For it is openly said that it is a sign of that which is to come, namely of rest, and of blessedness; or of the rest of the saints in the present, because they rest from the tumult of worldly works and of vices.

VERS. 18.-- Two tables (AUG., quaest. 140.) Whereas God spoke many things, two tables only of stone are given to Moses, which are called the tables of the testimony; 286Dbecause all the other things which God commanded are understood to hang upon those ten precepts which were written on the two tables, if they be diligently sought out and well understood; as those ten themselves upon two, namely the love of God and of the neighbour: on which, namely, the whole law hangs and the prophets (Matth. XXII). [Vulg. Mt 22:40 In his duobus mandatis universa lex pendet, et prophetae, "On these two commandments dependeth the whole law and the prophets"; Migne has tota for universa]

CAPUT XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- Fac nobis, etc. Deos pluraliter, cum unus tantum vitulus factus sit, ad similitudinem scilicet bovis Apis, quem in Aegypto coluerunt; sed qui idololatriam colit, omnibus vitiis subjicit se, et quasi tot diis et daemonibus quot vitiis servit.

VERS. 2.-- Dixitque ad, etc. (AUG., quaest. 141 287Ain Exod.) Intelligitur Aaron difficilia praecipere, ut hoc modo eos ab illa intentione revocaret. Factum est tamen illud ipsum difficile, ut esset aurum ad faciendum idolum: quod propter eos notandum putavi qui contristantur si quid tale propter vitam aeternam divinitus fieri, vel aequo animo tolerare jubeantur.

VERS. 6.-- Et sedit populus comedere, etc. Esus potusque ad lusum impulit, lusus ad idololatriam: quia si vanitatis culpa non caute compescitur, ab iniquitate mens incauta devoratur. Unde: Qui modica spernit, paulatim decidit. Si enim curare parva negligimus insensibiliter seducti, etiam majora audenter perpetramus.

VERS. 8.-- Isti sunt dii tui. (AUG., quaest. 142.) 287BDominus indicans Moysi quid fecerit populus de vitulo et de idolo quod ex auro suo fecerant, dicit eos dixisse: Hi sunt dii tui, Israel, qui te eduxerunt de terra Aegypti. Quod eos dixisse non legitur; sed animum eorum hunc fuisse Deus ostendit, horum quippe gerebant verborum in corde sententiam, quae Deum latere non poterat.

VERS. 10.-- Dimitte me, etc. (GREG., lib. IX Moral., cap. 11.) Quid est servo dicere, etc., usque ad iram Dei non valuit pro seipso temperare.

(ID., lib. XX Moral., cap. 6.) Aedificare lectorem juxta historiam potest, etc., usque ad nec misericordia disciplinae.

VERS. 13.-- Recordare Abraham, Isaac, etc. Patres, qui diliguntur, inducit pro filiis, qui offendunt, 287Cnec merentur audiri; quia bona sanctorum patrum saepe subveniunt filiis.

VERS. 14.-- Placatusque est Dominus ne faceret malum. (AUG.) Malitiam hic poenam intelligi voluit, sicut est illud: Aestimata est malitia exitus illorum. Secundum hoc dicitur et bonum et malum esse a Deo, non secundum malitiam qua homines mali sunt. Malus enim Deus non est: sed malis ingerit mala, quia justus est.

VERS. 19.-- Iratusque valde. (ID., quaest. 144.) Iratus Moyses, tabulas testimonii digito Dei scriptas fregisse videtur. Magno tamen mysterio figurata est iteratio Testamenti Novi, quia vetus fuerat abolendum, et Novum constituendum. Notandum sane quanta pro populo ad Deum supplicatione laboraverit, 287Dqui tam severus in eos vindicando fuit.

VERS. 20.-- Arripiensque vitulum quem fecerant. (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.) Diaboli corpus significatur in vitulo, etc., usque ad absorptus est.

Et dedit ex eo potum filiis Israel. Tradunt Hebraei quod filii Israel bibentes aquam vituli pulvere infectam, qui commiserant idololatriam in barbis pulverem auri praeferebant, quo etiam signo rei vel immunes sceleris apparebant, et rei interficiebantur.

VERS. 24.-- Et dederunt mihi, etc. (AUG., quaest. 145.) Compendiose locutus est, etc., usque ad non redarguit.

VERS. 25-- Spoliaverat enim eum Aaron in ignominiam 288Asordis et inter hostes, etc. Notandum, quod populus fecit, ipsi Aaron tribuitur, quia consensit ad faciendum quod male petiverat. Magis enim dictum est, nudavit eos Aaron, quia cessit eis, quam nudaverunt se ipsi, qui tantum malum flagitaverunt. Ornamentis quibus fabricatum fuerat idolum, vel quia malam voluntatem prius latentem ostenderat, vel auxilium et protectionem Dei perdiderat.

VERS. 27.-- Ponat vir gladium, etc. (GREG., Past. p. III, c. 26.) Gladium, etc., usque ad Unde subditur:

Occidat unusquisque fratrem suum, etc. Hos interfecit, quos puniendos invenit ab increpationis gladio, nec eis quos per cognationem carnis diligit, parcit. Si ergo ille Dei dicitur, qui ad ferienda vitia 288Bzelo divini amoris excitatur, profecto esse se Dei negat, qui quantum sufficit, vitam carnalium increpare recusat.

VERS. 28.-- Feceruntque, etc. (AUG. in Locutionibus, locut. 124.) Plurale pro singulari posuit, etc., usque ad certisque regulis istae locutiones interponuntur.

VERS. 31.-- Aut dimitte eis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 147 in Exod.) Securus quidem hoc dicit, etc., usque ad ut pro eis illa verba Deo funderet.

VERS. 35.-- Quem fecit Aaron. (AUG. quaest. 148.) Quaeritur cum superius populum nudasse dictus sit Aaron, etc., usque ad quod Apostolus dicit exclamans: Quam inscrutabilia enim sunt judicia ejus: etc. (Rom. XI).

CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- Make us, etc. Gods in the plural, although one calf only was made, after the likeness, namely, of the ox Apis, whom they worshipped in Egypt; but he who cultivates idolatry subjects himself to all vices, and serves as many gods and demons as vices.

VERS. 2.-- And Aaron said to, etc. (AUG., quaest. 141 287Ain Exod.) Aaron is understood to command hard things, that in this way he might call them back from that purpose. Yet that very hard thing was done, that there might be gold for the making of the idol: which I thought was to be noted on account of those who are grieved if they are bidden to have any such thing divinely done for eternal life, or to bear it with an even mind.

VERS. 6.-- And the people sat down to eat, etc. [Vulg. Ex 32:6 sedit populus manducare; Migne prints comedere] Eating and drinking drove them to play, and play to idolatry: because if the fault of vanity is not warily restrained, the unwary mind is devoured by iniquity. Whence: He that despises little things, falls little by little. [Vulg. Sir 19:1 qui spernit modica paulatim decidet, "he that despiseth small things shall fall by little and little", a future; Migne has the present decidit, and reverses the order of the first two words] For if we neglect to care for small things, being insensibly seduced, we boldly perpetrate even greater ones.

VERS. 8.-- These are thy gods. (AUG., quaest. 142.) 287BThe Lord, showing to Moses what the people had done concerning the calf and concerning the idol which they had made out of their own gold, says that they had said: These are thy gods, O Israel, who brought thee out of the land of Egypt. Which it is not read that they said; but God showed that this was their mind, for they bore in their heart the sense of these words, which could not lie hid from God.

VERS. 10.-- Let me alone, etc. (GREG., lib. IX Moral., cap. 11.) What is it to say to a servant, etc., as far as he could not temper the anger of God for himself.

(ID., lib. XX Moral., cap. 6.) It can build up the reader according to the history, etc., as far as nor mercy of discipline.

VERS. 13.-- Remember Abraham, Isaac, etc. He brings in the fathers, who are loved, on behalf of the sons, who offend, 287Cand do not deserve to be heard; because the good things of the holy fathers often come to the aid of the sons.

VERS. 14.-- And the Lord was appeased that he would not do the evil. (AUG.) By malice he wished punishment to be understood here, as is that: The malice of their going out was esteemed. According to this both good and evil are said to be from God, not according to the malice by which men are evil. For God is not evil: but he brings evils upon the evil, because he is just.

VERS. 19.-- And being very angry. (ID., quaest. 144.) Moses, being angry, seems to have broken the tables of the testimony written with the finger of God. Yet by a great mystery the repetition of the New Testament was figured, because the old was to be abolished, and the New to be established. It is indeed to be noted with what great supplication he laboured to God for the people, 287Dwho was so severe in taking vengeance upon them.

VERS. 20.-- And catching the calf which they had made. (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.) The body of the devil is signified in the calf, etc., as far as he was swallowed up.

And he gave it to the children of Israel to drink. The Hebrews hand down that, when the children of Israel drank the water infected with the dust of the calf, those who had committed idolatry bore the dust of the gold upon their beards, by which sign also the guilty or those free of the crime appeared, and the guilty were slain.

VERS. 24.-- And they gave me, etc. (AUG., quaest. 145.) He spoke compendiously, etc., as far as he does not refute.

VERS. 25.-- For Aaron had stripped him unto the ignominy 288Aof filth and among his enemies, etc. [Vulg. Ex 32:25 spoliaverat enim eum Aaron propter ignominiam sordis, et inter hostes nudum constituerat, "for Aaron had stripped him by reason of the ignominy of filth, and had set him naked among his enemies"; Migne has in ignominiam] It is to be noted that what the people did is attributed to Aaron himself, because he consented to the doing of what they had ill demanded. For it is rather said, Aaron stripped them, because he yielded to them, than that they stripped themselves, who demanded so great an evil. With the ornaments out of which the idol had been fashioned, either because he had shown their evil will which lay hid before, or because he had lost the help and protection of God.

VERS. 27.-- Let every man put his sword, etc. (GREG., Past. p. III, c. 26.) The sword, etc., as far as Whence it is subjoined:

Let every man kill his brother, etc. He killed those whom he found to be punished by the sword of rebuke, and he does not spare those whom he loves by kinship of the flesh. If therefore he is called of God who is stirred 288Bby the zeal of divine love to smite vices, assuredly he denies himself to be of God who, so far as suffices, refuses to rebuke the life of the carnal.

VERS. 28.-- And they did, etc. (AUG. in Locutionibus, locut. 124.) He put the plural for the singular, etc., as far as and these manners of speech are interposed by fixed rules.

VERS. 31.-- Or forgive them, etc. (AUG., quaest. 147 in Exod.) He says this indeed securely, etc., as far as that he might pour out those words to God on their behalf.

VERS. 35.-- Which Aaron made. (AUG. quaest. 148.) It is asked: since Aaron was said above to have stripped the people, etc., as far as that which the Apostle says, crying out: For how unsearchable are his judgments: etc. (Rom. XI). [Vulg. Rom 11:33 quam incomprehensibilia sunt judicia ejus, "how incomprehensible are his judgments," and without enim; Migne has inscrutabilia] [Vulg. Ex 32:35 quem fecerat Aaron, a pluperfect; Migne has the perfect fecit]

CAPUT XXXIII.

  • 288C

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque, etc. (AUG., quaest. 149 in Exod.) Deus iratus, etc., usque ad voce terrebat.

Vade, etc. (ID., quaest. 150.) Continuo tanquam ad ipsum Mosen, etc., usque ad a facie Domini.

VERS. 2.-- Mittam praecursorem. (AUG., quaest. 150.) Deus cum justis est visitando, miserando, et confortando: cum impiis vero, ulciscendo et puniendo. Et est sensus: Cum his non possum esse propter te, ut vindicem et puniam; nec propter eos, ut visitem et confortem: mittam angelum meum saltem.

VERS. 4.-- Audiens populus. (RAB.) Pessimum 288Dsermonem dicit audivisse populum, hoc est, crudelissimum atque moestissimum. Quod enim majus alicui praestari potest beneficium, quam Dei gratiam habere praesentem, ac conditorem suum agnoscere sibi esse propitium; aut quod majus est discrimen, quam auctoris sufferre iram? Nihil habet boni, qui creatoris sui carebit gratia atque praesentia.

VERS. 7.-- Tollens tabernaculum, etc. Hoc per prolepsim accipiendum, si de illo tabernaculo accipitur, quod Moysi in monte monstratum est, et fabricatum, praecipiente Domino, a Beseleel et Ooliab. Vel aliud tabernaculum fuit, ad quod jam conveniebant ad judicandum.

289AVERS. 8.-- Cumque egrederetur, etc. (GREG., hom. 13 in Ezech.) Hebraeus populus de Aegyptiaca servitute liberatus, etc., usque ad progredi conantur.

Aspiciebantque, etc. (STRAB.) Moyses tenet figuram legis. Moysi ergo tergum populus Judaicus aspiciebat, quia nihil mysticum, nihil spirituale in lege voluit intelligere, sed superficiem tantum litterae. Ideo ejus facies abscondita erat, id est, vera et mera cognitio. Ingresso illo tabernaculum, descendebat columna nubis, et stabat ad ostium. Alia translatio dicit quod Moysi volenti ingredi tabernaculum, opponebat se nubes ante et prohibebat eum. Hic autem dicitur, quod illo ingresso loquebatur nubes ante ostium cum eo. Sciendum ergo quod aliquam 289Bpartem ingrediebatur, atrium scilicet, et tunc antequam intraret in tabernaculum, opponebat se nubes: hoc ergo loco significat eos qui minus sunt perfecti scientia et opere, nec valent intima scripturarum penetrare. Alibi autem quando medium nebulae ingressus est, perfectorum figuram tenuit, scilicet qui mysticorum sensuum arcana investigant.

Loquebatur autem Dominus. Secundum opinionem populi loquitur Scriptura, qui putabat Moysen ore ad os loqui cum Deo, cum per subjectam creaturam, id est per angelum et nubem ei loqueretur et appareret. Nam ejus substantiam nec hominum nec angelorum (sicut est) quisquam videre potuit, unde: Regi autem saeculorum immortali, invisibili, etc. Et alibi: Lucem habitat inaccessibilem, etc. (I Tim. 289CI, VI).

(GREG., in I Reg., cap. 10.) Facies Dei cognitio ejus est. Cognoscitur autem Deus per speculum, cognoscitur per seipsum. Per speculum hic, per semetipsum in coelo. Speculum vero est illa lux quam habitat Deus: ipse autem lux illa quae ipse est.

Si ergo inveni gratiam, etc. (AUG., quaest. 151.) His verbis ostendit Moyses, etc., usque ad ut se mundos et inviolatos custodiant et proficiant.

VERS. 17.-- Et teipsum novi, etc. (AUG., quaest. 153.) Quoniam scio te prae omnibus, etc. Nunquid Deus plus aliqua scit et aliqua minus? etc., usque ad sed diligentius requirendum est in prioribus Scripturae 289Dpartibus an vere sit ita.

VERS. 19.-- Ego ostendam. (AUG., quaest. 154.) Cum dixisset Moyses ad Dominum, etc., usque ad vocatur Dominus Christus in omnibus gentibus.

Et vocabo. (AUG., quaest. 154.) Vocabo autem dixit, non vocabor, activum verbum pro passivo ponens, genere locutionis inusitato, in quo forte significare voluit seipsum hoc facere, id est, gratia sua fieri, ut vocetur Dominus in omnibus gentibus.

Et miserebor, etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Quod addidit, etc., usque ad sic Deus pronuntiavit misericordiam se esse facturum.

(AUG., lib. de Praedestinatione et Gratia, cap. 10.) 290AA Deo homines nullis praecedentibus meritis vocari, etc., usque ad si deserat qui vocavit.

Non poteris, etc. (AUG., quaest. 154.) Post misericordiae suae commendationem, etc., usque ad huic vitae moriendum est.

VERS. 20.-- Non enim, etc. (GREG., lib. XVIII Moral., cap. 37, tom. I.) Joannes quoque ait: etc., usque ad quia satietas ex desiderio semper accenditur.

VERS. 21.-- Ecce, inquit, locus est apud me, et stabis supra, etc. (AUG., quaest. 154.) Item interposito articulo dicente Scriptura, etc., usque ad stetit populus fidelis supra petram.

(GREG., lib. XXV Moral., cap. 16.) Est locus apud me, etc. Locus Ecclesia. Petra Dominus, Moyses 290BJudaica plebs, quae Domino praedicante in terra non credidit. Ipsa ergo in petra stetit terga Domini transeuntis aspiciens, quia, scilicet post passionem ascensionemque Domini intra Ecclesiam deducta per fidem Christum percipere meruit, et cujus praesentiam non vidit, ejus posteriora cognovit.

(GREG. ibid.) Est locus apud me, etc. Ecclesia scilicet, etc., usque ad De qua soliditate dicitur: Super hanc petram aedificabo Ecclesiam.

VERS. 22.-- Cumque transibit gloria mea, etc. (EUCHERIUS, lib. de Form. spirit.) Sed et transire dicitur Deus, cum de cordibus quorumdam hominum, in quibus ante per fidem credebatur, postea surrepente perfidia vel quolibet delicto, ab eis recedit et ad alios transit, quemadmodum de Judaeis ad gentes, 290Cde haereticis ad catholicos, et de quibuslibet irreligiosis ad religiosos.

Et protegam (AUG., ubi supra. ) et tegam manu mea super te, etc., usque ad deinde contexit quid Dominus postea dicat: Excide tibi duas tabulas lapideas, etc.

CHAPTER XXXIII.

  • 288C

VERS. 1.-- And he spoke, etc. (AUG., quaest. 149 in Exod.) God being angered, etc., as far as he terrified them with his voice.

Go, etc. (ID., quaest. 150.) Straightway, as though to Moses himself, etc., as far as from the face of the Lord.

VERS. 2.-- I will send a forerunner. (AUG., quaest. 150.) God is with the just by visiting, by showing mercy, and by strengthening; but with the impious by avenging and by punishing. And the sense is this: With these I cannot be for thy sake, so as to avenge and to punish; nor for their sake, so as to visit and to strengthen: I will at least send my angel.

VERS. 4.-- The people hearing. (RAB.) He says that the people heard a most evil 288Dword, that is, a most cruel and most sorrowful one. For what greater benefit can be bestowed upon anyone than to have God's grace present, and to acknowledge his Maker to be favourable to him; or what greater peril is there than to endure the wrath of his Author? He has nothing of good who shall lack the grace and the presence of his Creator.

VERS. 7.-- Taking the tabernacle, etc. This is to be taken by way of prolepsis, if it is taken of that tabernacle which was shown to Moses in the mount, and made, the Lord enjoining it, by Beseleel and Ooliab. Or else it was another tabernacle, to which they were already assembling for judgment.

289AVERS. 8.-- And when he went out, etc. (GREG., hom. 13 in Ezech.) The Hebrew people, freed from Egyptian servitude, etc., as far as they endeavour to go forward.

And they looked, etc. (STRAB.) Moses holds the figure of the law. The Jewish people therefore looked upon the back of Moses, because it willed to understand nothing mystical, nothing spiritual in the law, but only the surface of the letter. Therefore his face was hidden, that is, the true and pure knowledge. When he had entered the tabernacle, the pillar of cloud came down and stood at the door. Another rendering says that when Moses wished to enter the tabernacle, the cloud set itself before him and hindered him. Here, however, it is said that when he had entered, the cloud spoke with him before the door. It is to be known, therefore, that he was entering 289Bsome part, namely the court, and then, before he entered into the tabernacle, the cloud set itself against him: in this place, therefore, it signifies those who are less perfect in knowledge and in work, and are not strong enough to penetrate the inmost things of the Scriptures. But elsewhere, when he entered into the midst of the cloud, he held the figure of the perfect, namely of those who search out the secret things of the mystical senses.

And the Lord spoke. Scripture speaks according to the opinion of the people, who thought that Moses spoke mouth to mouth with God, whereas he was speaking and appearing to him through a subject creature, that is, through an angel and a cloud. For his substance neither of men nor of angels (as it is) could anyone see, whence: But to the King of ages, immortal, invisible, etc. And elsewhere: He dwelleth in light inaccessible, etc. (I Tim. 289CI, VI).

(GREG., in I Reg., cap. 10.) The face of God is the knowledge of him. Now God is known through a glass, and is known through himself. Through a glass here, through himself in heaven. But the glass is that light which God inhabits; and he himself is that light which he himself is.

If therefore I have found grace, etc. (AUG., quaest. 151.) By these words Moses shows, etc., as far as that they may keep themselves clean and inviolate, and may go forward.

VERS. 17.-- And thee thyself I have known, etc. (AUG., quaest. 153.) Because I know thee above all men, etc. [Vulg. Ex 33:17 reads ex nomine, "by name"; Migne prints Augustine's prae omnibus, "above all," and the question that follows is put only because of it] Does God know some things more and some things less? etc., as far as but it is to be sought more diligently in the earlier 289Dparts of Scripture whether it be truly so.

VERS. 19.-- I will show. (AUG., quaest. 154.) When Moses had said to the Lord, etc., as far as the Lord Christ is called upon among all nations.

And I will call. (AUG., quaest. 154.) I will call, however, he said, not "I shall be called," putting an active verb for a passive, by an unwonted manner of speech, in which perhaps he willed to signify that he himself does this, that is, that it comes about by his grace that the Lord is called upon among all nations.

And I will have mercy, etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) That which he added, etc., as far as thus God declared that he would show mercy.

(AUG., lib. de Praedestinatione et Gratia, cap. 10.) 290AThat men are called by God with no merits going before, etc., as far as if he who called should forsake them.

Thou shalt not be able, etc. (AUG., quaest. 154.) After the commendation of his mercy, etc., as far as one must die to this life.

VERS. 20.-- For not, etc. (GREG., lib. XVIII Moral., cap. 37, tom. I.) John also says: etc., as far as because satiety is ever kindled out of desire.

VERS. 21.-- Behold, he says, there is a place with me, and thou shalt stand upon, etc. (AUG., quaest. 154.) Again, when Scripture interposes the article and says, etc., as far as the faithful people stood upon the rock.

(GREG., lib. XXV Moral., cap. 16.) There is a place with me, etc. The place is the Church. The rock is the Lord; Moses, 290Bthe Jewish people, which did not believe the Lord preaching upon earth. It therefore stood upon the rock, beholding the back parts of the Lord as he passed by, because, that is, after the passion and ascension of the Lord, being led within the Church, it deserved to receive Christ by faith, and of him whose presence it did not see, it came to know the hinder parts.

(GREG. ibid.) There is a place with me, etc. The Church, namely, etc., as far as Of which solidity it is said: Upon this rock I will build the Church.

VERS. 22.-- And when my glory shall pass, etc. (EUCHERIUS, lib. de Form. spirit.) But God is said also to pass over, when out of the hearts of certain men, in which he was before believed by faith, afterwards, faithlessness creeping in or some offence whatever, he withdraws from them and passes over to others: as from the Jews to the Gentiles, 290Cfrom heretics to Catholics, and from any irreligious persons whatever to the religious.

And I will protect (AUG., ubi supra. ) and I will cover with my hand over thee, etc. [Vulg. Ex 33:22 protegam dextera mea, donec transeam, "I will protect with my right hand, till I pass by"; Migne prints Augustine's wording tegam manu mea super te], as far as then he weaves in what the Lord says afterwards: Hew thee two tables of stone, etc. [Vulg. Ex 34:1 Praecide … tibi duas tabulas lapideas, "Cut thee two tables of stone"; Migne prints Excide, "hew out"]

CAPUT XXXIV.

VERS. 7.-- Qui reddis iniquitatem patrum filiis ac nepotibus iu tertiam et quartam, etc., (GREG., lib. XV Moral., cap. 22.) Cum scriptum sit, etc., usque ad qui viderunt quod male sequerentur.

VERS. 14.-- Dominus zelotes. (AUG., quaest. 158 in Exod.) Dominus Deus enim zelans, etc., usque ad et perdit omnem qui fornicatur a se.

VERS. 20.-- Primogenitum asini, etc. (ISID.) Per 290Dasinum immunditia, per ovem innocentia designatur. Asini ergo primogenita ove mutare, est immundae vitae primordia ad innocentiae simplicitatem convertere, ut postquam illa peccator egit, quae ut immunda Dominus respuit, ea jam agendo proferat, quae Dei sacrificio munda imponat.

VERS. 21.-- Die septimo. (AUG., quaest. 160.) Cum de Sabbato praeciperet, etc., usque ad omni tempore sabbato debere cessari.

VERS. 24.-- Nullus insidiabitur (AUG., quaest. 161.) Quod dixit, etc., usque ad ubi tabernaculum vel templum Deus fuerat habiturus.

VERS. 25.-- Non immolabis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 162 et 163.) Quid est quod ait: Non occides super 291Afermentum sanguinem immolatorum meorum? An illa hoc loco dicit immolata sua, quae per pascha occiduntur, et praecipit ne tunc sit in domo fermentum, quando sunt dies azymorum?

VERS. 26.-- Non coques haedum in lacte. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Non coques agnum in lacte matris suae, etc., usque ad sed historica narratio est.

(ISID.) Moyses quadraginta diebus jejunavit, et Elias, et ipse Dominus. Praecipitur enim nobis ex lege, ex prophetis et Evangelio. Quod testimonium ex his habet. Unde in monte inter utramque personam medius Salvator effulsit, ut ab omnibus mundi illecebris aviditatem nostram tanquam jejunio temperantiae refrenemus, quandiu perfectio Decalogi per quatuor ejusdem mundi partes, id est toti orbi praedicatur, 291But decem quater ducta quadragenarium numerum signent.

VERS. 28.-- Et scripsit in tabulis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 166.) De Moyse dictum est quod ipse scripsit, etc., usque ad sed per gratiam Dei.

VERS. 29.-- Cumque descenderet. (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.) Ascendit Moyses denuo in montem, etc., usque ad cujus imago fuit in tabulis.

(ISID. ibid.) Descendente Moyse cum tabulis, etc., usque ad unde: Revela oculos meos et considerabo mirabilia de lege tua (Psal. CXVIII).

VERS. 30.-- Videns autem Aaron, etc. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Exod.) Scriptum est quia vidit Aaron et omnes filii Israel Moysen, etc., usque ad habent enim gloriam ea quae loquitur, sed tegitur et occultatur, 291Cet omnis gloria ejus intrinsecus.

(ORIG. ibid.) Illud quoque intuere quod in lege vultus Moysi glorificatur, licet velamine contegatur, etc., usque ad de servitute saeculi liberati libertatem scientiae requiramus.

VERS. 34.-- Et tunc loquebatur ad filios Isael omnia quae sibi fuerant, etc. (AUG., quaest. 172 in Exod.) Postquam descendit Moyses de monte, etc., usque ad ut hoc nomen acciperent.

CHAPTER XXXIV.

VERS. 7.-- Who renderest the iniquity of the fathers to the children and to the grandchildren unto iu the third and fourth, etc., (GREG., lib. XV Moral., cap. 22.) Since it is written, etc., as far as who saw that they were following after evil.

VERS. 14.-- The Lord is jealous. (AUG., quaest. 158 in Exod.) For the Lord God is jealous, etc., as far as and he destroys every one that goeth a-fornicating from him.

VERS. 20.-- The firstling of the ass, etc. (ISID.) By 290Dthe ass uncleanness is designated, by the sheep innocence. To exchange, therefore, the firstlings of the ass for a sheep is to turn the first beginnings of an unclean life to the simplicity of innocence, so that, after the sinner has done those things which the Lord rejects as unclean, he may now by his doing bring forth such things as he may lay clean upon the sacrifice of God.

VERS. 21.-- On the seventh day. (AUG., quaest. 160.) When he was giving precept concerning the Sabbath, etc., as far as that at every season there must be a resting on the sabbath.

VERS. 24.-- No man shall lie in wait (AUG., quaest. 161.) That which he said, etc., as far as where God was to have his tabernacle or temple.

VERS. 25.-- Thou shalt not immolate, etc. (AUG., quaest. 162 et 163.) What is it that he says: Thou shalt not slay upon 291Aleaven the blood of my immolated things? [Vulg. Ex 34:25 Non immolabis super fermento sanguinem hostiae meae, "Thou shalt not offer the blood of my victim upon leaven"; Migne's immolatorum meorum, "my immolated things," is the very wording the question that follows is built upon] Or does he here call those things his immolated things which are slain at the pasch, and give precept that there be no leaven in the house at that time, when the days of unleavened bread are come?

VERS. 26.-- Thou shalt not boil a kid in milk. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Thou shalt not boil a lamb in the milk of its mother, etc., as far as but it is a historical narrative.

(ISID.) Moses fasted forty days, and Elias, and the Lord himself. For it is enjoined upon us out of the law, out of the prophets, and out of the Gospel. Which testimony it has from these. Whence upon the mount the Saviour shone forth in the midst between the two persons, that we may bridle our greediness away from all the allurements of the world as by the fast of temperance, so long as the perfection of the Decalogue is preached through the four parts of that same world, that is, to the whole globe, 291Bso that ten multiplied four times may signify the number of forty.

VERS. 28.-- And he wrote upon the tables, etc. (AUG., quaest. 166.) It was said of Moses that he himself wrote, etc., as far as but by the grace of God.

VERS. 29.-- And when he came down. (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.) Moses went up again into the mount, etc., as far as whose image was in the tables.

(ISID. ibid.) When Moses came down with the tables, etc., as far as whence: Open thou mine eyes, and I will consider the wondrous things of thy law (Psal. CXVIII).

VERS. 30.-- But Aaron seeing, etc. [Vulg. Ex 34:30 Videntes autem Aaron et filii Israel cornutam Moysi faciem, "But Aaron and the children of Israel seeing the horned face of Moses"; Migne prints a singular participle, and the gloss beneath it has them see Moysen, Moses himself, not his horned face — which is the reading Origen's homily is built upon] (ORIG., hom. 12 in Exod.) It is written that Aaron and all the children of Israel saw Moses, etc., as far as for the things which he speaks have glory, but it is covered and hidden, 291Cand all his glory is within.

(ORIG. ibid.) Consider this also, that in the law the countenance of Moses is glorified, although it be covered with a veil, etc., as far as that we, freed from the servitude of the world, may seek after the liberty of knowledge.

VERS. 34.-- And then he spoke to the children of Israel Isael all things which had been to him, etc. (AUG., quaest. 172 in Exod.) After Moses came down from the mount, etc., as far as that they might receive this name.

CAPUT XXXV.

VERS. 5.-- Separate. (ORIG., hom. 13 in Exod.) Alia littera: Sumite a vobis ipsis redemptionem Domino, etc., usque ad et argentum tuum, id est sermo tuus, probatum.

291D(ID.) Nostis, qui divinis mysteriis interesse consuevistis, etc., usque ad haec omnia quae in nobis sunt faciunt.

Omnis. (ID.) Alia littera: Unusquisque vestrum, inquit, etc., usque ad ita si veniens dominus inveniat aliquid tuum in tabernaculo suo, sibi te defendit, et suum dicit.

Aes. (ID.) Pro fortitudine poni videtur, etc., usque ad Omnia ergo offerantur Deo, sensus, sermo et vox.

VERS. 6.-- Hyacinthum. (ID.) Videamus caetera, hyacinthum, etc., usque ad et offeretis primitias Domino.

Coccumque bis tinctum. (ID. homil. 13 in Exod.) Videamus quare coccus duplicatur, etc., usque ad ut 292Acum scientiae lumine igniculum severitatis admisceat.

(ID.) Si quis digne intelligat exitum Hebraeorum de Aegypto, etc., usque ad et nobiliora designat indumenta: Induite vos Dominum Jesum Christum, et carnis curam ne feceritis in deliciis (Rom. XIII).

(GREG., ubi supra. ) In constructione tabernaculi, etc., usque ad quasi sine luce sonitum dederunt.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) Post haec viri acceperunt a mulieribus suis, etc., usque ad unde: Non omnes capiunt verbum istud, sed quibus datum est (Matth. XIX).

VERS. 6.-- Pilosque caprarum. (ORIG. hom. 19.) Pili caprarum offeruntur, etc., usque ad qui Domino offert pelles arietum.

VERS. 21.-- Quidquid ad cultum, etc. (GREG., ubi 292Bsupra. ) In ornamento tabernaculi viri cum mulieribus dona offerunt, etc., usque ad prout ipse dederit, proferuntur.

VERS. 27.-- Principes vero obtulerunt lapides, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Principes obtulerunt dona sua, etc., usque ad ut praeesse populis mereantur.

(ORIG. hom. 9 in Exod.) Offerunt etiam principes oleum lucernis et chrismati profuturum, etc., usque ad cum te Dominus in loco viridi collocaverit super aquam refectionis.

VERS. 30.-- Beseleel. (RAB. in Exod., tom. II.) Umbra dicitur, etc., usque ad in justitia et judicio fratres suos exhortando roborant.

CHAPTER XXXV.

VERS. 5.-- Set apart. (ORIG., hom. 13 in Exod.) Another reading: Take from your own selves a redemption for the Lord, etc., as far as and thy silver, that is, thy word, tried.

291D(ID.) You know, who are wont to be present at the divine mysteries, etc., as far as all these things which are in us do them.

Every one. (ID.) Another reading: Each one of you, he says, etc., as far as so, if the master at his coming find anything of thine in his tabernacle, he claims thee for himself, and calls thee his own.

Brass. (ID.) It seems to be put for strength, etc., as far as Let all things therefore be offered to God: sense, speech, and voice.

VERS. 6.-- Hyacinth. (ID.) Let us see the rest: hyacinth, etc., as far as and you shall offer firstfruits to the Lord.

And scarlet twice dyed. (ID. homil. 13 in Exod.) Let us see why the scarlet is doubled, etc., as far as that 292Awith the light of knowledge he may mingle a little fire of severity.

(ID.) If any man worthily understand the going out of the Hebrews from Egypt, etc., as far as and it designates nobler garments: Put you on the Lord Jesus Christ, and make not provision for the flesh in its delights (Rom. XIII). [Vulg. Rom 13:14 induamini … in desideriis, "put ye on … in its desires"; Migne prints Induite vos and in deliciis, "in its delights"]

(GREG., ubi supra. ) In the construction of the tabernacle, etc., as far as they gave forth a sound as it were without light.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) After these things the men received from their wives, etc., as far as whence: All men take not this word, but they to whom it is given (Matth. XIX).

VERS. 6.-- And goats' hair. (ORIG. hom. 19.) Goats' hair is offered, etc., as far as he who offers to the Lord the skins of rams.

VERS. 21.-- Whatsoever for the service, etc. (GREG., ubi 292Bsupra. ) In the adorning of the tabernacle the men together with the women offer gifts, etc., as far as they are brought forth according as he himself shall have given.

VERS. 27.-- But the princes offered stones, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) The princes offered their gifts, etc., as far as that they may deserve to be set over the peoples.

(ORIG. hom. 9 in Exod.) The princes offer also oil that shall serve for the lamps and for the chrism, etc., as far as when the Lord shall have set thee in a green place, upon the water of refreshment.

VERS. 30.-- Beseleel. (RAB. in Exod., tom. II.) He is called a shadow, etc., as far as they strengthen their brethren by exhorting them in justice and judgment.

CAPUT XXXVI.

292CVERS. 1.-- Fecit ergo Beseleel, etc. (RAB., in Exod. ) Notandum quod non solum de tribu regali, etc., usque ad ad corporis sui unitatem voluit pertinere.

VERS. 2.-- Cumque vocasset eos, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Et postquam obtulit populus, vocavit, inquit Moyses, etc., usque ad et in suo loco aptare debeas et proferre?

Et qui sponte, etc. (AUG., quaest. 170 in Exod.) Et omnes qui sponte vellent ire ad opera, ut consummarent ea, etc. Commemoravit Moyses quosdam, etc., usque ad sed liberaliter et sponte devotus.

VERS. 4.-- Unde artifices, etc. (AUG., quaest. 171). Nota quia sapientes effectores operum sancti etiam moribus erant: poterant autem, si vellent, multa auferre; sed modestia prohibuit, vel religio terruit.

CHAPTER XXXVI.

292CVERS. 1.-- Beseleel therefore made, etc. (RAB., in Exod. ) It is to be noted that not only from the royal tribe, etc., as far as he willed them to belong to the unity of his own body.

VERS. 2.-- And when he had called them, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) And after the people had offered, Moses, he says, called, etc., as far as and oughtest thou to fit it in its own place and bring it forth?

And they who willingly, etc. (AUG., quaest. 170 in Exod.) And all who of their own will would go to the works, that they might finish them, etc. [Vulg. Ex 36:2 et qui sponte sua obtulerant se ad faciendum opus, "and who had of their own accord offered themselves to do the work"; Migne sets the Vulgate lemma and Augustine's vellent ire ad opera side by side] Moses made mention of certain men, etc., as far as but liberally and of his own accord devoted.

VERS. 4.-- Whereupon the craftsmen, etc. (AUG., quaest. 171). Note that the wise workers of the works were holy also in their manners: yet they could, if they had willed, have carried off much; but modesty forbade, or religion made them afraid.

CAPUT XXXVII.

  • 292D

VERS. 1.-- Fecit autem Beseleel arcam. (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.) In arca testamenti fuit urna aurea, etc., usque ad immortalis memoriae semper viriditate florentem.

VERS. 2.-- Coronam auream, etc. Quia in eo quod Ecclesia per quatuor mundi partes dilatatur, quatuor sancti Evangelii libris accincta praedicatur, quae tamen intra unius coronae ambitum, id est intra ejusdem fidei firmitatem, id est unitatem, concluditur.

VERS. 4.-- Vectes quoque. (ISID., ibid.) Quia fortes perseverantesque doctores velut imputribilia ligna quaerendi sunt, qui instructioni sacrorum voluminum 293Asemper inhaerentes, Ecclesiae unitatem denuntient, et quasi intromissis circulis arcam portent; qui auro quoque jubentur operiri, ut cum sermone aliis insonant, ipsi etiam vitae splendore fulgescant.

VERS. 6.-- Fecit et propitiatorium, etc. (ISID., ibid.) Propitiatorium super arcam testamenti, etc., usque ad quia Christus est Ecclesiae caput.

VERS. 17.-- Fecitque candelabrum, etc. Quod Spiritum sanctum significat, qui septiformi gratia illustrat Ecclesiam in unitate fidei consistentem, vel Christum portantem ecclesias septem, in quibus septiformis Spiritus splendor emicat.

VERS. 23.-- Cum emunctoriis. Quae in Isaia forcipes appellantur, duo Testamenta, scilicet quibus peccata purgantur, quaeque intra se sancti Spiritus 293Bunione sociantur.

CHAPTER XXXVII.

  • 292D

VERS. 1.-- And Beseleel made the ark. (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.) In the ark of the testament there was a golden urn, etc., as far as flourishing always with the greenness of immortal memory.

VERS. 2.-- A golden crown, etc. Because in that the Church is spread abroad through the four parts of the world, she is preached as girt about with the four books of the holy Gospel, which nevertheless is enclosed within the compass of one crown, that is, within the firmness of the same faith, that is, its unity.

VERS. 4.-- The bars also. (ISID., ibid.) Because strong and persevering teachers are to be sought out as timbers that cannot rot, who, ever cleaving to the instruction of the sacred volumes, 293Amay proclaim the unity of the Church, and, as it were with rings let in, may carry the ark; and they are commanded to be overlaid with gold also, that while they sound in the ears of others with their word, they themselves may also shine with the splendour of their life.

VERS. 6.-- He made also the propitiatory, etc. (ISID., ibid.) The propitiatory above the ark of the testament, etc., as far as because Christ is the head of the Church.

VERS. 17.-- And he made the candlestick, etc. Which signifies the Holy Spirit, who with sevenfold grace enlightens the Church that stands in the unity of faith; or Christ bearing the seven churches, in which the splendour of the sevenfold Spirit shines forth.

VERS. 23.-- With the snuffers. Which in Isaias are called tongs: namely the two Testaments, by which sins are purged, and which are joined together within themselves by the union of the Holy 293BSpirit.

CAPUT XXXVIII.

VERS. 8.-- Fecit et labrum aeneum, etc. (GREG., hom. 17 in Evang.) Labrum aeneum Moyses ponit, etc., usque ad ut per haec proficere contendamus, per quae profecisse cognovimus quos veneramur.

VERS. 20.-- Paxillos quoque. (STRAB.) Paxilli secundum litteram erant in summitate columnarum fixi, quibus funes religabantur: qui cortinas a terra sublevabant, firmati superius quibusdam ansulis, inferius vero religati ad praedictos paxillos cortinas extendebant, ne rugam contraherent.

Mystice vero funes significant sanctam Scripturam: de qua Salomon ait: Funiculus triplex difficile rumpitur 293C(Eccle. IV): quia sancta Scriptura, historia, moralitate et allegoria constans, nullis haereticorum dogmatibus potest disrumpi. Paxilli in modum linguae facti linguas doctorum significant qui Scripturam exponunt. Cortinas etiam a terra sublevant et extendunt: quia subjectos docent sequi, et imitari coelestia, nullamque mali operis vel fraudis rugam contrahere (Ephes. V).

CHAPTER XXXVIII.

VERS. 8.-- He made also a brazen laver, etc. (GREG., hom. 17 in Evang.) Moses sets a brazen laver, etc., as far as that we may strive to go forward by those things by which we know that they whom we venerate went forward.

VERS. 20.-- The pins also. (STRAB.) The pins, according to the letter, were fixed at the top of the pillars, to which the cords were bound: which cords raised the curtains from the ground, and, being made fast above by certain little loops but below bound to the aforesaid pins, stretched out the curtains, that they might not draw into a wrinkle.

But mystically the cords signify Holy Scripture, of which Solomon says: A threefold cord is hardly broken 293C(Eccle. IV): because Holy Scripture, consisting of history, morality and allegory, can be broken asunder by no doctrines of heretics. The pins, made after the manner of a tongue, signify the tongues of the teachers who expound Scripture. They raise the curtains also from the ground and stretch them out: because they teach their subjects to follow and to imitate heavenly things, and to draw no wrinkle of evil work or of deceit (Ephes. V).

CAPUT XXXIX.

. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

CHAPTER XXXIX.

. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

CAPUT XL.

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque est Dominus ad Moysen dicens: Mense primo, etc. (STRAB.) Primus anni mensis ipse est Nisan, qui et Aprilis, in quo Judaei 293Dpascha celebrabant, et tabernaculum erectum est. Allegorice autem primus mensis signat initium gratiae quando tabernaculum Dei, id est Ecclesia erecta est, cum Christus, scilicet crucifixus de latere suo produxit mysteria sanguinis et aquae, quibus Ecclesia dedicatur et consecratur. Et notandum quia hic primus mensis secundi anni fuit: primus enim annus ante legem fuit, secundus annus sub gratia Evangelii, cui Dominus benedixit. Unde: Benedices coronae anni benignitatis tuae (Psal. LXIV). Annus enim benignitatis fuit, quo salus humani generis per humanitatem Christi apparuit: hoc ergo anno tabernaculum Dei erectum est, id est Ecclesia fundata et exaltata.

294A(RAB. in Exod., tom. II.) Primus annus omne tempus legis, etc., usque ad ut doctores Evangelii his utantur in confirmatione fidei.

VERS. 9.-- Unges tabernaculum, etc. (STRAB.) Tabernaculum cum vasis suis unctionis oleo consecratur; quia sancta Ecclesia quidquid agit cum virtutibus suis debet dedicare et consecrare gratiae Spiritus sancti. Oleum quippe unctionis spirituali gratiae comparatur, qua omnium virtutum opera Domino consecrantur.

VERS. 12.-- Applicabisque Aaron, etc. (STRAB.) Fores tabernaculi, id est, sanctae Ecclesiae, sunt apostoli, per quos debet aulam coeli ingredi: ad hos applicandi sunt Aaron et filii ejus, id est omnis coetus sanctorum et maxime praepositorum, ut eos 294Bimitentur et eorum vestigia sequantur.

VERS. 16.-- Et vectes. Vectis non solum parietes nectit, sed et columnas introitus confirmat ne corruant, aut a recto statu deflectant, quia Dominus et utrumque populum in una fide dilectionis sociavit, et eorum doctores, ut in fide et actione et praedicatione perseverent, confortat. Nec mirum si et per capita ipsa, et per vectem capita ipsa continentem, Christus accipitur, quia cujusque electi regendo caput est, unde: Caput viri Christus est (II Cor. XI): et quia non solum singulos ad quaerenda coelestia erigit, sed etiam omnes continet, et ne a recto statu deflecti, nec ab invicem discordando possint separari, vinculo pacis confirmat. Cui simile est quod Dominus dicitur et agnus, et pastor, et ostium, 294Ccum pastor agnum pascat, et ambo intrent per ostium; cum proprie sit Verbum in principio (Joan. I, X): horum vero nullum sit proprie, sed figuraliter. Pastor, quia ducit ad pascua vitae; ostium quia non nisi per illum pervenitur ad Patrem; Agnus, quia ab Aegypto salvat. Sic et hic per capita figuratur propter regimen, quo singulis praeest, et per vectem, ob generalem providentiam, quia omnes confirmat (Num. IX; III Reg. VIII).

(BEDA.) Sic anima pro diversis actionibus diversa nomina sortitur. Dum vivificat corpus, anima nominatur; dum vult, animus est; dum scit, mens est: dum recolit, memoria est; dum judicat, ratio est; dum spirat, spiritus est; dum sentit, sensus est.

VERS. 33.-- Nec poterat Moyses, etc. (STRAB.) 294DMoyses ergo in hoc loco, sicut in aliis pluribus, figuram tenet Judaici populi. Moyses ergo, nube obstante, in tabernaculum non poterat intrare, quia Judaicus populus nunquam sacramenta Scripturae valuit penetrare. Nubes enim Domini per diem incubabat tabernaculo, et ignis in nocte: hoc loco ignis divinorum mysteriorum scientiam significat. Scriptura enim sancta sanctis, et ignorantiam suam fatentibus, lumen est: iniquis vero, et scientiam jactantibus, nubes, quia excaecat eos.

(AUG., quaest. 176 in Exod.) Notanda est res mirabilis multum, etc., usque ad nubes per diem, flamma per noctem.

In tabernaculo autem Domini erant tabulae, quarum 295Aannulis erant infixi vectes de lignis sethim, quibus possent levari et portari de mansione ad mansionem, quae omnia mysteriis plena sunt. Per tabernaculum intelligimus Ecclesiam praesentem, in qua sunt tabulae, scilicet sancti humilitate pleni, quibus quinque vectes sunt affixi, id est spiritualis notitia quinque librorum Moysi, per quam se et alios sustollant ad claritatem aeternae beatitudinis. Per vectes ergo, ut diximus, quinque libros Moysi intelligimus, qui sunt Genesis, Exodus, Leviticus, liber Numerorum, et Deuteronomium: in quibus continetur principium mundi et medium et consummatio. In Genesi describitur creatio mundi, et in Exodo recreatio: in Levitico sacramentorum Ecclesiae administratio; in libro Numerorum Evangelica 295Bpraedicatio; in Deuteronomio ultima benedictio. Unde et in benedictionibus terminatur. Est itaque in Genesi ordo creationis; in Exodo mysterium recreationis; in Levitico sanctitas Ecclesiasticae administrationis; in libro Numerorum consolatio evangelicae praedicationis; in Deuteronomio consummatio aeternae benedictionis. Inter hos autem quinque libros Moysi excellit Exodus, quem nunc ad manus habemus. In eo enim et quae filiis Israel in figura contingebant, et mysteria plene continentur Christi et Ecclesiae. Hic tamen primum de exitu actuali carnalium filiorum Israel, de historiali Aegypto agitur, in quo significatur spiritualis exitus spiritualium Israelitarum de vera Aegypto, scilicet de tenebris vitiorum. Agitur etiam de transitu maris Rubri 295Cet submersione Pharaonis et exercitu ejus, et de liberatione populi Dei: in quo figuratur baptismus sanguine Christi consecratus, in quo diaboli vitia submerguntur, et animae fidelium liberantur. Agitur etiam de pugna Israelitarum cum Amalech, in quo victoria Christi et suorum, et confusio diaboli figuratur. Tandem catalogo mansionum descripto agitur de lege data in monte Sina; in quo nobis innuitur ut, a terrenis sublevati discamus subjici coelestibus disciplinis. Agitur iterum de constructione tabernaculi et vasorum ejus, de vestibus sacerdotum et unctione; in quo intelligitur praesens Ecclesia, et diversae personae fidelium diversis officiis deputatae, sicut vasa diversis usibus deputata erant, vel officiis, et sacramentorum et orationum sacratarum suavissima odoramenta. De his hactenus; nunc dicendum est quod secundum ordinem historiae 296Aet secundum allegoriam, congrue in Pentateucho, secundus est liber iste: postquam enim in Genesi egit de creatione coeli et terrae, et de introitu filiorum Israel in Aegyptum, congruit ut consequenter de exitu eorum agat. Ecce quod juxta ordinem historiae congruit: juxta allegoriam quoque non incongrue factum est. Nam postquam dixit de carnali generatione, qua per peccatum primi hominis nascimur filii diaboli, congruit ut statim subdat de regeneratione spirituali, qua per justificationem secundi hominis, id est, Christi renascimur filii Dei. Liber autem iste vocatur ἔξοδος Graece, ואלה שמות veelle semoth Hebraice, exitus vel egressus Latine: ideo quia de exitu filiorum Israel de Aegypto agit. Sed quaeri potest cur liber 296Biste dicatur Exodus, quia de exitu illorum agit, cum in principio non de exitu, sed de ingressu illorum agatur. Ad quod dici potest, quia quamvis specialiter de exitu illorum agere intendat, tamen quia multum tempus profluxit inter exitum, de quo hic agit, et ingressum in quo primus liber terminatur vel terminaverat, ut competentius jungat narrationem suam breviter, prius repetit de introitu, et ita melius descendit ad exitum. Sic et nos in nostris sermonibus facta longa interpositione, dicturi alia, repetimus superiora, ut melius dicta continuemus. His consideratis, videnda est materia, quae est corporalis exitus filiorum Israel de Aegypto, et spiritualis exitus sanctus sanctorum de mundo. Intentione admonet nos spiritualiter exire de conformitate 296Cmundi, sicut illi qui corporaliter exierunt de Aegypto, ut sicut fuimus conformes saeculo, ita simus conformes Deo. Modus talis est: breviter tangens de introitu filiorum Israel in Aegyptum, agit de eorum multiplicatione; deinde de novo rege, qui ignoravit Joseph et coepit affligere populum; deinde de plagis Aegypti, tandem de exitu illorum et de transitu maris Rubri et submersione Aegyptiorum. Tandem pervenit ad montem Sina, in quo lex data est, et agit de tabernaculo; et vasis ejus, et de vestibus sacerdotum et unctione, et sic terminatur.

Nota quod cum eadem sit et indivisa historia, quam proposuit sibi Moyses describendam, tamen per capitula et partes sive per libros eam distinguunt, ut lectoribus fastidium auferatur, sicut viator viam, quae est indivisa, dividit per dietas, ut labor minuatur et absit fastidium.

CHAPTER XL.

VERS. 1.-- And the Lord spoke to Moses, saying: In the first month, etc. (STRAB.) The first month of the year is Nisan itself, which is also April, in which the Jews 293Dcelebrated the pasch, and the tabernacle was set up. Allegorically, however, the first month signifies the beginning of grace, when the tabernacle of God, that is, the Church, was set up, when Christ, namely, being crucified, brought forth out of his side the mysteries of blood and water, by which the Church is dedicated and consecrated. And it is to be noted that this was the first month of the second year: for the first year was before the law, the second year under the grace of the Gospel, which the Lord blessed. Whence: Thou shalt bless the crown of the year of thy goodness (Psal. LXIV). For it was the year of goodness, in which the salvation of the human race appeared through the humanity of Christ: in this year therefore the tabernacle of God was set up, that is, the Church founded and exalted.

294A(RAB. in Exod., tom. II.) The first year is the whole time of the law, etc., as far as that the teachers of the Gospel may use these things in the confirmation of the faith.

VERS. 9.-- Thou shalt anoint the tabernacle, etc. (STRAB.) The tabernacle with its vessels is consecrated with the oil of unction; because whatsoever holy Church does with her virtues she ought to dedicate and consecrate to the grace of the Holy Spirit. For the oil of unction is compared to spiritual grace, by which the works of all the virtues are consecrated to the Lord.

VERS. 12.-- And thou shalt bring Aaron, etc. (STRAB.) The doors of the tabernacle, that is, of holy Church, are the apostles, through whom one must enter the court of heaven: to these are Aaron and his sons to be brought, that is, the whole company of the saints and chiefly of those set over them, that they may 294Bimitate them and follow their footsteps.

VERS. 16.-- And the bars. The bar not only binds the walls together, but also strengthens the pillars of the entrance, that they fall not down, nor bend away from their upright standing; because the Lord has both joined either people together in one faith of love, and strengthens their teachers, that they may persevere in faith and in action and in preaching. Nor is it wonderful if by the heads themselves, and by the bar which holds those heads together, Christ is understood; because he is the head of every elect person by ruling him, whence: The head of the man is Christ (II Cor. XI) [Vulg. 1Cor 11:3 omnis viri caput, Christus est, "the head of every man is Christ"; these are the words Migne prints, reordered, but his own citation names II Corinthians]: and because he not only raises up single men to seek after heavenly things, but also holds all together, and, that they may not be bent away from their upright standing nor be parted from one another by discord, confirms them with the bond of peace. To which is like that the Lord is called both lamb, and shepherd, and door, 294Csince the shepherd feeds the lamb, and both enter by the door; whereas properly he is the Word in the beginning (Joan. I, X): but of these none is proper to him, but figurative. Shepherd, because he leads to the pastures of life; door, because it is not except through him that one comes to the Father; Lamb, because he saves from Egypt. So here also he is figured by the heads, on account of the rule by which he is set over individuals, and by the bar, on account of the general providence, because he confirms all (Num. IX; III Reg. VIII).

(BEDA.) So the soul obtains diverse names for its diverse actions. While it gives life to the body, it is called soul; while it wills, it is mind; while it knows, it is understanding; while it recalls, it is memory; while it judges, it is reason; while it breathes, it is spirit; while it perceives, it is sense.

VERS. 33.-- Nor could Moses, etc. (STRAB.) 294DMoses therefore in this place, as in very many others, holds the figure of the Jewish people. Moses, therefore, the cloud standing in the way, could not enter into the tabernacle, because the Jewish people was never able to penetrate the sacraments of Scripture. For the cloud of the Lord brooded over the tabernacle by day, and fire by night: in this place the fire signifies the knowledge of the divine mysteries. For Holy Scripture is light to the holy, and to those confessing their own ignorance; but to the wicked, and to those boasting of knowledge, it is a cloud, because it blinds them.

(AUG., quaest. 176 in Exod.) A thing much to be wondered at is to be noted, etc., as far as a cloud by day, a flame by night.

Now in the tabernacle of the Lord there were boards, in whose 295Arings were fixed bars of sethim wood, by which they could be lifted and carried from station to station, all which things are full of mysteries. By the tabernacle we understand the present Church, in which there are boards, namely the saints full of humility, to whom five bars are fastened, that is, the spiritual knowledge of the five books of Moses, by which they may raise up themselves and others to the brightness of eternal blessedness. By the bars, therefore, as we have said, we understand the five books of Moses, which are Genesis, Exodus, Leviticus, the book of Numbers, and Deuteronomy: in which are contained the beginning of the world and its middle and its consummation. In Genesis is described the creation of the world, and in Exodus its re-creation; in Leviticus the administration of the sacraments of the Church; in the book of Numbers the evangelical 295Bpreaching; in Deuteronomy the last blessing. Whence also it is ended in blessings. And so there is in Genesis the order of creation; in Exodus the mystery of re-creation; in Leviticus the holiness of the Church's administration; in the book of Numbers the consolation of evangelical preaching; in Deuteronomy the consummation of eternal blessing. Now among these five books of Moses Exodus excels, which we now have in hand. For in it are contained both the things which befell the children of Israel in a figure, and fully the mysteries of Christ and of the Church. Here, however, it treats first of the actual going out of the carnal children of Israel, of the historical Egypt, in which is signified the spiritual going out of the spiritual Israelites from the true Egypt, that is, from the darkness of the vices. It treats also of the passage of the Red Sea 295Cand of the drowning of Pharao and his army, and of the deliverance of the people of God: in which is figured the baptism consecrated by the blood of Christ, in which the vices of the devil are drowned, and the souls of the faithful are delivered. It treats also of the battle of the Israelites with Amalec, in which is figured the victory of Christ and of his own, and the confounding of the devil. Then, when the list of the stations has been described, it treats of the law given on mount Sina; in which it is intimated to us that, being raised up from earthly things, we may learn to be made subject to heavenly disciplines. It treats again of the construction of the tabernacle and of its vessels, of the vestments of the priests and of the unction; in which is understood the present Church, and the diverse persons of the faithful appointed to diverse offices, just as the vessels were appointed to diverse uses, or offices, and the most sweet perfumes of the sacraments and of the consecrated prayers. Thus far of these things; now it is to be said that according to the order of history 296Aand according to allegory, this book is fittingly the second in the Pentateuch: for after he had treated in Genesis of the creation of heaven and earth, and of the entering in of the children of Israel into Egypt, it was fitting that he should consequently treat of their going out. Behold what is fitting according to the order of history: according to allegory also it was not unfittingly done. For after he had spoken of the carnal generation, by which through the sin of the first man we are born sons of the devil, it was fitting that he should straightway add concerning the spiritual regeneration, by which through the justification of the second man, that is, of Christ, we are born again sons of God. Now this book is called ἔξοδος in Greek, ואלה שמות veelle semoth in Hebrew, going out or departure in Latin: for this reason, because it treats of the going out of the children of Israel from Egypt. But it may be asked why this book 296Bis called Exodus, because it treats of their going out, whereas in the beginning it treats not of the going out, but of their entering in. To which it may be said that although it purposes to treat especially of their going out, yet because much time flowed by between the going out, of which it here treats, and the entering in with which the first book is ended or had been ended, in order that it may more suitably join its narrative together briefly, it first repeats concerning the entering in, and so descends the better to the going out. So we also in our discourses, when a long interposition has been made, being about to say other things, repeat what went before, that we may the better continue what has been said. These things being considered, the matter is to be looked at, which is the bodily going out of the children of Israel from Egypt, and the spiritual going out, holy, of the saints from the world. By its intention it admonishes us to go out spiritually from the conformity 296Cof the world, as they did who went out bodily from Egypt, that as we have been conformed to the world, so we may be conformed to God. The manner is such: touching briefly upon the entering in of the children of Israel into Egypt, it treats of their multiplication; then of the new king, who knew not Joseph and began to afflict the people; then of the plagues of Egypt, and at length of their going out and of the passage of the Red Sea and of the drowning of the Egyptians. At length it comes to mount Sina, in which the law was given, and it treats of the tabernacle, and of its vessels, and of the vestments of the priests and of the unction, and so it is ended.

Note that although the history is one and undivided which Moses set before himself to describe, yet they distinguish it by chapters and parts, or by books, that weariness may be taken away from readers, just as a traveller divides the road, which is undivided, into days' journeys, that the labour may be lessened and weariness be absent.
